# rapaio-lib error review -- 2026-09-28

Full read of every source file under `rapaio-lib/src` (619 files, 112,497 lines) at commit `4adf2809`,
looking for **defects only**: wrong results, crashes or non-termination on valid input, missing or wrong
validation that lets invalid input produce silent garbage, contract violations (`equals`/`compareTo`/
`Iterator`/`Serializable`/Javadoc promises), aliasing and thread-safety bugs, copy-paste asymmetries
between type variants. Style, naming, performance, design and missing tests were out of scope and are not
listed. This document was written as analysis only; since then it is used as the work list for the fixes
recorded under Status. Conventions: a ~~struck~~ finding has been fixed (the note after it names the
date and, where useful, the fix); a finding marked "moot" or "not a bug" was re-checked and withdrawn;
everything else is still open.

## Method

The tree was split into 12 parts of 5-12k lines along package boundaries. Each part was read top to bottom
by one reviewer with the same brief: confirm every candidate against the code before recording it, give a
concrete triggering input, and tag each finding with a severity and a confidence:

- severity `bug` = wrong result or failure on valid input; `likely-bug` = wrong with one plausible
  assumption or on an edge that real callers reach; `contract` = the code does not honour what its
  Javadoc, interface or Java contract promises (serializability, comparator laws, documented ranges).
- confidence `HIGH` = traced and certain; `MEDIUM` = one unverified assumption; `LOW` = suspicious,
  needs a run to confirm.

Each part's report is reproduced verbatim in Part 2 below, including its per-file coverage list. Every
part reports all of its files fully read; no file is marked NOT COVERED. The reviewers were told not to
rely on any earlier review of this code base.

Independent spot check by the coordinator, 13 HIGH findings from 8 parts re-read in the source:
12 confirmed as written (`ADTestGoodness` correction sign, `Fisher.mode()`, `KSTestTwoSamples` tie
handling, `Ints.equals` bound, `Pair.update(Pair)` no-op, `SigmoidKernel` `atan`, `ExponentialKernel`
squared norm, `MinkowskiDistance` missing `abs`, `KnnRegression` swapped `clamp_` arguments,
`FinBarSize._15min` = 115 minutes, `RowSampler.Sample.getComplementMapping` iterating the sample,
`AbstractNetwork` using `this.getClass()` inside the superclass walk); one confirmed with a nuance
(`Normal.of(mu, 0)` is accepted on purpose because the z-tests build degenerate sampling
distributions from it, the NaN half of that finding stands); none refuted.

**2026-09-28 -- `darray.operator` batch.** All 14 operator-layer findings of part 03 that concern the
`impl` and `unary` packages, reproduced first with a probe against `4adf2809` (SIMD lengths 8 double / 16 float
on the test host), then fixed: `ReduceOpProd`/`ReduceOpNanProd` (8 paths each) and
`UnaryOpSoftmax`/`UnaryOpLogSoftmax` (4 paths each) now create their SIMD accumulator inside the offsets
loop; `applyGenericDouble` in `UnaryOpLogSoftmax` takes the log; `UnaryOpCompareMask` double paths use
`doubleValue()`; `ReduceOpNanMean` float-unit tail tests `!isNaN`; `ReduceOpMin`/`ReduceOpNanMin`
`reduceFloatDefault` start from `+Inf`; `ReduceOpNanProd` double-unit tail reads doubles; `ReduceOpVarc`
divides the correction term by `n` (the `var(ddof, mean)` Javadoc now says the mean is a shift and the result
is the variance about the sample mean); the four `reduceIntDefault` read `getInt`; `UnaryOpAbs/Neg/Sqr`
`applyGenericInt` advance by `loop.step`. The two MEDIUM items on NaN masking were resolved in the IEEE
direction: `ReduceOpNanSum`, `ReduceOpNanProd` and `ReduceOpNanMean` no longer mask the accumulator's own
NaN (`+Inf + -Inf`, `0 * Inf`), so the result no longer depends on which lane the operands fall in and
matches the scalar and `Default` paths (numpy's `nansum([inf, -inf])` is `nan`). Real NaN inputs are still
skipped. The `Broadcast` finding is moot: `Shape` rejects any zero dimension in its constructor, so a
`(0,)` shape cannot exist. The `darray.matrix` validation items (integer dtypes, QR `m < n`, LU `m > n`,
`EigenDecomposition` aliasing and `power()`) are not in this batch. New test
`rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/darray/operator/OperatorPathsRegressionTest.java` (12 tests, each on a path not
reached by contiguous SIMD arrays: multi-offset views, strided views, scalar tails, `Var`-backed non-SIMD
storages, and a test-local non-SIMD `IntStorage` for the previously latent int paths); all 12 fail on the
previous sources, each on its own defect.

**2026-09-28 -- kernel batch.** All 15 `ml.common.kernel` and `ml.common.distance` findings of part 09.
`AbstractKernel` gained `deltaNorm` (`||u - v||`) next to `deltaSumSquares` (`||u - v||^2`), and each kernel
now computes the formula in its own Javadoc: Exponential, GeneralizedStudentT, Log and Power use the norm;
Cauchy divides the squared norm by `sigma^2`; RationalQuadratic, MultiQuadric and InverseMultiQuadric use
the squared norm once; Sigmoid is `tanh`. Circular and Spherical test `||x - y|| / sigma >= 1` for the zero
region and evaluate the formula on the norm inside it, so `k(x, x) = 1` and the value reaches 0 continuously
at the support boundary. One deliberate deviation from the old Circular Javadoc: it read `arccos(-f)`, which
gives 2 at the boundary and a discontinuous, non-positive-definite function; the geostatistical circular
covariance is `(2/pi)(arccos(f) - f sqrt(1 - f^2))`, which is what the code and the corrected Javadoc now
say. Wave computes `theta / ||x - y|| * sin(||x - y|| / theta)` with `k(x, x) = 1`, and its Javadoc (a copy
of Wavelet's) was replaced. ChiSquare skips coordinates with equal values instead of producing `0/0`.
`Kernel.isLinear()` is now documented as "inner product plus a constant", which is exactly what the
weight-vector shortcut in `BinarySMO` can represent (the constant cancels under `sum(alpha_i y_i) = 0`):
`LinearKernel` returns true, `PolyKernel` requires `exponent == 1` and `slope == 1`. `MinkowskiDistance`
takes `|delta|^p` and names itself `Minkowski(p=...)`. Two `BinarySMOTest` pins moved with the corrected
kernels: the XOR fixture with `WaveKernel(2)` (the fixed kernel is nearly constant on that pattern, so the
four training points are recovered with a small margin and the centre stays on the boundary) and the
`LogKernel(1)` decision function on iris (9 support vectors instead of 6). New test
`rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/ml/common/kernel/KernelFormulaTest.java` (13 tests, values written out by hand on
vectors with `||x - y|| = 3`); all 13 fail on the previous sources.

**2026-09-28 -- `darray.matrix` validation batch.** The five remaining part-03 findings. `LUDecomposition`,
`QRDecomposition`, `SVDecomposition` and `EigenDecomposition` now reject a non-matrix rank and an integer
dtype at construction, with the message `CholeskyDecomposition` already used, instead of running the
algorithm through `setDouble` truncation (an integer `[[2,1],[4,3]]` returned `det() == 6` where the value
is 2). `QRDecomposition` enforces the `m >= n` its own Javadoc requires, so a wide matrix fails at
construction rather than from `q()` with an out-of-bounds error; `solve` already refused such a matrix as
rank deficient, so no caller loses a working path. `LUDecomposition.solve` (and `inv`, which goes through
it) requires a square matrix: for `m > n` the loops only ran to `dim(1)`, so rows `n..m-1` of the result
were the permuted right-hand side with no least-squares meaning; `l()`, `u()` and `pivots()` still work for
a tall matrix, which is the part of the rectangular decomposition that is defined. `EigenDecomposition.real()`
and `imag()` clone the internal arrays, which for `DType.DOUBLE` were wrapped without a copy, so mutating
the returned array (or `clampRealValues`) changed a previously returned one and `d()`; `power()` keeps a
`symmetric` flag and is refused for a non-symmetric matrix, since `V * diag(lambda)^p * V'` needs orthogonal
eigenvectors and leaves the complex pairs of `d()` un-powered (for the rotation `[[0,1],[-1,0]]` it returned
something other than `-I`). One existing `LUDecompositionTest` case was updated: it used a 4x3 matrix to
trigger the row-mismatch message, which the new square check now precedes, so it asserts the square message
and a separate `eye`-based case covers the row mismatch (deterministic, because the printable pins later in
that suite depend on the shared random stream). New test
`rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/darray/matrix/DecompositionValidationTest.java` (6 tests); all 6 fail on the previous
sources.

**2026-09-28 -- `darray` layout and view batch (parts 01 and 02).** All 31 part-01 and 13 part-02 findings.

*Layout classes.* The behaviour no longer depends on which factory built a layout: `moveAxis` shifts in both
directions (`src > dst` used to overwrite the destination and duplicate an axis); `narrowAll` squeezes when
`keepdim` is false, and `ArrayStrideLayout.narrow` on a rank-1 layout does too; `MatrixStrideLayout.permute`
compares against `rank()` instead of `rank() - 1`, which had rejected every input including the identity;
`index(int)` subtracts the offset, divides by the larger stride first and treats a zero stride as a fixed
index 0; `VectorStrideLayout.narrow(keepDim=false)` drops the axis only when the result is unit length, which
is what `DArray.narrow` documents and what `copyTo`'s blocked path needs for a large vector view;
`VectorStrideLayout.attemptReshape` handles a rank-0 target and `ScalarStrideLayout.attemptReshape` sizes the
new strides by the target rank, so scalar and unit-vector reshapes round-trip; `ScalarStrideLayout.squeeze(int...)`
with no axes is a no-op; `VectorStrideLayout.isDense()` is true for a unit stride; `stretch` bounds its axes by
`rank() + axes.length`; `ArrayStrideLayout.squeeze` rejects a negative axis and no longer carries the matrix
error message; `strides()` returns a copy in all implementations. `equals`/`hashCode` moved into
`AbstractStrideLayout` as one structural implementation, so a dense layout and its `StrideLayout.of` twin are
equal; the previous `getClass()` comparison made them unequal and made `squeeze(5)` unequal to its own input.

*Core.* `cat` rejects arrays of different ranks (it used to compare only the first array's dimensions and stop
the copy when the destination slice ran out, dropping 18 of 24 elements in the review's example) and validates
the axis. `asDoubleArray`/`dv` share the backing array only when the darray covers it exactly, so the first row
of a matrix no longer returns all of its elements. `gather_` compares shapes with `equals` rather than by
identity, which had rejected every destination not built by `gather()` itself; `scatter_` requires index and
input to have the same shape, since it pairs their elements by flat position; both validate the axis.
`IndexIterator` handles a rank-0 shape (one empty index) and documents that `next()` returns its own buffer.
`StrideWrapper.quickSortIndirect` returns for a range shorter than two elements, which is what
`externalSort(new int[0], asc)` produces. `ravel`, `flatten` and `toDoubleArray` resolve `Order.A` against the
layout the way `reshape` does, instead of reaching the `default -> throw` of `computeFortranLayout`. `Shape`
publishes its lazily computed strides only once filled. `DArray.stream()`'s Javadoc now describes the default
order it actually uses, and points at `stream(Order.S)` for the storage order.

*Strided implementation.* `varOn`'s mean check was inverted; it now requires the mean to hold as many values as
the result, which accepts both the reduced shape its message demands and the keepDim form the `nn` layers pass
(requiring exact shape equality would have broken `StdOnNode` and `StandardizeOnNode`). `reduceOn`, `varOn` and
`reduceTo` return a shape of unit dimensions rather than a scalar when every axis is reduced and `keepDim` is
true. `diag` bounds the diagonal length by both dimensions, so a non-square matrix no longer reads out of the
storage. `mm(other, to)` validates the target shape and `copyTo` the destination shape, instead of writing
through the target's strides. `argmax`/`argmin` start from the first element instead of a sentinel, which is a
legal value for the integral dtypes and for the infinities. The column path of `mv` no longer skips a zero
coefficient, so `0 * NaN` and `0 * Inf` give NaN as in the row path. `conv1d`/`unfold1d` use the same output
length formula as the 2d/3d routines, keeping the window that the old formula dropped whenever the padded
length did not divide the stride; the test helper in `DArrayConvolutionsTest` re-implemented the same wrong
formula and was corrected with it. `conv1d`, `convTranspose1d`, `conv3d` and `convTranspose3d` flatten their
kernel with an explicit `Order.C`, matching the C-order `unfold` side, so an F-ordered kernel gives the same
result as its C-order copy. `ceilMode` pooling drops a trailing window that would start past the input and its
left padding. `conv2d`/`conv3d` validate `outChannels % groups` and `convTranspose2d`/`3d` validate
`inChannels % groups`, which the 1d variants already did.

The part-02 files are generated: `BaseDoubleStrideDArray` and `BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions` are the
sources and `rapaio-code-gen`'s `CodeGenApp` derives the Float, Int and Byte variants from them. Every edit was
made in the Double files and the three variants were regenerated with the generator's own replacement lists and
`FREEZE`/`UNFREEZE` handling; the replica was first validated by reproducing all six generated files byte for
byte at `4adf2809`, and re-validated after the edits, so a future `CodeGenApp` run is a no-op.

New tests `rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/darray/layout/StrideLayoutContractTest.java` (17 tests, each exercised through
both layout factories where that distinction matters) and
`rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/darray/manager/base/StrideDArrayContractTest.java` (11 tests); all 28 fail on the
previous sources, each on its own defect.

## Part 1 -- Cross-package summary

### 1.1 The most serious findings

Wrong numbers returned silently on ordinary input, ordered roughly by how many users hit them:

1. ~~**`darray` operators over-count on non-contiguous views** (03). `ReduceOpProd`, `ReduceOpNanProd`,
   `UnaryOpSoftmax` and `UnaryOpLogSoftmax` hoist a SIMD accumulator out of the offset loop and add the
   partial result once per offset, so any 2-D+ view that is not contiguous (a transposed or sliced
   matrix) gets a wrong product / softmax. `UnaryOpLogSoftmax.applyGenericDouble` omits `Math.log`
   entirely. `ReduceOpMin`/`NanMin` float paths start from `0`; `ReduceOpNanMean` float tail has its NaN
   test inverted; `UnaryOpCompareMask` compares doubles against a float-rounded reference.~~ FIXED
   2026-09-28, see Status.
2. ~~**`darray` layout and view defects** (01, 02)~~ FIXED 2026-09-28, see Status (the decomposition validation items of 03 were fixed in the same session). `asDoubleArray()`/`dv()` return the whole backing
   array for a vector view at offset 0 (the first row of any matrix); `DArrayManager.cat` silently
   drops data when ranks differ; `moveAxis(src > dst)` produces a corrupt layout; `MatrixStrideLayout.
   permute` always throws; `VectorStrideLayout.narrow(keepDim=false)` throws on valid input, which
   breaks `copyTo`/`copy(F)`/`pad` on large vector views; `varOn` has its mean-shape check inverted;
   `diag(int)` on a non-square matrix reads out of bounds; `mm(other, to)` and `copyTo` never check the
   target shape; `conv1d`/`unfold1d` lose one output position whenever the padded length is not
   divisible by the stride; F-ordered kernels are reshaped in F order while the `unfold` side is C.
3. ~~**Kernels** (09). 12 of 22 kernels do not compute their documented function: `deltaSumSquares` (a
   squared norm) is used where the norm is required in Exponential, Cauchy, StudentT, RationalQuadratic,
   InverseMultiQuadric, MultiQuadric, Log, Power and Wave; Circular/Spherical have an inverted support
   test (0 on the diagonal); Sigmoid uses `atan` for `tanh`. Every kernel machine built on them (SVM
   with a non-RBF kernel, kernel k-NN, RVM) computes something other than the model it advertises.~~
   FIXED 2026-09-28, see Status.
4. **Learners that ignore what they accept** (07, 08). Sample weights are accepted by `fit` and ignored
   or half-applied by `ctree Search.Binary`, `GBTRegressionModel`, `BinaryLogistic`,
   `RidgeRegressionModel`, `LinearRegressionModel`, `ManualClassifier`, the `boostUpdate` leaf refits,
   and by every model in part 07 except `BinarySMO` and the naive-Bayes prior. `BinaryLogisticNewton`
   drops the L2 penalty from the gradient, so regularization has no effect on the solution.
5. **Clustering and k-NN** (07). `KMedoids` never performs an improving swap (candidate cost is
   evaluated against the old medoids); `KMCluster` never repairs empty clusters; `MWKMeans` computes
   Minkowski centres on unsorted data; `KnnRegression` swaps the `clamp_` bounds so every kernel
   degenerates to a uniform mean.
6. **Forests and trees** (05, 08). `RowSampler.Sample.getComplementMapping` iterates the sampled frame
   instead of the original, so out-of-bag rows are wrong for every non-default sampler; `CForest` OOB
   error counts never-out-of-bag rows as misclassified; `RTree` assigns a target mean as a node weight;
   `CTree.newDecisionStump()` grows two levels; `Pruning.ReducedError` with its default collapses the
   tree to the root; `ctree.Search.NumericRandom` loops forever on an all-missing column.
7. **Autograd edges** (10). `AbstractLoss.batchForward` doubles all gradients for two or more batches
   (each loss pre-sets `grad = 1` and `addGrad` accumulates); `AbstractNetwork.parameters()/state()` use
   `this.getClass()` inside the superclass walk, so layers declared in a superclass are never optimized
   or saved; `GatherNode` loses gradient on repeated indices; `Conv2dNode` uses one `outputPadding` for
   both axes; `MSELoss` mutates its target. The elementwise, binary, softmax and standardize gradients
   were all derived by hand and match.
8. **`data` type conversions** (04). `VarInt`/`VarLong.setDouble/addDouble` and `VarDouble`/`VarFloat.
   getInt/getLong` saturate to `MAX_VALUE`, which is the missing sentinel, so large present values become
   missing; `VarInt.addInstant/setInstant` truncate epoch millis to 32 bits; the custom `readObject`
   methods drop `parser`/`formatter` so `getLabel` NPEs after deserialization; `MappedVar.removeRow/
   clearRows` and `MappedFrame.clearRows` mutate the caller's shared `Mapping`.
9. **Statistics** (06, 09). `ADTestGoodness` has the sign of the Marsaglia small-sample correction
   wrong; `KSTestTwoSamples.D` is wrong with ties; `DensityVector.normalize` leaves `sum()` stale;
   `Fisher.mode()` uses the wrong formula and `Fisher` has no parameter validation; `Normal`/`LogNormal.
   cdf(+Inf) == 0` because `MathTools.normalCdf` returns 0 for both infinities, so `erf(+Inf) == -1`;
   `RootFind1D` secant never re-evaluates `f` and reports `converged=true` at a wrong point;
   `IRLSSolver` discards the weight squaring; LDA eigen-decomposes `Sb·Sw⁻¹` instead of `Sw⁻¹·Sb` and
   standardizes test data with test statistics; `Confusion` swaps true/false for `VarBinary`.
10. **I/O** (05). `Csv` silently truncates the frame when a column appears late, loops forever on an
    all-rejecting `keepRows`, has two `StringIndexOutOfBounds` paths in quote handling, and its writer
    emits `∞`, loses `LONG` precision and writes `INSTANT` as millis so round-trips fail;
    `JavaIO.storeToFile` never flushes.

### 1.2 Recurring patterns

- **Copy-paste asymmetry between type variants.** The `darray` operator layer, `GroupFunMax/Min`,
  `VarType` switches and the hash-collection helpers all show the same shape: one dtype path correct, a
  sibling path with a wrong accumulator, sentinel, comparison or bound. Part 02 is the exception: its
  generated variants are mutually consistent, and every defect there is shared by all four.
- **`VarBinary` disagrees with itself.** `levels()` returns `true/false` while `getLabel()` returns
  `0/1`; `OneRule`, `ZeroRule`, `ManualClassifier` and `Confusion` each break on binary targets in a
  different way (08, 09).
- **Missing-value level 0.** Densities and argmaxes that start at index 0 include the `?` level; several
  learners predict `?` or shift class columns because of it (07, 08).
- **`Serializable` claimed, not deliverable.** `DensityTable`, `Grid2D`, ~10 `Transform`/`Spots`
  classes, every `Figure`/`POpts`, and several nn classes declare `Serializable` (often with a
  `serialVersionUID`) while holding lambdas, `Stream`s or `DArray`s. The IDEA printer, which serializes
  figures, cannot work (05, 06, 10, 11).
- **Boundary inputs.** `cdf(±Inf)`, `quantile(NaN)`, `p` outside `[0,1]`, empty or single-element input,
  all-missing columns, constant data and 1-CPU hosts each produce NaN, an exception or an infinite loop
  somewhere; the distributions moved to `QuantileSearch.checkProbability` are the ones that behave (06,
  04, 08).
- **Options and parameters silently ignored.** `Plot(opts)` discards plot-level options; null `fill`
  defaults plus `Graphics2D.setColor(null)` being a no-op make `color(...)` a no-op for several artists;
  `predict(df, withClasses, withDistributions)` NPEs in `AdaBoost` and `GBTClassifierModel` (08, 11).
- **Hand-written helpers around ported code.** Where the code is a faithful port (libsvm solver, JAMA
  decompositions, fastutil hash maps, Murmur3, Loader/Stirling special functions) the reviewers found
  no arithmetic error; the defects sit in the glue written around them (kernel `Cache` grow path,
  `SvmClassifier` label alignment, decomposition input validation, `Ints.equals`, `Doubles.
  binarySearch`).

### 1.3 Reading the per-part reports

Part 2 reproduces each reviewer's report unchanged: a coverage table listing every file read with its
line count, findings grouped by file with `path:lines`, severity, confidence, the triggering input and
actual-vs-expected behaviour, and the reviewer's own summary. Line numbers refer to commit `4adf2809`.

### Findings

#### rapaio/darray/DArray.java

For a F-ordered or transposed array the stream order differs from `iterator()` (which really uses `Order.S`). Should either use `Order.S` or fix the Javadoc.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: the Javadoc now describes the default order and points at `stream(Order.S)`.

Input: `cat(DOUBLE, 0, [seq(2,3), seq(2,3,4)])`. Validation passes (2==2, 3==3), result shape is `[4,3]`, and the copy loop stops when the 6-element destination slice is exhausted, silently dropping 18 of the 24 elements of the second array. Expected: `IllegalArgumentException`. (If the second array has *fewer* dims the same loop throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` instead of a clear message.)~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/Shape.java

A concurrent reader (Shape instances are shared immutable values, and `Shape.index/position` can be called from parallel tasks) can observe a non-null, still all-ones strides array and compute wrong indexes. Input: `DArrays.seq(Shape.of(2,5)).selsq(0, 0).asDoubleArray()` (first row, 5 elements) -> returns all 10 elements `[0..9]`; `DArrays.seq(Shape.of(10)).narrow(0,0,3).dv()` -> `VarDouble` of size 10. Only `gather()` (which builds the destination from `index.shape()` itself) passes. Input: `dst = DArrays.zeros(Shape.of(2,2)); dst.gather_(0, index /*shape (2,2)*/, src)` -> `IllegalArgumentException("Index must have the same shape as destination.")` for equal shapes. Expected: `equals` comparison.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/AbstractStrideDArray.java:256-277` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `scatter_` pairs `input` and `index` elements by flat C-order position (`ptrSrcIt`/`ptrIdxIt` advanced in lockstep) and only checks ranks. With equal ranks but different shapes (allowed by the Javadoc "The input must have the same rank as index") the pairing is wrong: `index` `(2,2)`, `input` `(2,3)`: `index[1,0]` receives `input[0,2]` instead of `input[1,0]`; with a smaller `input` it throws `NoSuchElementException`. Either validate `input.shape().equals(index.shape())` or index `input` by logical position. Also `gather_`/`scatter_` do not validate `axis` or that the gathered index values are in `[0, dim(axis))`; out-of-range indices silently address other elements of the shared storage.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: `scatter_` requires equal index and input shapes, and both methods validate the axis. The range of the gathered index values is still unchecked.

The `DArray.ravel/toDoubleArray` Javadoc does not restrict the order; `Order.A` is accepted by `reshape` and `cast`. Expected: resolve `A` like `reshape` does, or document.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/storage/**
- ByteStorage, DoubleStorage, FloatStorage, IntStorage, ArrayStorageManager, Byte/Double/Float/IntArrayStorage, VarDoubleStorage, VarFloatStorage: clean. Narrowing conversions (e.g. `IntStorage.incDouble` truncating the increment, byte wrap in `incByte`) match the documented "no type promotion" policy in package-info. `ArrayStorageManager.from(dt, Storage)` `return null;` after an exhaustive enum switch is unreachable.

### Other files checked and clean
- Compare.java, Order.java, DType.java, Layout.java, Simd.java, Storage.java (Serialization only covers the four array storages, by design), StorageManager.java, DArrays.java, package-info.java.

### Summary

The primitives (storages, pointer iterators, loop descriptors, the numpy-derived `attemptReshape`, stride flag detection) are correct and carefully written. All findings below were fixed 2026-09-28, see Status. The defects cluster in the three concrete `StrideLayout` classes, whose behaviour diverges depending on whether a layout came from `ofDense` (always `ArrayStrideLayout`) or `StrideLayout.of` (rank-specialised classes): `MatrixStrideLayout.permute` always throws, `moveAxis(src > dst)` silently produces a corrupt layout in both Array and Matrix layouts, `VectorStrideLayout.narrow(keepDim=false)` throws on valid input (breaking `copyTo`/`copy(F)`/`pad` for large vector views), and scalar/vector reshapes to/from rank 0 throw.

Most serious for users: `asDoubleArray()`/`dv()` return the entire backing array for a vector view that starts at offset 0 (e.g. the first row of a matrix), a silent wrong-length result; `DArrayManager.cat` silently drops data when ranks differ; `gather_` rejects equal shapes because it compares `Shape` by identity.

Counts: bug 17, likely-bug 3, contract 11 (31 findings; HIGH 26, MEDIUM 3, LOW 2). Packages with no findings: `storage/**`, `iterators/` except `IndexIterator`, root files except `DArray.stream()` Javadoc, `DArrayManager.cat`/`stride`, and `Shape` lazy strides.


---

## Part 02 -- darray.manager.base

### Coverage

All 9 files read fully. The four `Base*StrideDArray` files and the four `Base*StrideDArrayConvolutions` files are template-generated: after normalising the dtype tokens (`Double/Float/Integer/Byte`, `double/float/int/byte`, `*Vector`, `get*/set*/inc*`) a textual diff shows them identical line for line (the only difference is a trailing `// UNFREEZE` marker pair in the Double file and a final newline). Every finding below was therefore verified in the Double file and confirmed present at the same line numbers in the Float, Int and Byte variants; dtype-specific consequences are called out where they differ.

| File | Lines |
|---|---|
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseByteStrideDArray.java` | 1642 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseByteStrideDArrayConvolutions.java` | 889 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDArrayManager.java` | 75 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java` | 1643 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java` | 889 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseFloatStrideDArray.java` | 1642 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseFloatStrideDArrayConvolutions.java` | 889 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseIntStrideDArray.java` | 1642 |
| `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseIntStrideDArrayConvolutions.java` | 889 |

Total: 10 200 lines. No file NOT COVERED.

Contracts confirmed against `rapaio/darray/DArray.java` (Javadoc of `reshape`, `t_`, `mm(other,to)`, `reduceOn`, `diag`, `argmax`), `rapaio/darray/manager/AbstractStrideDArray.java` (`reshape` order resolution, `t_` view semantics), `rapaio/darray/Layout.java` (`storageFastTandemOrder`), `rapaio/darray/layout/*StrideLayout.java` (`pointer()` performs no bounds check), `rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpMax.java` / `ReduceOpMin.java` (sentinel values), and `rapaio-lib/tst/rapaio/darray/DArrayConvolutionsTest.java`.

### Findings

#### `BaseByteStrideDArray.java`, `BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java`, `BaseFloatStrideDArray.java`, `BaseIntStrideDArray.java`

The four files are identical after dtype normalisation, with **identical line numbers** (offset 0) in all four. Line numbers below are from `BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java` and apply unchanged to the Byte, Float and Int variants.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java:576-578` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `varOn` has an inverted validation: `if (mean.shape().equals(firstLayout.shape())) throw new IllegalArgumentException("Mean darray must have the same shape as the result array.")`. A `mean` with exactly the result shape (which is what the message demands and what the sibling `var1d` at lines 498-501 requires) is rejected, while a `mean` of any other shape is accepted and iterated blindly with `meanIt.next()`. Trigger: `x` of shape `(3,4)`, `x.varOn(Shape.of(4), 0, x.mean1d(1), false, Order.C)` (mean shape `(3)`) → IllegalArgumentException; `x.varOn(Shape.of(4), 0, dm.zeros(DType.DOUBLE, Shape.of(3,1)), ...)` → runs. Current nn callers (`StdOnNode`, `StandardizeOnNode`) pass a keepDim mean and so happen to dodge the check; a mean smaller than 3 elements would additionally exhaust `meanIt`. Expected: the condition must be negated (`!mean.shape().equals(...)`).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java:388-390` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `reduceOn` returns `dm.scalar(dt, reduce(op))` when `rank() == shape.rank()` regardless of `keepDim`, although the Javadoc states `keepDim=true` keeps the original number of dimensions with size 1. Trigger: `x` of shape `(2,3)`, `x.sumOn(Shape.of(2,3), true)` → rank-0 scalar; expected shape `(1,1)`. Same defect in `varOn` at lines 566-568 (`return dm.scalar(dt, var(ddof, mean.getDouble()))`) and in `reduceTo` at lines 418-420 (`targetShape.rank()==0` short-circuits to a scalar before the keepDim handling at 481-486).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java:1478-1486` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `diag(int)` on a non-square matrix computes the diagonal length from a single dimension: `d = diagonal >= 0 ? dim(1) : dim(0); len = diagonal >= 0 ? d - diagonal : d + diagonal`, never clamping to the other dimension. Trigger: matrix of shape `(2,5)`, `diag(0)` → `len=5`, reads `getDouble(2,2)`, `(3,3)`, `(4,4)`; `StrideLayout.pointer()` has no bounds check, so for a dense `(2,5)` array pointer 12 exceeds the 10-element storage (`ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`), and for a view into a larger storage it silently returns unrelated elements. Symmetric case: shape `(5,2)`, `diag(-1)` → `len=4`, reads column index 3 of a 2-column matrix. Expected length: `min(dim(0), dim(1)-diagonal)` for `diagonal>=0` and `min(dim(0)+diagonal, dim(1))` otherwise.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java:1102-1111` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `mm(DArray<?> other, DArray<?> to)` validates only `to.dt()`; the Javadoc says `to` "must have the correct data type and shape", but neither rank nor dimensions are checked before `mmBlocked` writes with `to`'s strides (`cOff + (ti+i)*cs0 + tj*cs1`, lines 1188-1195). Trigger: `a (3x4)`, `b (4x5)`, `to = zeros(2,5)` → row 2 is written past the end of `to` (out-of-bounds on dense storage, silent corruption of neighbouring data when `to` is a view); `to = zeros(4,5)` → result silently lands in the first 3 rows with no error. Expected: IllegalArgumentException on shape mismatch.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

Trigger: `zeros(2,3).copyTo(zeros(3,3))` → returns a half-filled array without error. Expected: shape validation with an exception.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

When every element equals the sentinel the method returns `-1`, an invalid position. For Int the sentinel is `Integer.MIN_VALUE`, for Byte `Byte.MIN_VALUE`, both legal data values: `dm.stride(DType.BYTE, Shape.of(2), new byte[]{-128,-128}).argmax()` → `-1` (expected `0`); `new int[]{Integer.MAX_VALUE, Integer.MAX_VALUE}` → `argmin()` = `-1` (expected `0`). For Double/Float the same happens for all-`-Infinity`/all-`+Infinity` arrays and for arrays that are entirely NaN. `argmax1d`/`argmin1d` (lines 619-694) then store the `-1` into the result array.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

IEEE semantics require `0 * NaN = NaN` and `0 * Inf = NaN`, so `mv` returns different results for the same matrix depending on storage order. Trigger: `A = [[NaN],[1]]` stored F-order (`as0==1, as1!=1`), `x = [0]` → `[0, 0]`; same `A` stored C-order → `[NaN, 0]`. Expected: identical result regardless of layout. (Int/Byte variants have no NaN and are unaffected.)~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

Clean. (`random` ends with an unreachable `return null` after an exhaustive switch; not a defect.)

#### `BaseByteStrideDArrayConvolutions.java`, `BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java`, `BaseFloatStrideDArrayConvolutions.java`, `BaseIntStrideDArrayConvolutions.java`

The four files are identical after dtype normalisation, with **identical line numbers** (offset 0) in all four. Line numbers below are from `BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java` and apply unchanged to the Byte, Float and Int variants.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java:84` and `:207` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `conv1d` and `unfold1d` compute the output length as `floorDiv(L + 2p - (k-1)d, s)`; the correct formula (used by `conv2d`/`conv3d`/`unfold2d`/`unfold3d` at 259-260, 431-433, 473-475 and by every pooling routine) is `floorDiv(L + 2p - (k-1)d - 1, s) + 1`, i.e. `ceil((L+2p-(k-1)d)/s)`. The two agree only when `L+2p-(k-1)d` is divisible by `s`; otherwise one valid window is dropped. Trigger: input length 5, `k=3, stride=2, padding=0, dilation=1` → windows at 0 and 2 both fit, expected 2 outputs, code produces 1. The existing test `testPaddingStrideDilation` uses `8+6-6=8`, divisible by 2, so it does not catch this; the test itself (`DArrayConvolutionsTest.java:180`) re-implements the same wrong formula.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java:102`, `:171`, `:444`, `:541-542` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `conv1d`, `convTranspose1d`, `conv3d` and `convTranspose3d` flatten the kernel (and in `convTranspose3d` also the input slice) with `reshape(Shape)` = `reshape(shape, Order.A)`. Per `AbstractStrideDArray.reshape` (lines 86-111) `Order.A` resolves to `Order.F` whenever the layout `isFOrdered()`. The other operand of the `mm` (the `unfold*` result, or the C-order flat index `oc*kD*kH*kW + kd*kH*kW + kh*kW + kw` used at 549-559 / `id*inH*inW + ih*inW + iw` at 559) is always C-order, so an F-ordered kernel (or, in `convTranspose3d`, an F-ordered input) is multiplied against a permuted flattening and the result is wrong. Trigger: `kernel = dm.random(DType.DOUBLE, Shape.of(2,2,3), rnd, Order.F)` passed to `conv1d` gives a different answer than `kernel.copy(Order.C)`. Expected: identical results. `conv2d` (line 280) correctly passes `Order.C`; the other routines must do the same.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java:596`, `:683-684`, `:788-790` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `maxPool1d/2d/3d` in `ceilMode` use `ceil((L + 2p - d(k-1) - 1)/s + 1)` but omit the standard correction "if `(out-1)*s >= L + p` then `out--`" that guarantees the last window starts inside the input or left padding. Without it a window can lie entirely in right padding, producing an output element of `-Infinity` (`Integer.MIN_VALUE`/`Byte.MIN_VALUE` for Int/Byte) with index `-1`. Trigger: `inLen=5, k=2, stride=2, padding=1, dilation=1, ceilMode=true` → code allocates 4 outputs, the 4th window covers positions 5-6 (both out of range) → `-Inf`, idx `-1`; expected output length is 3. (Assumption: PyTorch semantics are intended, as the parameter set `padding/dilation/ceilMode` indicates.)~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java:258-284` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `conv2d` does not validate `outChannels % groups == 0` (which `conv1d` does at 68-70) nor `kernel.rank() <= 4`. Trigger: `outChannels=3, groups=2` → `outDepth=1`, `chunk(0,true,1)` yields 3 output/kernel slices but the loop runs `groups=2` times, so output channel 2 stays zero silently and kernel slice 2 is never used. Expected: IllegalArgumentException.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28 (the `kernel.rank() <= 4` check was not added).

- ~~`rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java:351-353` and `:517-518` (same lines in Byte/Float/Int) -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `convTranspose2d` and `convTranspose3d` derive `inDepth = inChannels / groups` and `outChannels = outDepth * groups` without checking `inChannels % groups == 0` (checked in `convTranspose1d` at 148-150). Trigger: `inChannels=3, groups=2, weights.dim(0)=3` → `inDepth=1`, three input/kernel chunks are created but only two groups are processed, so the third input channel is silently ignored and the output is wrong with no error. Expected: IllegalArgumentException.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

### Summary

The template-generated dtype variants are mutually consistent (no copy-paste asymmetries between Byte/Int/Float/Double were found); every defect is shared by all four instances of a file. All findings below were fixed 2026-09-28, see Status. The core strided kernels (`binary_`, `fma_`, `reduce1d`, `mmBlocked`, `mvInternal` row path, `copy`, `sameLayoutCopy`, `directCopyTo`, the batched `bmm/bmv/bvtm` dispatch) trace correctly.

Most serious: (1) `varOn`'s inverted mean-shape check rejects the documented input and accepts wrong ones; (2) `diag` on non-square matrices reads out of bounds / returns garbage; (3) `conv1d`/`unfold1d` drop one output position whenever the padded length is not divisible by the stride; (4) `conv1d/conv3d/convTranspose1d/3d` produce wrong results for F-ordered kernels because `reshape` follows storage order while the `unfold` side is C-order; (5) `argmax/argmin` return `-1` for all-`MIN_VALUE`/all-`MAX_VALUE` Int/Byte arrays (a legal input).

Counts (per logical defect, each present in 4 files): bug 7, likely-bug 5, contract 1 = 13 findings. Confidence: 12 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM.

Background facts used below (verified in supporting files):
- `IntStorage.setDouble(ptr, v)` is `setInt(ptr, (int) v)` and `IntStorage.getByte(ptr)` is `(byte) getInt(ptr)`; `DoubleStorage.getByte` is `(byte) getDouble`.
- `DArrayManager.stride(DType.DOUBLE, ..., double[] a)` wraps `a` in `DoubleArrayStorage` WITHOUT copying; for FLOAT/INT/BYTE the array is converted (copied).
- `StrideLoopDescriptor.offsets` has more than one entry whenever the layout cannot be compacted to rank 1 (e.g. `m.narrow(1, 0, 8)` on a 2x16 C-order matrix gives offsets {0,16}, bound 8, step 1). The `*Default` / `applyGeneric*` paths are reached for storages with `supportSimd()==false`, which exist today for float/double (`VarFloatStorage`, `VarDoubleStorage`, produced by `Var.darray()`); no non-SIMD int/byte storage exists today, so int/byte `Default`/`Generic` bugs are latent.

### Findings

#### rapaio/darray/matrix/CholeskyDecomposition.java
Clean. Left and right algorithms match JAMA step by step (index ranges, SPD detection via symmetry check and `d <= 0`, `sqrt(max(d,0))`), forward/backward substitution correct for both `l` and `r.t()`; integer dtype is rejected up front; `solve`/`inv` refuse non-SPD input.

#### rapaio/darray/matrix/EigenDecomposition.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/EigenDecomposition.java:76-101` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Integer-typed input is accepted and produces silent garbage: `vectors = a.copy()` (symmetric path) or `tm.zeros(dt, ...)` keeps the integer dtype, and every `setDouble`/`incDouble` on it truncates to `(int)`. Input: `DArray<Integer>` 2x2 `[[2,1],[1,2]]` -> eigenvectors stored as 0/1 truncations, `v()` is not orthogonal, `power()` wrong. Expected: reject integer dtypes like `CholeskyDecomposition` does (or compute in a floating dtype).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/EigenDecomposition.java:942-953` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `real()`/`imag()` alias internal state only for DOUBLE dtype: `tm.stride(dt, Shape.of(n), Order.C, real)` wraps the internal `double[] real` without copy for `DType.DOUBLE` but copies for `FLOAT`. Input: `eig.real()` then `eig.clampRealValues(1e-9)` (or `eig.real().setDouble(0, 0)`) -> previously returned array changes / `d()` changes, for double but not for float. Expected: consistent (copied) result independent of dtype.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: both `real()` and `imag()` return a clone.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/EigenDecomposition.java:955-961` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `power()` computes `V * pow(diag(D)) * V'`, which is only valid for the symmetric case; for non-symmetric input the 2x2 complex blocks of `d()` are kept un-powered off the diagonal and `V'` is used instead of `V^-1`, so a wrong matrix is returned silently. Input: `[[0,1],[-1,0]]` (eigenvalues ±i), `power(2)` should be `-I`; returned value is not. Expected: either restrict to symmetric (throw) or document.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: `power()` is refused for a non-symmetric matrix.
- Algorithm check: tred2/tql2/orthes/hqr2 and the back-substitution match JAMA line by line, including `cdiv`, the overflow controls and the `iter==10/30` ad-hoc shifts. `reverseOrder()` combined with the swapped `imag>0 -> d[i][i-1]` / `imag<0 -> d[i][i+1]` in `d()` is self-consistent (verified `A*V = V*D` for a reversed complex pair).

#### rapaio/darray/matrix/LUDecomposition.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/LUDecomposition.java:79-92` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Integer dtype accepted: `LU = ref.copy()` keeps int storage, multipliers `LU[i][j]/LU[j][j]` are truncated to `(int)` by `setDouble`, so `l()`, `u()`, `det()`, `solve()` are wrong without any error. Input: `DArray<Integer> [[2,1],[4,3]]` -> multiplier 0.5 stored as 0, `det()` returns 6 instead of 2. Expected: reject integer dtypes (as Cholesky does).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/LUDecomposition.java:245-294` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] For m > n (allowed by the constructor) `solve()`/`inv()` return an m-row result whose rows n..m-1 are just the permuted right-hand side (forward/backward loops only run to `ref.dim(1)`), with no error or least-squares meaning. Input: 3x2 A with `isNonSingular()==true`, `solve(b)` with 3-row b -> shape (3, nx), third row = `b[piv[2]]`. Expected: reject non-square input in `solve`/`inv` (as `det()` does) or document the semantics.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: `solve`/`inv` require a square matrix; `l()`/`u()`/`pivots()` still serve the tall case.
- Algorithm check: Crout and Gaussian elimination variants, pivot search, pivot sign, `l()`/`u()` extraction, `det()` and square `solve()` match JAMA.

#### rapaio/darray/matrix/QRDecomposition.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/QRDecomposition.java:57-97` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Integer dtype accepted; `QR = ref.copy()` keeps int storage and the Householder vectors (`QR[i][k]/nrm`, `QR[k][k]+1`) are truncated to `(int)`, `diag` too. Input: any int matrix -> `q()` not orthogonal, `solve()` wrong, no exception. Expected: reject integer dtypes.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/QRDecomposition.java:57-97,147-168` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] No check for m >= n (Javadoc requires it). For m < n the constructor succeeds (`nrm == 0` for k >= m, `diag[k] = 0`) and `q()` then throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBounds`/layout exception at `q.setDouble(1.0, k, k)` for k >= m; `r()` returns an n x n matrix with zero rows. Input: 2x3 matrix, `qr().q()` -> exception instead of `IllegalArgumentException` at construction. Expected: validate `dim(0) >= dim(1)` (and `rank()==2`) in the constructor.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- Algorithm check: Householder QR, `h()`, `r()`, `q()`, least-squares `solve()` match JAMA.

#### rapaio/darray/matrix/SVDecomposition.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/matrix/SVDecomposition.java:96-117` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Integer dtype accepted; `s`, `u`, `v` are created with the integer dtype, all `setDouble` truncate, so singular values and vectors are garbage (e.g. every `s` value truncated, `u` entries 0/±1) with no exception. Input: any `DArray<Integer>`. Expected: reject integer dtypes.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- Algorithm check: bidiagonalization, U/V generation, kase selection and the four cases match the Commons-Math/JAMA reference; `tol`, `rank()`, `conditionNumber()` consistent; `m < n` correctly rejected.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/Broadcast.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/Broadcast.java:48-74` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Zero-length dimensions are not broadcastable against 1: `max` becomes 1, the shape with `size == 0` is neither `== max` nor `== 1`, so `elementWise(Shape.of(0), Shape.of(1))` returns `valid=false`, whereas the broadcast rule (numpy) yields shape (0). Input: empty (0,) array combined with a (1,) array -> "not compatible" instead of an empty result. Expected: treat 0 vs 1 as broadcastable to 0.~~ Moot: `Shape` rejects a zero dimension in its constructor (`Invalid shape dimensions`), so a `(0,)` shape cannot be built. Withdrawn 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpNanSum.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpNanSum.java:158-163,183-188,223-228,248-253` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] The final `a.test(IS_NAN)` mask silently drops a lane whose partial sum became NaN via `+Inf + -Inf`, while the scalar tail and the `Default` path propagate NaN. Expected: consistent IEEE result (NaN) regardless of element position.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: the final mask is gone in `NanSum`, `NanProd` and `NanMean`, so a NaN produced by the arithmetic propagates on every path.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpProd.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpProd.java:49,69,101,121,153,173,205,225` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same hoisted-accumulator defect as ReduceOpNanProd: `a = Simd.broadcast(1)` is outside the offsets loop, `result *= a.reduceLanes(MUL)` inside it. Input: 2x16 double C-order matrix `m` with row products P0, P1; `m.narrow(1, 0, 8).prod()` -> P0*P0*P1 instead of P0*P1 (all byte/int/float/double SIMD paths). Contiguous arrays (single offset) are unaffected, which is why tests likely pass. Expected: reset `a` per offset (compare `ReduceOpSum`).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpProd.java:139-147` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `reduceIntDefault` uses `storage.getByte(p)` instead of `getInt(p)` (latent; int {300} -> 44).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpSum.java
Clean.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpVarc.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/impl/ReduceOpVarc.java:111,142,163,199,230,250` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] The compensated-variance correction term divides by `(size - ddof)` instead of `size`: `(sum2 - sum3*sum3/(size-ddof)) / (size-ddof)`. The corrected two-pass formula (Numerical Recipes / Welford) is `(sum2 - sum3^2/n) / (n - ddof)`. With the data mean this is only a rounding difference, but with the public `var(ddof, mean)` overload and a mean that differs from the sample mean the result is wrong. Input: {0, 2}, `var(1, 0.0)`: sum2 = 4, sum3 = 2, size = 2 -> (4 - 4/1)/1 = 0; correct compensated value is (4 - 4/2)/1 = 2 (and the naive variance about 0 would be 4); 0 is wrong under either reading. Expected: divide the correction by `size`.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: divides by `size`; `var(1, 0.0)` of `{0, 2}` is now 2 for any shift, and the Javadoc states that the mean is a shift.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpAbs.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpAbs.java:131-139` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `applyGenericInt` advances with `p++` instead of `p += loop.step`, so for strided int views only every `step`-th element is wrong/ignored and out-of-view elements are touched. Latent today (no non-SIMD int storage), but the code is wrong: a step-3 view of {-1,x,x,-2,x,x} would abs() elements 0,1 instead of 0,3. Expected: `p += loop.step` as in `applyGenericByte/Float/Double`.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpNeg.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpNeg.java:131-139` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same generated template defect: `applyGenericInt` uses `p++` instead of `p += loop.step` (latent).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpSqr.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpSqr.java:131-139` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same generated template defect: `applyGenericInt` uses `p++` instead of `p += loop.step` (latent).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpCompareMask.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpCompareMask.java:203,224,245` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] All three double paths compute the reference as `double ref = value.floatValue();` (the float paths at 149/170/191 correctly use `floatValue()`), so doubles are compared against a float-rounded constant. Input: double array {0.1}, `compareMask(Compare.EQ, 0.1)` -> ref = 0.10000000149011612, mask = 0 instead of 1; `LT 0.1` on element 0.1 -> true instead of false. Expected: `value.doubleValue()`.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpLogSoftmax.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpLogSoftmax.java:292-312` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `applyGenericDouble` never takes the logarithm: after accumulating `logsum = sum(exp(x - max))` it writes `x - max - logsum`, whereas every other path does `logsum = Math.log(logsum)` first (lines 103, 152, 184, 226, 275). Reachable today via a `VarDouble`-backed DArray (`VarDoubleStorage.supportSimd()==false`): `VarDouble.wrap(0,0).darray().logsoftmax_()` -> each element becomes 0 - 0 - 2 = -2 instead of -log(2) = -0.693. Expected: `logsum = Math.log(logsum)` before the last loop.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpLogSoftmax.java:88-97,137-146,211-220,260-269` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `vlogsum` is created once before the offsets loop and `logsum += vlogsum.reduceLanes(ADD)` runs once per offset, so the SIMD partial sums of earlier offsets are added again for each later offset (offsets {0,16}: logsum = S0 + (S0+S1) = 2*S0 + S1). Input: `m.narrow(1, 0, 8).logsoftmax_()` on a 2x16 double matrix -> exp of the results sums to less than 1. Expected: reset `vlogsum` per offset (the `Generic` paths, which have no vector accumulator, are correct apart from the finding above).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpSoftmax.java
- ~~`rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOpSoftmax.java:87-100,139-152,218-231,270-283` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same hoisted-accumulator defect: `vsum` is created once before the offsets loop while `sum += vsum.reduceLanes(ADD)` runs per offset, so the normaliser over-counts earlier offsets and the output does not sum to 1. Input: 2x16 double C-order matrix of zeros, `m.narrow(1, 0, 8).softmax_()` -> offsets {0,16}, S0 = S1 = 8, sum = 8 + 16 = 24, every element = 1/24 instead of 1/16 (total 2/3). `unary1d_`/`softmax1d_` (rank-1 slices, single offset) are unaffected. Expected: `vsum = Simd.zero…()` inside the offsets loop.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

#### rapaio/darray/operator/unary/UnaryOp{Acos,Asin,Atan,Ceil,Clamp,Cos,Cosh,Exp,Expm1,Fill,FillNan,Floor,Log,Log1p,NanToNum,Pow,Rint,Sigmoid,Sin,Sinh,Sqrt,Tan,Tanh}.java
Clean. The 14 generated transcendental ops are byte-identical apart from the operator; Ceil/Floor/Rint correctly no-op on integer dtypes; Clamp/Fill/FillNan/NanToNum/Sigmoid/Pow have matching SIMD and scalar semantics and correct `p += step` in all generic paths.

### Summary

The decompositions are faithful JAMA/Commons-Math ports and I found no arithmetic or indexing error in them; their defects are input validation (all fixed 2026-09-28, see Status) (LU/QR/SVD/Eigen silently accept integer dtypes and produce truncated garbage; QR accepts m < n and then throws from `q()`; LU `solve` on m > n returns partly meaningless rows) plus dtype-dependent aliasing in `EigenDecomposition.real()/imag()`.

The operator layer has real numerical bugs (all fixed 2026-09-28, see Status), mostly copy-paste asymmetries between dtype variants: (1) a systematic hoisted-SIMD-accumulator error in `ReduceOpProd`, `ReduceOpNanProd`, `UnaryOpSoftmax`, `UnaryOpLogSoftmax` that double-counts partial results whenever the loop has more than one offset (any non-contiguous 2-D+ view), (2) `UnaryOpLogSoftmax.applyGenericDouble` forgets `Math.log`, (3) `UnaryOpCompareMask` compares doubles against a float-rounded reference, (4) `ReduceOpNanMean` float-unit tail has its NaN test inverted, (5) `ReduceOpMin`/`ReduceOpNanMin` float default paths start at 0, (6) `ReduceOpNanProd` double-unit tail reads floats, (7) `ReduceOpVarc` uses `n-ddof` in the correction term. Items 1-6 are reachable today (non-contiguous views, or `Var`-backed float/double arrays); the `getByte`-in-int and `p++`-in-generic-int defects are latent because no non-SIMD int storage exists yet.

Counts: bug 20 (17 HIGH, 3 MEDIUM-confidence latent/asymmetry), likely-bug 4, contract 7. Most serious: softmax/logsoftmax/prod over-counting on views, logsoftmax generic-double missing log, CompareMask double precision loss, NanMean tail inversion, Min/NanMin float default returning 0.

### Findings

#### rapaio/data/AbstractFrame.java

- `rapaio/data/AbstractFrame.java:49-72` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `toString()` for frames with more than 20 variables appends the last 4 variables without a `"\n"` separator (line 67), so they are concatenated onto one line, unlike the head variables. Input: any frame with 21+ vars; the last four `Var.toString()` outputs run together.
- `rapaio/data/AbstractFrame.java:110-119` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `toSummary` creates `Executors.newFixedThreadPool(availableProcessors() - 1)`; on a 1-CPU host (containers, CI runners) this is `newFixedThreadPool(0)` and throws `IllegalArgumentException` before any output. `executor.shutdown()` (119) is not in a `finally`, so if any `fillSummary` throws (e.g. `Quantiles` on an all-missing column) the non-daemon pool threads leak and keep the JVM alive.
- `rapaio/data/AbstractFrame.java:141-188` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `selection(head, tail, ...)` does not clamp `tail` when `head < rowCount < head + tail`. `dots` becomes 0 (156) and the tail loop (166, 183) prints rows `rowCount - tail .. rowCount - 1`, which overlap the head rows. Input: a 12-row frame, `toContent` (head 10, tail 5) prints rows 0..9 followed by rows 7..11, so rows 7, 8, 9 appear twice and the table has 16 data rows for a 12-row frame.

#### rapaio/data/AbstractVar.java

- `rapaio/data/AbstractVar.java:120` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `writeObject` calls `out.writeUTF(name)`; `Var.name(String)` accepts `null`, so serializing a variable whose name was set to `null` throws `NullPointerException` (default serialization would have handled the null).
- `rapaio/data/AbstractVar.java:310-331` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `toContent` for `size() > 100` fills table rows 1..80 with data rows 0..79 (line 318: `tt.intRow(i + 1, 0, i)` for `i < 80`, so data row 79 lands in table row 80), then overwrites table row 80 with `"..."` (323-324). Data row 79 is silently hidden and the 102-row table leaves its last row (101) empty. Should loop `i < 79` or place `"..."` at row 81.

#### rapaio/data/BoundVar.java

- `rapaio/data/BoundVar.java:66-96` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] In the constructor the non-bound branch records the cumulative boundary as `counts.get(i) + last` (90) but advances `last` by `vars.get(i).size()` (92) instead of `counts.get(i)`. When a supplied count differs from the variable size (exactly why `BoundFrame.byRows` passes frame row counts, see its comment "avoid to take rowCount from variable"), the boundaries become inconsistent. Input: vars A(size 5, count 3), B(size 4, count 4), C(size 2, count 2) -> `counts = [3, 9, 11]`, `rowCount = 9`; `getDouble(7)` should read C[0] but `findIndex(7)` returns 1 (B) with `localRow = 4`, past B's size (garbage or exception). Triggered via `SolidFrame.byVars(3, VarDouble.seq(10)).bindRows(...)`.
- `rapaio/data/BoundVar.java:98-106` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `findIndex` uses `Collections.binarySearch(counts, row)` and returns `pos + 1` on an exact hit. With two or more consecutive empty variables the cumulative `counts` list contains repeated values and `binarySearch` may return the first duplicate. Input: vars A(3 rows), B(0), C(0), D(2) -> `counts = [3,3,3,5]`; `binarySearch(3)` returns 1, `pos = 2` (C, empty), `localRow = 0`; `getDouble(3)` reads C[0] (AIOOBE on an empty `VarDouble`) instead of D[0].
- `rapaio/data/BoundVar.java:305-311` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `textTablePutValue` delegates to `vars.get(0).textTablePutValue(..., row, ...)` with the *global* row and always the first variable, ignoring `findIndex`/`localRow`. `toContent`/`toFullContent` of a bound var prints values of the first source only, and for `row >= vars.get(0).size()` reads past the first source's size (stale capacity data or exception). Input: `BoundVar.from(VarDouble.wrap(1,2), VarDouble.wrap(3,4)).printContent()` shows rows 2 and 3 taken from the 2-element first array.
- `rapaio/data/BoundVar.java:226-228` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `levels()` returns only `vars.get(0).levels()`, while `getInt(row)` returns the level index of whichever source var owns the row. For NOMINAL bound vars with different dictionaries the int view is inconsistent with `levels()` (same index means different labels in different segments). `getLabel` remains correct.

#### rapaio/data/Frame.java

- `rapaio/data/Frame.java:228-237` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `removeRows(Mapping)` sizes `map` as `rowCount() - remove.size()`; if the mapping contains any row `>= rowCount()`, fewer rows are actually skipped than counted and `map[pos++]` throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` at the end of the loop instead of a clear validation error. Input: `df.removeRows(Mapping.wrap(rowCount()))`.

#### rapaio/data/Group.java

- `rapaio/data/Group.java:629-637` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `toFrame(unstackLevel > 0)` switches on the aggregate var type handling only DOUBLE, BINARY and INT; FLOAT, LONG, NOMINAL, STRING, INSTANT hit `default -> throw`. Any `GroupFun` producing a LONG or FLOAT column (the current built-ins appear to produce DOUBLE/INT, hence MEDIUM) makes unstacking fail even though `setLong`/`setFloat`/`setLabel` exist on `Frame`.
- `rapaio/data/Group.java:691-700,745-752` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `selectedContent`: the "show everything" test is `headRows + tailRows > aggregateDf.rowCount()`; when the group count is exactly `headRows + tailRows` (39 groups with the default 30/9) it takes the head/tail path, and at `pos == headRows + 1` the values of group index 30 are overwritten with `"..."` although every group fits. Off-by-one, should be `>=`.

#### rapaio/data/MappedFrame.java

- `rapaio/data/MappedFrame.java:153-157` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `clearRows()` calls `this.mapping.clear()` on the mapping object handed in by the caller (the constructor stores it without copying when the source is not itself a `MappedFrame`). Input: `Mapping m = Mapping.wrap(0,1,2); Frame a = df.mapRows(m); Frame b = df.mapRows(m); a.clearRows();` -> `b.rowCount() == 0` and `m.size() == 0`. All `MappedVar`s previously obtained via `rvar()` collapse too.
- `rapaio/data/MappedFrame.java:264-267` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The override `isMissing(int row)` delegates to `source.isMissing(mapping.get(row))`, which scans *all* source columns, not the columns selected in this mapped frame. Input: source with vars `a` (complete) and `b` (missing at row 0); `df.mapRows(Mapping.range(0,n)).mapVars("a").isMissing(0)` returns `true` although the frame has only `a`. Affects `stream().complete()` counts and anything filtering complete rows on a column-projected mapped frame.
- `rapaio/data/MappedFrame.java:129-131,175-177,190,205,220,235,250,260,275` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] All the `(row, String varName)` accessors and `type(varName)` resolve the name directly in `source`, bypassing this frame's column selection, while `varIndex(name)` returns -1 for the same name. Input: `df.mapRows(...).mapVars("a").getDouble(0, "b")` returns a value from a column the frame claims not to have, instead of failing.

#### rapaio/data/MappedVar.java

- `rapaio/data/MappedVar.java:224-231` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `removeRow` and `clearRows` mutate the shared `Mapping`. `MappedFrame.rvar(int)` builds each `MappedVar` over the frame's own mapping object, so `mf.rvar(0).removeRow(0)` shrinks `mf.rowCount()` and shifts every other column of the frame; likewise `var.mapRows(m).clearRows()` empties the caller's `m`.

#### rapaio/data/SolidFrame.java

- `rapaio/data/SolidFrame.java:139-160` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] The constructor accepts duplicate variable names silently: `colIndex.put` (155) keeps only the last position, so `rvar("x")`, `varIndex("x")` and all name-based accessors hit the last duplicate while `varNames()` lists both. `BoundFrame` rejects this case. Input: `SolidFrame.byVars(VarDouble.empty(3).name("x"), VarInt.empty(3).name("x"))` produces a frame where `mapVars("x")` yields the INT column twice.
- `rapaio/data/SolidFrame.java:173-175` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `varNames()` returns the internal `names` array (Javadoc: "Returns an array of variable names"); `df.varNames()[0] = "z"` desynchronises `names` from `colIndex`. Same in `MappedFrame.varNames()` (line 100) and `BoundFrame.varNames()` (line 238).

#### rapaio/data/Var.java

- `rapaio/data/Var.java:131-141` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `removeRows(Mapping)` has the same over-count problem as `Frame.removeRows`: a mapping containing a row `>= size()` makes `map` too short and the loop throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`. Input: `VarDouble.wrap(1,2,3).removeRows(Mapping.wrap(5))`.
- `rapaio/data/Var.java:333-343,349-359` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `setLevels` Javadoc says the result has "indexes 1,2,1,1,1,1"; `VarNominal.setLevels` produces 0-based indexes `0,1,0,0,0,0` (missing is -1). Stale 1-based description.

#### rapaio/data/VarBinary.java

- `rapaio/data/VarBinary.java:44-49` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Class Javadoc states missing values are returned as `Integer.MIN_VALUE` / `Long.MIN_VALUE` and that the variable "does not allows update operations"; the code returns `VarInt.MISSING_VALUE == Integer.MAX_VALUE`, `VarLong.MISSING_VALUE == Long.MAX_VALUE`, and supports every `setX`.
- `rapaio/data/VarBinary.java:315-320,439-441` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `isMissing`, `getInt`, `getDouble`, `getFloat`, `getLong`, `getLabel` never check `row < rows`; `BitSet.get` returns `false` for any index beyond the set, so `VarBinary.empty().getInt(1000)` returns `0` (a valid, non-missing value) for a variable of size 0. Every other Var implementation at least throws once past its capacity.
- `rapaio/data/VarBinary.java:474-485` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `readObject` restores `rows`, `values`, `missing` but not `parser`/`formatter`; the custom `writeObject` does not write them and field initialisers do not run during deserialization, so both are `null` after a round trip and `getLabel`/`setLabel`/`addLabel` throw `NullPointerException`. `VarNominal` handles this correctly (writes/reads both objects).

#### rapaio/data/VarDouble.java

- `rapaio/data/VarDouble.java:227-235` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `seq(double end)` Javadoc says "exclusive end"; the implementation (via `seq(0, end, 1)`, `while (current <= end)`) is inclusive, and the sibling overloads document inclusive. `VarDouble.seq(3)` yields 0,1,2,3.
- `rapaio/data/VarDouble.java:257-267` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `seq(start, end, step)` has no validation of `step`: `step == 0` (or negative with `start <= end`) loops forever growing the array until OOM. Input: `VarDouble.seq(0, 1, 0)`. Same in `VarFloat.seq` (230-240).
- `rapaio/data/VarDouble.java:431-441,446-448,480-490` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `getDouble`, `setDouble`, `isMissing`, `getFloat`, `setFloat`, `getLabel`, `setLabel`, `getInstant` index `data[row]` without checking `row < rows`; after `removeRow`/`clearRows` (which only decrement `rows`) stale values in `[rows, data.length)` are readable and writable with no exception. Input: `VarDouble v = VarDouble.wrap(1,2,3); v.removeRow(2); v.getDouble(2)` returns `3.0` for a variable of size 2. Same pattern in `VarFloat` (404-414/419/445-455), `VarInt` (296-304/445/460-470), `VarLong` (485-493/508/523-533), `VarNominal` (249-258/282/447), `VarInstant` (145-156/283). `checkRowIndex` exists in `AbstractVar` but is only used by `removeRow`.
- `rapaio/data/VarDouble.java:496-501` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `getInt` returns `(int) Math.rint(data[row])`, which saturates. A present value `>= 2147483647.5` (or `+Infinity`) yields `Integer.MAX_VALUE == VarInt.MISSING_VALUE`, so a stored, non-missing number reads back as *missing* through the int view; large negative values wrap to `Integer.MIN_VALUE` silently. Input: `VarDouble.wrap(3e9).getInt(0) == VarInt.MISSING_VALUE`. `getLong` (539-544) likewise saturates to `Long.MAX_VALUE == VarLong.MISSING_VALUE` for `>= 9.2e18` / `+Infinity`.
- `rapaio/data/VarDouble.java:610-625` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Custom `writeObject`/`readObject` skip `parser` and `formatter`; after deserialization both are `null` and `getLabel`, `setLabel`, `addLabel`, `textTablePutValue` throw `NullPointerException`. Reproduce: serialize/deserialize any `VarDouble`, call `getLabel(0)`.

#### rapaio/data/VarFloat.java

- `rapaio/data/VarFloat.java:466-471,509-514` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same saturation defect as `VarDouble.getInt`/`getLong`: a present value `>= 2^31` reads as `VarInt.MISSING_VALUE`, `>= 2^63` as `VarLong.MISSING_VALUE`. Input: `VarFloat.wrap(3e9f).getInt(0) == VarInt.MISSING_VALUE`.
- `rapaio/data/VarFloat.java:527-543,549-554` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `addInstant`/`setInstant` store `value.toEpochMilli()` in a `float`, which has a 24-bit mantissa; current epoch millis (~1.7e12) lose the low ~17 bits (granularity ~131 s). `getInstant` then returns a different instant than the one set. Input: `VarFloat f = VarFloat.empty(); f.addInstant(Instant.parse("2024-01-01T00:00:00.123Z")); f.getInstant(0)` is off by up to about two minutes.
- `rapaio/data/VarFloat.java:566-581` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same serialization defect as `VarDouble`: `parser`/`formatter` are not written or restored; `getLabel` NPEs after deserialization.

#### rapaio/data/VarInt.java

- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:56,185` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Class Javadoc: "Missing value is `Integer.MIN_VALUE`"; code: `MISSING_VALUE = Integer.MAX_VALUE`. Callers following the Javadoc will store `Integer.MIN_VALUE` as a regular value and never see it as missing.
- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:279-285` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `rowCount(int rowCount)` accepts negative values: `rowCount(-5)` sets `rows = -5`, `size()` becomes negative and `intStream()`/`iterator()` throw. No validation.
- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:288-294` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `addRows(rowCount)` with a negative argument silently decreases `rows` (the fill loop does not execute), truncating data with no error. Same in `VarLong.addRows` (377-383), `VarNominal.addRows` (240-246), `VarBinary.addRows` (223-229), `VarInstant.addRows` (128-131). `VarDouble`/`VarFloat` throw from `Arrays.fill`.
- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:323-325` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `iterator(start, end)` does no bounds validation; `end > rows` iterates stale capacity values silently (or AIOOBE past capacity), `start > end` yields an empty iterator.
- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:340-367` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `setFloat`/`addFloat`/`setDouble`/`addDouble` convert with `(int) Math.rint(value)`, which saturates to `Integer.MAX_VALUE == MISSING_VALUE`. Input: `VarInt v = VarInt.empty(); v.addDouble(3e9); v.isMissing(0)` is `true`: a present value silently became missing; `+Infinity` does the same; `-3e9` becomes `Integer.MIN_VALUE` garbage. The `Var` Javadoc promises only `NaN` maps to missing.
- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:419-434` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `addInstant`/`setInstant` store `(int) value.toEpochMilli()`, truncating a long to its low 32 bits. Any instant after 1970-01-25 (millis > 2^31) is stored as garbage and `getInstant` returns a wrong date. Input: `v.addInstant(Instant.parse("2024-01-01T00:00:00Z")); v.getInstant(0)` -> a date in 1970 (some values also collide with `MISSING_VALUE`). `Math.toIntExact` is used elsewhere in this class for the same conversion.
- `rapaio/data/VarInt.java:485-499` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same serialization defect as `VarDouble`: `parser`/`formatter` not written/restored; `getLabel` NPEs after deserialization.

#### rapaio/data/VarInstant.java

- `rapaio/data/VarInstant.java:200-205` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `getInt` uses `Math.toIntExact(epochMilli)` and therefore throws `ArithmeticException` for every instant after 1970-01-25, i.e. for essentially every real value; `Frame.getInt(row, instantCol)` and `RowComparators.integerComparator` over an INSTANT var are unusable. The `Var` Javadoc lists `getInt` as an available accessor that returns `MISSING_VALUE` on missing rows but says nothing about throwing on present rows.

#### rapaio/data/VarLong.java

- `rapaio/data/VarLong.java:411-433` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `setFloat`/`addFloat`/`setDouble`/`addDouble` use `(long) Math.rint(value)`, which saturates to `Long.MAX_VALUE == MISSING_VALUE`; `addDouble(1e19)` or `addDouble(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` silently stores a missing value instead of the number or an error.
- `rapaio/data/VarLong.java:566-569` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `textTablePutValue` prints `String.valueOf(getLong(row))`, bypassing `getLabel`/the formatter; missing rows print as `9223372036854775807` instead of `?` in `toContent`/`toFullContent`, and a custom formatter is ignored.

#### rapaio/data/VarNominal.java

- `rapaio/data/VarNominal.java:286-293` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `setInt(row, value)` validates only `-1 <= value <= Short.MAX_VALUE - 1`, not `value < dict.size()`. Input: `VarNominal.empty(1, "a", "b").setInt(0, 5)` succeeds; `getLabel(0)` later throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException` from `dict.get(5)` while `isMissing(0)` is `false`. `addInt` (297-303) correctly fails fast via `dict.get(value)`. (The upper bound `Short.MAX_VALUE - 1 = 32766` is also one past the maximum reachable level index 32765.)
- `rapaio/data/VarNominal.java:347-351,364-370` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `setLabel`/`addLabel` call `value.equals(MISSING_VALUE)` after the identity parser, so a `null` label throws `NullPointerException`; `VarString.addLabel(null)` and the `VarBinary`/`VarInstant` parsers accept the same input. Nothing in `Var.addLabel` forbids `null`.
- `rapaio/data/VarNominal.java:382-385` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `levels()` returns the internal `dict` `ArrayList` by reference. Mutating it (`var.levels().remove(0)` or `.add("z")`) desynchronises `dict` from `reverse` and from stored indexes: after `remove(0)` every row's `getLabel` shifts to the next level and the last index throws. `setLevels` is the intended mutator. `MappedVar.levels()`/`BoundVar.levels()` propagate the same reference.
- `rapaio/data/VarNominal.java:388-413` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `setLevels` does not filter the `"?"` label that `empty(rows, dict)` filters out. `setLevels("a", "?")` makes `"?"` a real level: rows with that index have `isMissing == false` but `getLabel == "?"`, and a later `setLabel(row, "?")` on them sets `-1` instead. Ambiguous state between missing and the `"?"` level.

#### rapaio/data/VarRange.java

- `rapaio/data/VarRange.java:161,232-282` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `parseVarIndexes` Javadoc: "If a variable is not found, then it will be omitted." Numeric ranges past the last column are not omitted: `VarRange.of("0~100").parseVarNames(df)` on a 3-column frame adds indexes 3..100 (265-267) and `parseVarNames` (282) throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` from `df.varNames()[i]`. Likewise `VarRange.of(-2)` (a negative index other than -1) adds `-2`.
- `rapaio/data/VarRange.java:240-249` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] A range token with a trailing separator (`"a~"`) makes `range.split("~")` return one element and `parts[1]` throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` rather than a parse error or omission.

#### rapaio/data/mapping/ArrayMapping.java

- `rapaio/data/mapping/ArrayMapping.java:96-101` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `removeAll(positions)` assumes the positions arrive in strictly ascending order (`remove(pos - offset++)`); the `Mapping.removeAll` Javadoc states no such precondition. Input: `Mapping.wrap(10,11,12,13).removeAll(IntStream.of(2,1).iterator())` removes position 2 then position `1-1 = 0`, giving `[11,13]` instead of `[10,13]`; `(3,0)` throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException(-1)`.

#### rapaio/data/mapping/IntervalMapping.java

- `rapaio/data/mapping/IntervalMapping.java:58-63` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `get(pos)` returns `pos + start` with no range check while in interval mode; `Mapping.range(0,3).get(7) == 7`. A `MappedVar`/`MappedFrame` over such a mapping reads source rows outside its declared size without error (`ArrayMapping.get` throws in the same situation).
- `rapaio/data/mapping/IntervalMapping.java:115-118` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `elements()` always returns `Ints.seq(start, end)`, ignoring `listMapping` once `onList` is true. `Mapping.from(mapping, fun)` and `Mapping.copy(mapping, ...)` use `elements()` together with `size()`, so after any `add`/`remove` the two disagree. Input: `Mapping m = Mapping.range(0,3); m.add(7); mappedVar.mapRows(m)` (composition path in the `MappedVar`/`MappedFrame` constructors) throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` (size 4, array length 3); after `m.remove(0)` it silently maps the wrong rows.
- `rapaio/data/mapping/IntervalMapping.java:120-125` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `shuffle` unconditionally replaces `listMapping` with a fresh `ArrayMapping(start, end)`, discarding all prior `add`/`remove`/`clear` modifications. Input: `Mapping m = Mapping.range(0,3); m.remove(0); m.shuffle(rnd)` -> size back to 3 and contains 0.

#### rapaio/data/string/StringBag.java

- `rapaio/data/string/StringBag.java:76-96` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `compareTo(null)` returns 1 instead of throwing `NullPointerException` as `Comparable` requires, and `map.get(key).compareTo(...)` (91) throws NPE when a value is `null` (possible via `VarString` labels), while `equals` handles null values. `TreeSet<StringBag>` behaviour differs from `HashSet<StringBag>` on such inputs.

#### rapaio/data/finance/FinBarSize.java

- `rapaio/data/finance/FinBarSize.java:38` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `_15min` is declared as `Duration.of(115, ChronoUnit.MINUTES)`; `FinBarSize._15min.duration()` is 115 minutes, not 15. Typo in the constant.

### Summary

Overall the code is straightforward and mostly correct; the interfaces (`Var`, `Frame`, `VarType`, `Unique`) are clean and the exhaustive switches on `VarType` are complete except the unstack switch in `Group.Aggregate.toFrame`. The defects cluster in four places: (1) **numeric conversions into integer-backed vars**: `(int)/(long) Math.rint(...)` saturates to `Integer.MAX_VALUE`/`Long.MAX_VALUE`, which are exactly the missing sentinels, so large present values silently become missing (`VarInt`, `VarLong`, and the `getInt`/`getLong` views of `VarDouble`/`VarFloat`); `VarInt.addInstant` truncates epoch millis to 32 bits and `VarFloat.addInstant` loses ~2 minutes of precision. (2) **Serialization**: `VarDouble`, `VarFloat`, `VarInt`, `VarBinary` drop `parser`/`formatter` in their custom `readObject`, so any deserialized instance NPEs on `getLabel`. (3) **View/mapping aliasing**: `MappedVar.removeRow/clearRows` and `MappedFrame.clearRows` mutate the caller's shared `Mapping`, `MappedFrame.isMissing(row)` checks columns not in the frame, `IntervalMapping.elements()/shuffle()` ignore list-mode edits, `ArrayMapping.removeAll` assumes sorted input, and `BoundVar` mixes supplied counts with actual sizes. (4) **Display code off-by-ones**: `AbstractVar.toContent` hides row 79, `AbstractFrame.selection` prints rows twice for `head < rowCount < head+tail`, `BoundVar.textTablePutValue` prints the wrong source, `VarLong` prints raw sentinels, `Group.selectedContent` overwrites a real group with `...`, `toSummary` fails on 1-CPU hosts. Plus a stand-alone constant typo (`FinBarSize._15min` = 115 minutes) and Javadoc contracts naming the wrong missing sentinel (`VarInt`, `VarBinary`) or 1-based indexes (`Var.setLevels`).

Counts: **bug** 24, **likely-bug** 14, **contract** 10 (48 findings; 47 HIGH confidence, 1 MEDIUM). Most serious: VarInt/VarLong/VarDouble/VarFloat saturation-to-missing, VarInt instant truncation, the four `readObject` NPEs, `MappedFrame.isMissing(row)`, `IntervalMapping.elements()/shuffle()`, the `BoundVar` count/size mix-up, and `FinBarSize._15min`.
