### Findings

#### rapaio/data/transform

- `rapaio/data/transform/AddDoubleApply.java:38` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Transform extends Serializable` and the class declares a `serialVersionUID`-style contract via `AbstractTransform`, but the field `ToDoubleFunction<FSpot> function` is a `java.util.function` type; any lambda passed to `AddDoubleApply.from(...)` is not `Serializable`, so `ObjectOutputStream.writeObject(transform)` throws `NotSerializableException`. Should use the library's `SFunction`/serializable functional types as `AddNominalApply` already does.

- `rapaio/data/transform/ApplyTransform.java:65,68` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Same Serializable violation: `Consumer<FSpot> spotConsumer` and `Function<String, String> stringFunction` hold non-serializable lambdas (`onSpot(...)`, `onLabel(...)`), so serializing the transform fails with `NotSerializableException`. (`doubleFunction`/`intFunction` are fine: rapaio's `Double2DoubleFunction`/`Int2IntFunction` extend `SFunction`.)

- `rapaio/data/transform/Jitter.java:86` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `df.setDouble(i, varIndex, df.getDouble(i, varIndex) + d.sampleNext(random))` is applied to every variable in the range regardless of type. On an `INT` variable `VarInt.setDouble` does `(int) Math.rint(value)`, so with the default `Normal(0, 0.1)` the jitter is silently rounded away (value 5 + 0.04 -> 5) and with larger sd it is quantized; on a `BINARY` variable any result other than exactly 0.0/1.0 becomes missing. Input: `Jitter.on(VarRange.all()).fitApply(df)` on a frame with an int column. Expected: either a real-valued jittered result or a rejection of non-floating-point vars (as `VarStandardScaler` does); actual: silent no-op / silent data corruption.

- `rapaio/data/transform/PCATransform.java:83` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `BiFunction<DArray<Double>, DArray<Double>, Integer> kFun` is always a non-serializable lambda (created in every factory), so a fitted `PCATransform` (declared `Serializable`, has `serialVersionUID`) cannot be serialized: `NotSerializableException`.

- `rapaio/data/transform/RandomProjection.java:60,100-102` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Method` is a plain (non-`Serializable`) functional interface and `method` always holds a lambda from `gaussian(k)`/`achlioptas(s)`; serialization of the transform throws `NotSerializableException` despite the class declaring `serialVersionUID`.

- `rapaio/data/transform/RefSort.java:45` and `rapaio/data/transform/VarRefSort.java:45` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `rapaio.util.IntComparator` does not extend `Serializable`; `RowComparators.from(...)` and user-supplied comparators are lambdas, so both transforms fail to serialize even though they carry a `serialVersionUID`.

- `rapaio/data/transform/RenameVars.java:77-85` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `vars.add(var.name(outputVarNames[index]))` calls `AbstractVar.name(String)`, which mutates the variable in place (`this.name = name; return this;`). The input frame is therefore modified: after `RenameVars.of(VarRange.of("a"), "b").fitApply(df)`, `df.rvar(0).name()` is `"b"` while `df.varNames()` still says `"a"` and `SolidFrame.colIndex` still maps `"a"` -> 0, so `df.rvar("b")` returns `null`, `df.rvar("a")` returns the var now named `"b"`, and the transform is not idempotent (applying it to the same frame again finds no var named `"a"` and renames nothing). Expected: return a frame with renamed *views/copies* and leave the source untouched. Also `outputVarNames[index]` is never length-checked against the parsed range (fewer output names than matched vars -> `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` at apply time instead of a clear error at construction).

- `rapaio/data/transform/VarApply.java:77,80` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Same Serializable violation as `ApplyTransform`: `Consumer<VSpot>` and `Function<String,String>` fields hold non-serializable lambdas.

- `rapaio/data/transform/VarBoxCoxTransform.java:69-76` and `rapaio/data/transform/VarPowerTransform.java:70-80` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] Both write `Math.log(...)` / `Math.pow(...)` results back with `var.setDouble(i, ...)` into the *source* variable with no type check. For an `INT` (or `BINARY`) var the real-valued result is rounded to an int (`VarInt.setDouble` uses `Math.rint`), e.g. Box-Cox with lambda=0 on int values 1..10 yields 0,1,1,1,2,2,2,2,2,2 instead of 0, 0.69, 1.10, ... Expected: reject non-floating-point vars or return a new double var; actual: silent truncation. (`BoxCoxTransform` at the frame level inherits this.)

- `rapaio/data/transform/VarQuantileTransform.java:93-107,116-127` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Interval predicates are stored in a `HashMap` keyed by their *display label*, and labels are built with `Format.floatFlexShort` (max 3 decimals). Two distinct quantile boundaries that round to the same 3-decimal string produce identical labels, and the second `predicates.put` silently overwrites the first predicate. Trigger: quantile values `qv = [1.0001, 1.0002, 1.0003]` (e.g. `VarQuantileTransform.split(4)` on data clustered in `[1.0000, 1.0004]`): labels are `"-Inf~1"`, `"1~1"` (i=1), `"1~1"` (i=2, overwrites i=1's predicate `1.0001 < x <= 1.0002`), `"1~Inf"`. In `coreApply`, a value 1.00015 matches *no* remaining predicate, so no label is appended for that row and the result var is shorter than the input and misaligned with every later row. Expected: one label appended per row (predicates keyed by index, not by formatted label).

- `rapaio/data/transform/VarQuantileTransform.java:71` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Map<String, Predicate<Double>> predicates` holds lambdas capturing `qv`; `java.util.function.Predicate` is not `Serializable`, so a fitted `VarQuantileTransform` (and a fitted `QuantileTransform`, whose `filters` map contains them) throws `NotSerializableException` on serialization despite declaring `serialVersionUID`.

- `rapaio/data/transform/VarToDoubleTransform.java:116-122,188-193` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `DefaultByType` for `NOMINAL, STRING` calls `Double.parseDouble(x)` and only catches `NumberFormatException`. `VarString.getLabel` returns `null` for a `null` entry (which `VarString.isMissing` treats as missing), and `Double.parseDouble(null)` throws `NullPointerException`, not `NumberFormatException`. Trigger: `VarToDoubleTransform.byDefault().fapply(VarString.copy("1", null))`. Expected: missing value; actual: NPE. (`VarToIntTransform` is unaffected because `Integer.parseInt(null)` throws `NumberFormatException`.) `TransformLabel` also never checks `isMissing`, unlike the other three `Transform*` helpers.

- `rapaio/data/transform/VarToDoubleTransform.java:92,109,129,146,163,180` and `rapaio/data/transform/VarToIntTransform.java:62,79,99,116,133,150` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `fun` is a `Function<Var,Var>` implemented by static nested classes (`DefaultByType`, `TransformSpot`, ...) that do not implement `Serializable`, and they in turn wrap `Function<VSpot,Double>`/`Function<String,Double>` lambdas. `VarTransform extends Serializable` and both classes declare `serialVersionUID`, yet `byDefault()` etc. can never be serialized (`NotSerializableException`).

#### rapaio/data/unique

- `rapaio/data/unique/UniqueLabel.java:56-57,122-129` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `VarString.getLabel` may return `null` (a `null` entry is a legal missing value per `VarString.isMissing`). `keySet.add(null)` succeeds, and with `sorted == true` `values.sort(new UniqueLabelComparator())` reaches `v1.compareTo(v2)` with a `null` operand -> `NullPointerException`. Trigger: `UniqueLabel.of(VarString.copy("a", null), true)`. Expected: `null` treated like `"?"`; actual: NPE.

#### rapaio/data/stream

- `rapaio/data/stream/VSpotSpliterator.java:34` and `rapaio/data/stream/FSpotSpliterator.java:34` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Characteristics are `SIZED | SUBSIZED | CONCURRENT | NONNULL | IMMUTABLE`. The `Spliterator` Javadoc states a top-level spliterator must not report both `CONCURRENT` and `SIZED`, and `IMMUTABLE`/`CONCURRENT` describe mutually exclusive source policies. The sources (`Var`/`Frame`) are neither immutable (every `VSpot`/`FSpot` setter mutates them) nor safe for concurrent modification, so both flags are false claims; a parallel stream may rely on them and skip synchronization/CME detection.

- `rapaio/data/stream/FSpotSpliterator.java:47-60` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `tryAdvance` advances `row` but `estimateSize()` is *not* overridden (unlike `VSpotSpliterator`); only `getExactSizeIfKnown()` is. The inherited `AbstractSpliterator.estimateSize()` returns the constructor-time `est` and is never decremented by this class's `tryAdvance`, so after partial traversal `estimateSize()` (stale, too large) and `getExactSizeIfKnown()` (exact) disagree, violating the `SIZED` contract that `estimateSize()` be exact. Trigger: create the spliterator, call `tryAdvance` twice, compare the two size methods.

- `rapaio/data/stream/FSpots.java:288-341` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `collectFSpotList`, `collectRowList`, `collectMapping` and therefore `toMappedFrame` use `forEach(list::add)` / `forEach(s -> m.add(s.row()))` on the wrapped stream. `FSpots.parallel()` is a public operation on this class; after it, `forEach` runs the non-thread-safe `ArrayList`/`Mapping.add` from multiple threads (lost updates / `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`) and, even when it happens to succeed, does not preserve encounter order, so `toMappedFrame()` returns rows in arbitrary order. Trigger: `df.stream().parallel().filter(...).toMappedFrame()`. `VSpots.toMappedVar` (line 345) is correct because it uses `mapToInt(...).toArray()`, which is ordered and safe; `FSpots` should do the same (or use `forEachOrdered`).

- `rapaio/data/stream/VSpots.java:59,64` and `rapaio/data/stream/FSpots.java:59,65` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Both classes `implement Serializable` and declare `serialVersionUID`, but their `Stream<...> stream` field is a non-serializable, non-transient JDK pipeline object; serializing either instance throws `NotSerializableException`.

#### rapaio/data/group/function

- `rapaio/data/group/function/GroupFunMax.java:72-82` and `rapaio/data/group/function/GroupFunMin.java:73-83` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The `LONG` branch uses `Long.MIN_VALUE` as the "unset" sentinel, but `VarLong.MISSING_VALUE` is `Long.MAX_VALUE`. (a) If every row in a group is missing, `max3`/`min3` stays `Long.MIN_VALUE` and `aggregate.setLong(row, Long.MIN_VALUE)` stores a *real* value `-9223372036854775808` instead of a missing value. (b) In `GroupFunMin`, a legitimate data value `Long.MIN_VALUE` is indistinguishable from "unset": for group values `[Long.MIN_VALUE, 5]` the loop sets `min3 = MIN_VALUE`, then on `5` the test `min3 == Long.MIN_VALUE` is true and `min3` becomes `5`; result `5`, expected `Long.MIN_VALUE`. Compare the `DOUBLE` branch, which correctly uses `NaN` as the sentinel.

- `rapaio/data/group/function/GroupFunMax.java:47,83-92` and `rapaio/data/group/function/GroupFunMin.java:48,84-93` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The `switch` has no `FLOAT` (or `INSTANT`) case, so a `FLOAT` variable falls into `default`, which compares `df.getLabel(...)` strings lexicographically and writes the winner back with `setLabel` (parsed by `VarFloat`). For float group values `{9.5f, 10.5f}` the labels `"9.5"` and `"10.5"` compare as `"9.5" > "10.5"`, so `max` returns `9.5` and `min` returns `10.5`. Expected numeric comparison as for `DOUBLE`.

- `rapaio/data/group/function/GroupFunMax.java:84-90` and `rapaio/data/group/function/GroupFunMin.java:85-91` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] Unlike the numeric branches, the `default` (nominal/string) branch never skips `df.isMissing(row, varIndex)`, so the missing marker `"?"` takes part in the comparison. Since `'?'` (0x3F) sorts after digits and before letters, a nominal group `{"1", "2", "?"}` yields max `"?"` (should be `"2"`) and a group `{"a", "?"}` yields min `"?"` (should be `"a"`). For a `VarString` holding a `null` entry, `max4.compareTo(null)` additionally throws `NullPointerException`.

#### rapaio/data/sample

- `rapaio/data/sample/Identity.java:51-54` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `equals` is overridden (`obj instanceof Identity`) but `hashCode` is not, so two `Identity` instances are equal yet have different identity hash codes, violating the `equals`/`hashCode` contract (breaks use as `HashMap`/`HashSet` key, and `RowSampler` values are compared in model parameter sets).

- `rapaio/data/sample/RowSampler.java:79-89` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `Sample.getComplementMapping()` allocates `originalRowCount - inSet.size()` slots but iterates `for (i = 0; i < df.rowCount(); i++)`, where `df` is the *sampled* frame, not the original. Whenever the sample has fewer rows than the source the tail of the source is never examined and the unfilled slots stay `0`. Trigger: `RowSampler.subsampler(0.5)` on 10 rows drawing `{0,1,2,3,4}`: complement should be `[5,6,7,8,9]`; actual `[0,0,0,0,0]`. Same for `bootstrap(p)` with `p < 1`. This is the out-of-bag mapping consumed by `CForest.buildWeakPredictor` (CForest.java:403), so OOB error is silently wrong for any non-default sampler (default `bootstrap(1.0)` has `df.rowCount() == originalRowCount` and is unaffected).

#### rapaio/io

- `rapaio/io/ArffPersistence.java:178-183` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `fullTrim`: the second condition is `in.endsWith("'") || in.startsWith("\"")` (copy-paste; should be `endsWith("\"")`). For a double-quoted token `"abc"` the leading quote is removed, then the trailing quote is not (the string no longer starts with `"`), producing `abc"`. Trigger: attribute name or nominal level written as `"abc"` in an ARFF header. (Class is `@Deprecated` but still used by `Datasets.loadSonar`.)

- `rapaio/io/Csv.java:244-247` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `while (!keepRows.get().test(allRowsNum)) { reader.readLine(); allRowsNum += 1; }` never checks for EOF; `BufferedReader.readLine()` returns `null` forever at EOF, so a `keepRows` rule that accepts no index (`IntRule.none()`, `IntRule.less(0)`, or any rule whose first accepted index is astronomically large) makes `read` spin forever instead of returning an empty frame or failing.

- `rapaio/io/Csv.java:303-310` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] When a *later* row has more fields than any previous row, the new `VarSlot` is created with `rows` pre-filled missing values and then `continue` skips adding the current field, so the first observed value of the new column is dropped and recorded as missing. Worse, the slot's `text` mirror is created empty (`VarString.empty()`, line 490) while `var` already has `rows` entries; on the first type upgrade (`addValue`, lines 593-603) `var` is rebuilt from the empty `text`, shrinking to size 0, and `SolidFrame.byVars(rows, vars)` (line 322, `byVars` takes `min` of sizes) silently truncates the *whole frame*. Trigger, header off: rows `"1,2"`, `"3,4,5"`, `"6,7,x"`: V3 should be `[?, 5, x]`; actual: V3 becomes `["x"]` (size 1) and the returned frame has 1 row instead of 3, with no error.

- `rapaio/io/Csv.java:379-385` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `clean` with `quotes=true`: the `!tok.isEmpty()` guard is evaluated before the leading quote is stripped; for a token that is exactly `"` (a line ending in a lone opening quote, e.g. `a,"`), `tok` becomes `""` and `tok.charAt(tok.length() - 1)` throws `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException`. Expected: a `TextParserException` or an empty/missing field.

- `rapaio/io/Csv.java:390-391` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] In the unescape loop the bounds check is `len < tok.length() - 1` (output length) but the lookahead is `tok.charAt(i + 1)` (input index). Once an escape pair has been consumed `len < i`, so at `i == tok.length()-1` with `tok.charAt(i) == '"'` the lookahead reads past the end -> `StringIndexOutOfBoundsException`. Trigger (`quotes=true`): raw field `"""""` (five quotes) -> after outer-quote stripping `"""` -> i=0 consumes a pair (len=1, i=2), i=2: `1 < 2`, `tok.charAt(2)=='"'`, `tok.charAt(3)` throws. Only malformed quoting reaches it, but it should be a parse error, not SIOOBE.

- `rapaio/io/Csv.java:435,448` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `write` formats every non-nominal/non-string column with `new DecimalFormat("0.###...", Format.SYMBOLS)` (`Locale.ROOT`). `DecimalFormat` renders `Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY` as `"∞"` and negative infinity as `"-∞"`, which `Parsers.DEFAULT_VAR_DOUBLE_PARSER` (accepts only `"Inf"`, `"-Inf"`, `Double.parseDouble` forms) cannot read back; under default type inference the whole column is silently promoted to `NOMINAL` on re-read. Expected: write `Inf`/`-Inf` (or `Infinity`) symmetric with the reader.

- `rapaio/io/Csv.java:445-449` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Non-nominal columns are written via `df.getDouble(i, j)`. For `LONG` this loses precision above 2^53 (e.g. `9007199254740993L` is written as `9007199254740992`), and for `INSTANT` it writes epoch milliseconds as a number while the reader's `INSTANT` parser expects `Instant.parse` text, so `write` -> `read` with `varTypes.add(INSTANT, ...)` fails. Expected: use `getLabel` (the type's own formatter) for `LONG`/`INSTANT` as is done for nominal/string.

- `rapaio/io/JavaIO.java:43-57` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `storeToFile` never flushes or closes the `ObjectOutputStream`/`FileOutputStream`; `ObjectOutputStream` buffers up to 1024 bytes internally and `writeObject` does not flush, so for any object serialized into fewer than 1024 bytes *nothing* (not even the stream header) reaches the file, and larger objects are truncated to a multiple of the block size -- the file is unreadable. `restoreFromFile` likewise leaks the `FileInputStream`. Both should use try-with-resources.

#### rapaio/io/atom

- `rapaio/io/atom/BinaryAtomProtocol.java:189-194` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `readBytes` ignores the return value of `inputStream.readNBytes(buffer, 0, len)`. On a truncated stream it returns fewer bytes and the remainder of `buffer` is silently zero, whereas every other `read*` method (via `DataInputStream.readInt/readDouble`) throws `EOFException`. Should be `readFully`. Trigger: read an atom file cut in the middle of a byte array.

#### rapaio/datasets

- `rapaio/datasets/Datasets.java:149` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `row % 10 == -1` is never true for non-negative `row` (Java `%` of a non-negative int is non-negative), so the `"double"` column of `loadRandom` never contains the intended missing values; dead branch indicating a logic error (`== 1` or `== 0` intended, as in the neighbouring lines).

- `rapaio/datasets/Datasets.java:151` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `VarLong.from(n, row -> row % 17 == 0 ? Long.MIN_VALUE : ...)` uses `Long.MIN_VALUE` as the missing marker, but `VarLong.MISSING_VALUE == Long.MAX_VALUE`; the `"long"` column therefore contains the real value `-9223372036854775808` every 17th row and no missing values, contrary to the evident intent (all other columns inject their type's missing marker).

- `rapaio/datasets/MNISTDatasets.java:60-99` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Both `DataInputStream`s are closed only on the success path; any exception (bad magic number at lines 68/78, count mismatch at line 82, `EOFException`, or an NPE when `Datasets.resourceAsStream` returns `null`) leaks two open `GZIPInputStream`s. Should be try-with-resources.

- `rapaio/datasets/TabularDataset.java:125-130` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `BatchIndexIterator.next()` does not throw `NoSuchElementException` when exhausted; calling `next()` after `hasNext()` is false computes `Arrays.copyOfRange(index, pos*batchSize, ...)` with `from > index.length` and throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` (from `copyOfRange`), violating the `Iterator` contract. `BatchIterator.next()` inherits this.

- `rapaio/datasets/TabularDataset.java:43-49` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Arrays with different first dimensions are accepted; `len` is silently set to the minimum and the extra rows of longer arrays are ignored by every batch and by `trainTestSplit`. Input: `new TabularDataset(tm, x /*100 rows*/, y /*99 rows*/)`. Expected: `IllegalArgumentException` (a features/labels length mismatch is always a caller error); actual: silent truncation to 99.

#### rapaio/ts

- `rapaio/ts/Acf.java:43-44` vs `rapaio/ts/Pacf.java:40-41` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Acf.from(ts, maxLag)` builds `VarInt.seq(0, maxLag)`, i.e. `maxLag` *values* 0..maxLag-1, so the lag named `maxLag` is excluded; `Pacf.from(ts, maxLag)` builds `seq(1, maxLag)` = 1..maxLag, which *includes* it. The two public entry points interpret the same parameter name differently (existing tests call `Acf.from(ts, ts.size())` and `Pacf.from(ts, ts.size() - 1)` to compensate). One of the two is misnamed/mis-specified; a caller asking `Acf.from(ts, 10)` for lag 10 silently gets lags 0..9.

### Summary

Overall the code is straightforward and mostly correct on the happy path; the numerics in the Var* transforms, `Pacf` (Durbin-Levinson matches R's `uni_pacf`), the unique/count logic and the binary atom protocol are sound. The serious problems are concentrated in a few places: (1) `RowSampler.Sample.getComplementMapping` iterates the sampled frame instead of the original, so out-of-bag rows are wrong for every non-default sampler in `CForest`; (2) `GroupFunMax`/`GroupFunMin` use the wrong missing sentinel for `LONG`, compare `FLOAT` values as strings and let `"?"` win nominal comparisons; (3) `RenameVars` mutates the input frame's variables and leaves `SolidFrame`'s name index stale; (4) `Csv` has a late-appearing-column path that silently truncates the whole frame, an infinite loop on an all-rejecting `keepRows`, two `StringIndexOutOfBounds` paths in quote handling, and a writer that emits `∞`, loses `LONG` precision and writes `INSTANT` as millis so round-trips fail; (5) `JavaIO.storeToFile` never flushes, producing empty/truncated files; (6) `VarQuantileTransform` keys predicates by 3-decimal display labels and can drop rows. A cross-cutting contract issue is that ~10 `Transform`/`VarTransform`/`Spots` classes declare `Serializable` (with `serialVersionUID`) but hold non-serializable lambdas or `Stream` objects. Counts: bug 19, likely-bug 6, contract 12 (37 findings in 28 bullets; HIGH 32, MEDIUM 5, LOW 0).

### Findings

#### rapaio/core/SamplingTools.java
- `rapaio/core/SamplingTools.java:552-580` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `trainTestSplit` does not validate `p`. `p = 1.5` on a 10-row frame gives `trainSize = 15`, `testSize = -5` and `Ints.copyOf(rows, 15, -5)` throws `NegativeArraySizeException` (no message about `p`); `p = NaN` gives `(int) NaN == 0`, i.e. an empty train set and the whole frame as test set, silently. Expected: `IllegalArgumentException` for `p` outside `[0, 1]` or NaN, as `normalized(...)` does for the other samplers.

Otherwise clean: `sampleWR`, `sampleWOR` (reservoir), Vose alias table (`makeAliasWR`), Efraimidis-Spirakis with exponential jumps, `randomSampleSlices` and the stratified split were traced and are correct; all Random-seeded overloads consume only the given `Random`.

#### rapaio/core/correlation/
- `AbstractCorrelation.java`, `CorrPearson.java`, `CorrSpearman.java`, `Correlation.java` -- clean. Pearson uses the `n-1` estimator consistently (sd with `n-1` divisor, sum divided by `(n-1)`); Spearman uses average ranks for ties (`1 + (start+end)/2`) and complete-case filtering across all variables; single/empty complete sets yield NaN.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Distribution.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Distribution.java:104-113` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] the default `sampleNext(Random)` is `quantile(random.nextDouble())`; `nextDouble()` returns `0.0` with probability 2^-53, and `quantile(0)` is `minValue()`, which is `-Infinity` for `Normal`/`StudentT` and `0` for the others. A sample from `Normal.std()` can therefore contain `-Infinity` (it is the only sample value with non-zero probability that is not a draw from the distribution). Expected: draw from `(0, 1)`, e.g. `1 - nextDouble()`, or reject `0`.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Bernoulli.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Bernoulli.java:119-121` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `entropy()` returns NaN for `p == 0` and `p == 1`: `-0 * log(0) = -0 * -Inf = NaN`. Expected `0` (the convention `0 log 0 = 0`; scipy `bernoulli(0).entropy() == 0`).
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Bernoulli.java:44-49` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] the constructor accepts `p = NaN` (`NaN < 0 || NaN > 1` is false); every method then returns NaN or an arbitrary branch (`pdf(0) == NaN`, `quantile(0.5) == 1`, `mode() == 1`). Expected `IllegalArgumentException`, as `Binomial` does.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Bernoulli.java:76-79` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile(p)` does not validate `p`; `quantile(-1) == 0`, `quantile(2) == 1`, `quantile(NaN) == 1`. The `Distribution.quantile` Javadoc requires `IllegalArgumentException` for `p` outside `[0, 1]` or NaN.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Bernoulli.java:127-135` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: LOW] `sampleNext` uses `nextDouble() <= prob`, so `P(X = 1) = prob + 2^-53` and `Bernoulli.of(0)` can return `1` (when `nextDouble()` is exactly `0.0`). `<` gives exactly `prob`.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Binomial.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Binomial.java:99-108` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile` returns NaN for `p` outside `[0, 1]`, NaN or infinite, instead of throwing `IllegalArgumentException` as the `Distribution.quantile` contract states and as all the `QuantileSearch.checkProbability` based distributions do.
- Formulas otherwise verified: `cdf(k) = I_{1-p}(n-k, k+1)`, R's `qbinom` search (left/right `doSearch`) matches the reference, `mode`, `skewness`, `kurtosis`; `entropy` is documented as the `O(1/n)` normal approximation (it returns `-Infinity` for `p in {0,1}` where the true value is `0`, consistent with the documented approximation).

#### rapaio/core/distributions/ChiSquare.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/ChiSquare.java:54-63` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] the constructor accepts `df = NaN` (`NaN < 1` is false); `ChiSquare.of(Double.NaN)` is built and every method returns NaN. The message also says "greater than zero" while the check rejects `df in (0, 1)`, which is a valid chi-square (scipy/R accept it). Expected: reject NaN (and either accept `0 < df < 1` or word the message accordingly).
- `rapaio/core/distributions/ChiSquare.java:88-93` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` returns NaN instead of `1`: `incGamma(a, Inf)` enters `1 - incGammaC(a, Inf)`, where `ax = a*log(Inf) - Inf - lnGamma(a) = Inf - Inf = NaN`, the continued fraction runs with `ans = Inf/Inf = NaN` and returns NaN. Any caller computing `1 - cdf(statistic)` with an infinite statistic gets a NaN p-value (see `ChiSqGoodnessOfFit` below). Expected: guard `x == +Inf -> 1` (and `x == -Inf` already gives 0).
- pdf/cdf/quantile/moments/entropy formulas and the Monahan ratio-of-uniforms sampler match the reference (Colt `ChiSquare`).

#### rapaio/core/distributions/DUniform.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/DUniform.java:130-133` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `mode()` returns NaN; `Distribution.mode()` promises "if multiple modes are defined, then one of them is returned". Every support point is a mode, so `a` (or any point) should be returned; NaN is what the other distributions use for "undefined".
- `rapaio/core/distributions/DUniform.java:147-149` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `kurtosis()` for `a == b` (`n == 1`) is `-6*2/(5*0) = -Infinity`; the distribution is a point mass with `var == 0`, for which excess kurtosis is undefined (NaN) -- `+Infinity` is documented as "fourth moment diverges", which is not the case here.
- pdf/cdf/quantile (`a + ceil(p n) - 1`), mean, var `(n^2-1)/12`, entropy `log n` are correct; `n` is kept in double so `b - a + 1` cannot overflow.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Fisher.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Fisher.java:86-90` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `mode()` uses `(df1 - 2) * df2 / (df1 * (df2 - 2))`; the reference is `((d1 - 2) / d1) * (d2 / (d2 + 2))` (Wikipedia, scipy `f.stats`... derived from `d/dx pdf = 0`). `Fisher.of(4, 4).mode()` returns `1.0`, correct value is `1/3`; for `df2 == 2` the code divides by zero (`Infinity`) where the correct mode is finite (`(d1-2)/(2 d1)`).
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Fisher.java:44-46` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] no parameter validation: `Fisher.of(0, 0)`, `Fisher.of(-1, 5)`, `Fisher.of(NaN, NaN)` are all accepted and produce NaN/garbage (`pdf(0)` for `df1 = -1` returns `+Infinity`, `cdf` calls `betaIncReg` with negative shape). Expected `IllegalArgumentException` for `df1 <= 0`, `df2 <= 0` or NaN (record compact constructor, as `Exponential`/`Gamma` do).
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Fisher.java:69-72` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile(p)` does not validate `p`; `quantile(NaN)` and `quantile(2)` are passed straight to `invBetaIncReg` instead of throwing as the interface requires.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Fisher.java:64-67` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` evaluates `df1*x/(df1*x + df2) = Inf/Inf = NaN` and returns NaN instead of `1`.
- var, skewness, kurtosis and entropy (`ln B(a,b) + (a+b) psi(a+b) - (a-1) psi(a) - (b+1) psi(b) + ln(d2/d1)`, derived from the scaled beta prime) verified correct.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Gamma.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Gamma.java:90-96` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] the compact constructor accepts `alpha = NaN` or `beta = NaN` (`NaN <= 0` is false) and infinite values; `Gamma.of(NaN, 1)` is constructed and every method returns NaN. Expected `IllegalArgumentException` (use `!(alpha > 0 && Double.isFinite(alpha))`, as `Exponential` does).
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Gamma.java:131-136` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` returns NaN instead of `1`, same `incGamma(a, Inf)` path as `ChiSquare.cdf`.
- pdf, mode, moments, entropy (`alpha + ln beta + lnGamma(alpha) + (1-alpha) psi(alpha)`) and the Ahrens-Dieter sampler (compared line by line with Colt `Gamma.nextDouble`; `beta` correctly used as scale) are correct.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Hypergeometric.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Hypergeometric.java:239-251` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile(NaN)` is not rejected (`NaN < 0 || NaN > 1` is false); the loop never satisfies `cdf >= NaN` and silently returns `maxValue()`. The interface requires `IllegalArgumentException`.
- pdf (factor-cancelling product), cdf, mean, mode `floor((k+1)(m+1)/(N+2))`, var, skewness and the kurtosis formula from the reference were checked and are correct; all products are in double.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/LogNormal.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/LogNormal.java:81-86` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` returns `0` instead of `1`: `erf(Inf)` is `2*normalCdf(Inf) - 1` and `MathTools.normalCdf` returns `0` for both infinities, so `(1 + (-1))/2 = 0`.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/LogNormal.java:88-99` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile(NaN)` passes the range check and returns NaN from `inverf(NaN)` instead of throwing.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/LogNormal.java:53-60` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `mu = NaN` is accepted (only `sigma` is checked); `LogNormal.of(NaN, 1)` yields NaN everywhere.
- pdf, moments (mean, mode, median, var, skewness `(e^{s^2}+2) sqrt(e^{s^2}-1)`, excess kurtosis `e^{4s^2}+2e^{3s^2}+3e^{2s^2}-6`) and entropy are correct.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Normal.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Normal.java:74-81` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` returns `0` instead of `1` (`MathTools.normalCdf` returns `0` for any infinite `x`, with the comment "for infinity we return 0"). Concretely `Normal.std().cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY) == 0.0`; `KSTestOneSample` on a sample containing `+Infinity` and any `pValue = 1 - cdf(+Inf)` caller are affected.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Normal.java:53-60` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `sd == 0` and `sd = NaN`/`mu = NaN` are accepted (`sd < 0` only). With `sd == 0`: `var == 0`, `pdf(x) = 1/sqrt(0) * exp(-(x-mu)^2/0)` is `Inf * 0 = NaN` for `x != mu` and `Inf * exp(NaN) = NaN` for `x == mu`, so `Normal.of(0, 0).pdf(x)` is NaN for every `x`; `cdf(mu)` is `cdfMarsaglia(0/0) = NaN`. Expected: reject `!(sd > 0)` (scipy rejects `scale <= 0`), or at least reject NaN.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Normal.java:83-86` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile(NaN)` is not rejected by `MathTools.normalQuantile` (`NaN < 0 || NaN > 1` false) and returns NaN instead of throwing as the interface requires.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/Poisson.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Poisson.java:52-56` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] the compact constructor accepts `lambda = NaN` and `lambda = +Infinity` (`NaN <= 0` is false); `Poisson.of(NaN)` is constructed and returns NaN from every method, `Poisson.of(Inf).entropy()` loops `(int) Inf == Integer.MAX_VALUE` times.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/Poisson.java:81-86` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `cdf(Double.POSITIVE_INFINITY)` returns NaN for `lambda > 1`: `incGammaC(Inf, lambda)` goes to `1 - incGamma(Inf, lambda)`, where `ax = Inf*log(lambda) - lambda - lnGamma(Inf)` is `Inf - Inf = NaN`, the series returns `ans * NaN / Inf = NaN`. Expected `1`. (For `lambda < 1` the `ax < -MAXLOG` shortcut happens to give the right answer.)
- pdf, cdf `Q(floor(x)+1, lambda)`, quantile search, moments, and the entropy identity `lambda(1 - log lambda) + E[log k!]` are correct.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/StudentT.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/StudentT.java:66-73` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `sigma` and `mu` are not validated: `StudentT.of(3, 0, -1)` and `StudentT.of(3, 0, 0)` are accepted. With `sigma = -1`, `pdf` computes `log(sigma) = NaN`; with `sigma = 0`, `cdf(t)` has `pow((t-mu)/0, 2) = Inf`, `x = 0`, so `cdf == 0` for `t < mu` and `1` for `t > mu` while `pdf` is NaN. Expected `IllegalArgumentException` for `!(sigma > 0)` and NaN `mu`.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/StudentT.java:131-134` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `mean()` returns `mu` for every `df`, but the mean does not exist for `df <= 1` (Cauchy for `df == 1`); `var()`/`skewness()`/`kurtosis()` in the same class return NaN for their undefined ranges and scipy `t(1).mean()` is `nan`. Expected NaN for `df <= 1`.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/StudentT.java:95-109` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `quantile(NaN)` passes `p < 0 || p > 1`, skips the median shortcut, calls `invBetaIncReg(NaN, ...)` and returns `mu - x` instead of throwing.
- pdf, cdf (`I_{df/(df+t^2)}(df/2, 1/2)/2`), quantile, var, kurtosis and entropy are correct.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/ (remaining)
- `Exponential.java`, `Uniform.java`, `QuantileSearch.java` -- clean. Every closed form (pdf, cdf, quantile, mean, mode, median, var, skewness 2 / 0, excess kurtosis 6 / -6/5, entropy `1 - ln lambda` / `ln(b-a)`) checked; parameter validation rejects NaN and infinities; `QuantileSearch` terminates on bracket width and returns `+Inf` when the cdf never reaches `p`.

#### rapaio/core/distributions/empirical/KDE.java
- `rapaio/core/distributions/empirical/KDE.java:87-91` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] the bandwidth is never validated: `KDE.of(values, 0)` or a negative/NaN bandwidth is accepted and `pdf` returns NaN/Infinity (`sum / (n * 0)`); `KDE.of(values)` on a single complete value gets `silvermanBandwidth == NaN` (sd of one value is NaN, `sd == 0` is false) and `pdf` is NaN everywhere. Expected `IllegalArgumentException` for `!(bandwidth > 0)`.
- `rapaio/core/distributions/empirical/KDE.java:93-108` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `pdf` sums sample values in `[from, to)` where `to` is the index returned by `binarySearch(values, kernel.maxValue(x, h))`; when a sample value equals `x + h` exactly it is *found* and excluded, whereas a value equal to `x - h` is included. For every kernel that is non-zero at `|u| == 1` this is asymmetric: `KDE.of(VarDouble.wrap(0, 2), new KFuncUniform(), 1).pdf(1)` sums only `values[0]` and returns `0.5/(2*1) = 0.25` instead of `(0.5+0.5)/2 = 0.5`. The same `binarySearch` on duplicates returns an arbitrary element of the run, so several values equal to `x - h` can be skipped too. Expected: `to` = first index with `values[i] > maxValue` (upper bound), `from` = first index with `values[i] >= minValue` (lower bound).
- `rapaio/core/distributions/empirical/KDE.java:130-138` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `silvermanBandwidth` uses `Variance.of(vector)` (which ignores missing values) but counts `vector.stream().complete().count()` -- consistent -- yet for `sd == NaN` (fewer than 2 complete values) the `sd == 0` fallback does not fire and the bandwidth is NaN (see first item). Documented as Silverman's rule; the constant `1.06 sigma n^{-1/5}` is the normal reference rule, which is acceptable.
- `KFunc*.java` -- clean; all kernel constants (`15/16`, `pi/4`, `3/4`, `70/81`, `35/32`, `1/2`) integrate to one on `[-1, 1]`. Note `KFuncGaussian` truncates at `+-4h` (mass `6.3e-5` outside), a documented-nowhere approximation but not a formula error.

#### rapaio/core/param/
- `rapaio/core/param/ListParam.java:115-118` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `clear()` empties the list, whereas `ValueParam.clear()`, `MultiParam.clear()` and `MultiListParam.clear()` all restore the default value. After `listParam.clear()` on a parameter with a non-empty default, `hasDefaultValue()` is `false` and `getStringParameterValues(true)` prints it as non-default. Expected: `replace(defaultValues)`.
- `rapaio/core/param/ListParam.java:46-53` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] the constructor calls `set(defaultValues)`, which does `values.addAll(newValues)`; a `null` default (which `hasDefaultValue()` explicitly supports at line 90) throws `NullPointerException` in the constructor, so that branch of `hasDefaultValue` is unreachable.
- `rapaio/core/param/ParamSet.java:55-57` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `ParamSet(T source)` calls `copyParameterValues(source)` while `parameterMap` is still empty (subclass `Param` fields register themselves only after the super constructor returns), so the constructor copies nothing, silently. All in-tree callers use `new X().copyParameterValues(this)` instead; the constructor is a trap.
- `rapaio/core/param/MultiListParam.java:65-83` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `set`/`add` store the caller's `List<T>` instances by reference and `get(K)` returns them; `get()` wraps only the outer map in `unmodifiableMap`, so `param.get().get(key).add(x)` (for a mutable list) mutates the parameter without the validator, contrary to the `get()` Javadoc "use set/add/clear so that the validator is applied". The default `copyFrom` then shares the same lists between two parameter sets.
- `MultiParam.java`, `Param.java`, `ParametricEquals.java`, `ValueParam.java` -- clean.

#### rapaio/core/stat/
- `rapaio/core/stat/GeometricMean.java:30-34,86-90` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] the class Javadoc says the mean "is also undefined when all the available values are negative or missing" and that `isDefined()` reports it, but `isDefined()` only tests `negativeCount == 0`: for an all-missing (or empty) variable `value()` is NaN while `isDefined()` returns `true`. (The Javadoc sentence "isDefined() will return true" for negative values is also inverted relative to the code.)
- `Covariance.java`, `Kurtosis.java` (g2, G2 `((n+1)g2+6)(n-1)/((n-2)(n-3))`, b2 `(g2+3)(1-1/n)^2-3` match e1071 types 1-3), `Maximum.java`, `Mean.java` (compensated), `Minimum.java`, `OnlineStat.java` (Welford/Cook update and pairwise combine verified term by term), `Quantiles.java` (R-7 and R-8 including the `p = 0/1` ends), `Skewness.java` (g1, G1, b1 match e1071), `Sum.java` (Neumaier), `Variance.java`, `WeightedMean.java`, `WeightedOnlineStat.java` (West's algorithm and merge) -- clean.

#### rapaio/core/tests/ADTestGoodness.java
- `rapaio/core/tests/ADTestGoodness.java:175-187` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] sign error in the Marsaglia & Marsaglia (2004) `errfix` correction: the code has `z = -0.01265 + 0.1757 / n`, the reference (and R `ADGofTest::ad.test.pvalue`) is `c = .01265 + .1757/n`. With `n = 20` the threshold should be `0.0214`; the code uses `-0.0038`, so the `x < z` branch is dead (x is a cdf value >= 0) and the interpolation `v = (x - z)/(0.8 - z)` is computed against the wrong anchor. `pValue()` (case 0) is therefore off by the size of the small-sample correction for all `x <= 0.8`; the effect is O(1/n) but the value never matches R/ADGofTest exactly.
- The statistic `A^2 = -n - (1/n) sum (2i-1)[ln F(y_i) + ln(1 - F(y_{n+1-i}))]`, the case-3 adjustment `A^2 (1 + 0.75/n + 2.25/n^2)` and the D'Agostino-Stephens Table 4.9 p-value formula match `nortest::ad.test`.

#### rapaio/core/tests/ChiSqGoodnessOfFit.java
- `rapaio/core/tests/ChiSqGoodnessOfFit.java:104-115` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] dead branch: the second `if (Math.abs(e) < 1e-50 && Math.abs(o - e) < 1e50) continue;` can never execute because the first `if (Math.abs(e) < 1e-50) { sum += Inf; break; }` already exits for every `e == 0`. The apparent intent (skip a cell with `e == 0 && o == 0`) is not implemented; instead any zero expected probability makes `chiValue = +Infinity` and, because `ChiSquare.cdf(+Inf)` is NaN (see ChiSquare above), `pValue()` is NaN. R gives NaN statistic for `0/0` cells and a finite statistic when `o > 0, e == 0` is impossible because it rejects `p` with zeros only via "probabilities must sum to 1"... in any case a NaN p-value from a valid `+Inf` statistic is wrong; expected `pValue == 0` for an infinite statistic.
- `rapaio/core/tests/ChiSqGoodnessOfFit.java:85-100` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] the expected probabilities `p` are not checked to sum to 1 (R `chisq.test` errors "probabilities must sum to 1"); `p = {0.5, 0.5, 0.5}` on three observed counts silently yields expected counts summing to `1.5 * total` and a meaningless statistic.

#### rapaio/core/tests/KSTestTwoSamples.java
- `rapaio/core/tests/KSTestTwoSamples.java:51-62` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] the merged walk advances at most one element of each sample per iteration and evaluates `|fn1 - fn2|` in the middle of a tie run, so `D` is wrong when a sample contains repeated values. `v1 = {1, 1}`, `v2 = {1}`: after one iteration `fn1 = 1/2`, `fn2 = 1`, `D = 0.5`, then the loop exits; the true `D = sup|F1 - F2| = 0` (both ECDFs are 0 below 1 and 1 at 1). NR3 `kstwo` fixes this with a `do ... while (data[j] == d)` loop per side; R's `ks.test` computes the ECDF difference at the distinct values.
- `rapaio/core/tests/KSTestTwoSamples.java:97-104` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `ciHigh()`/`ciLow()` return `0`, while `HTest.ciHigh` states "Tests without an interval return NaN" and `KSTestOneSample` returns NaN. A caller printing or checking the interval gets a bogus `[0, 0]`.

#### rapaio/core/tests/TTestTwoPaired.java
- `rapaio/core/tests/TTestTwoPaired.java:83-88` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] paired samples of different sizes are silently truncated to `min(x.size(), y.size())` instead of being rejected; `x` of 10 rows paired with `y` of 8 rows tests the first 8 pairs with no error. Expected `IllegalArgumentException` (R: "'x' and 'y' must have the same length").
- `rapaio/core/tests/TTestTwoPaired.java:107-108` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] with exactly one complete pair `df == 0` and `StudentT.of(0)` throws `IllegalArgumentException("degrees of freedom in student t distribution must have a value greater than 0.")` out of the test constructor; the `complete.size() < 1` guard handles only the empty case. Same for `TTestOneSample.java:170-176` with a one-element sample. Expected either a NaN result (as for size 0) or a message that names the sample size.

#### rapaio/core/tests/ZTestTwoPaired.java
- `rapaio/core/tests/ZTestTwoPaired.java:96-100` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] same silent truncation to `min(x.size(), y.size())` for unequal paired samples as `TTestTwoPaired`.

#### rapaio/core/tests/ (remaining)
- `ChiSqConditionalIndependence.java`, `ChiSqIndependence.java` (expected counts, Yates `diff -= min(0.5, diff)`, `df = (r-1)(c-1)`; `VarNominal.levels()` does not include the missing label, so the degrees of freedom are right), `HTest.java`, `KSTestOneSample.java` (two-sided `D` and NR `probks`), `TTestOneSample.java` (see the `n == 1` note above), `TTestTwoSamples.java` (pooled and Welch-Satterthwaite `df`), `ZTestOneSample.java`, `ZTestTwoSamples.java` -- statistics, p-values and confidence intervals verified correct.

#### rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java
- `rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java:55,157-159,388-403` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `DensityTable implements Serializable` but its non-transient fields cannot be serialized: `values` is a `DArray<Double>` (`rapaio.darray.DArray` does not implement `Serializable`), and `concreteRowAverageEntropy`/`concreteRowIntrinsicInfo`/`concreteTotalColEntropy` are instances of the nested `record DensityTableFunction`, which is not `Serializable` either and holds a non-serializable lambda. `new ObjectOutputStream(...).writeObject(DensityTable.empty(true, List.of("a"), List.of("b")))` throws `NotSerializableException`. Same for `DensityVector` only if its `Index` is not serializable (it is: `Index extends Serializable`), so `DensityVector` is fine.
- `rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java:151-153` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `fromBinaryLevelWeights(false, ...)` skips rows where `rowVar` is missing but not rows where `colVar` is missing: `colIndex.getIndex(colVar, i)` returns `-1` for a label not in the index (`IndexLabel.getIndex` uses `getOrDefault(..., -1)`) and `dt.inc(rowId, -1, w)` is called with a negative column. `fromLabels` guards this with `containsValue`; the same guard is needed here.
- `rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java:320-323` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `giniGain` returns `1` for an empty table (`total <= 0`). Gini gain is bounded by the parent Gini impurity `1 - sum p^2 < 1`, so an empty table scores higher than any real split; a tree search comparing candidate splits by `splitByRowGiniGain()` would prefer a split whose table is empty. Expected `0` (no information).
- `rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java:255-264` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `normalizeOnRows()`/`normalizeOnCols()` divide by row/column totals without the `total > 0` guard that `normalizeOverall()` has; a row (or column) whose total is 0 becomes all NaN (`0/0`) rather than staying 0.

#### rapaio/core/tools/DensityVector.java
- `rapaio/core/tools/DensityVector.java:218-233` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `normalize()`/`normalize(pow)` rescale `values` in place through `DArrays.stride(values).normalize_(pow)` (which wraps the same array and calls `div_`), but the cached `total` is not updated, so `sum()`, `sumExcept(...)` return the pre-normalization total afterwards. `dv` with counts `{1, 3}`: after `normalize()`, `get(0) == 0.25`, `get(1) == 0.75`, but `sum() == 4` (expected `1`) and `sumExcept(0) == 3.25` (expected `0.75`). The existing test only checks `get(i)`.
- `rapaio/core/tools/DensityVector.java:200-208` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `findBestIndex()` Javadoc says "If there are multiple maximal values, one at random is chosen"; `argmax()` is deterministic (first maximum). Harmless unless a caller relies on tie randomization, but the documented contract is not honoured.

#### rapaio/core/tools/Grid2D.java
- `rapaio/core/tools/Grid2D.java:45,124` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Grid2D implements Serializable` with a non-transient `DArray<Double> values` field; `DArray` is not `Serializable`, so serialization throws `NotSerializableException`.
- `rapaio/core/tools/Grid2D.java:203-216` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `seq(int intervals, double ext)` computes `start = min*(1-ext)`, `end = max*(1+ext)`; this extends the range only when `min >= 0 <= max`. For `min = -10, max = 10, ext = 0.1` it returns a sequence from `-9` to `11` (the lower end *shrinks*), and the Javadoc claim "equivalent with one percent extension of the whole interval" is false (it is a percentage of the endpoint values, not of the interval). The step `(max - min)/intervals` is also not adjusted, so the number of intervals differs from `intervals` whenever `ext != 0`. Expected `start = min - ext*(max-min)`, `end = max + ext*(max-min)`, step `(end-start)/intervals`.

#### rapaio/core/tools/ (remaining)
- `DistanceMatrix.java` (lower-triangular index `i(i+1)/2 + j`, symmetric get/set) and `HistogramTable.java` (Freedman-Diaconis with Sturges fallback, clamped bin index) -- clean.

### Summary

Overall the package is in good shape: the sampling algorithms, the estimators in `core/stat`, the t/z/chi-square/KS-one-sample tests and almost every closed-form pdf/cdf/quantile/moment/entropy are correct against R/scipy semantics. The defects cluster in three places: (1) boundary and parameter handling in `core/distributions` -- `Normal`/`LogNormal.cdf(+Inf)` return 0, `ChiSquare`/`Gamma`/`Poisson`/`Fisher.cdf(+Inf)` return NaN, several constructors accept NaN (Bernoulli, ChiSquare, Gamma, Poisson, Normal incl. `sd == 0`, StudentT `sigma`), `Fisher` has no validation at all and a wrong `mode()` formula, `Bernoulli.entropy` is NaN at the ends, and most non-`QuantileSearch` distributions return NaN instead of throwing on an invalid `p`; (2) three genuine statistical errors -- the sign of the Marsaglia small-sample correction in `ADTestGoodness`, the tie handling in `KSTestTwoSamples.D`, and `DensityVector.normalize` leaving `sum()` stale; (3) contract breaches -- `DensityTable` and `Grid2D` are declared `Serializable` but cannot be serialized, `KSTestTwoSamples` returns `0` instead of NaN for the (absent) confidence interval, `ListParam.clear()` differs from every other `Param`, `ParamSet(T)` copies nothing.

Counts: 21 bug, 17 likely-bug, 15 contract (53 findings); by confidence 42 HIGH, 10 MEDIUM, 1 LOW. Most serious: `Normal.cdf(+Inf) == 0`, `Fisher.mode()`, `ADTestGoodness` sign error, `KSTestTwoSamples` ties, `DensityVector.normalize` stale total, missing NaN/`sd == 0` validation in `Normal`/`Gamma`/`Poisson`/`Fisher`/`StudentT`.

SolverC, SolverNU, SvcKernelMatrix, OneClassKernelMatrix, SvrKernelMatrix and the numeric routines in Svm.java were compared line by line and match the reference; the only libsvm-port defects found are in Cache (below) and in the rapaio glue (ProblemInfo/SvmClassifier).

### Findings

### svm/

- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/BinarySMO.java:236-247` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] With `prob=true` and a linear kernel (the DEFAULT kernel `PolyKernel(1)` has `isLinear()==true`), `compactVectors` takes the linear branch and never assigns `_vectors`, then `coreFit` calls `_vectors.dim(0)` -> NullPointerException. Trigger: `BinarySMO.newModel().prob.set(true).fit(iris2class, "class")`. Expected: fit succeeds and a logistic calibration is built; actual: NPE during fit.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/BinarySMO.java:584-590` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Linear-kernel `predictScore` multiplies `_sparseWeights[i]` with `df.getDouble(row, i)` instead of `df.getDouble(row, _sparseIndices[i])`. `compactVectors` (lines 517-533) drops weights with `|w| < 1e-200` and compacts the rest, so as soon as one weight is dropped (e.g. an all-zero input column, or a column never involved in a step) every subsequent weight is applied to the wrong feature. `toSummary` (line 650) uses `_sparseIndices` correctly, so printing and prediction disagree. Expected: use `_sparseIndices[i]`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/BinarySMO.java:270-276,606-614` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] One-vs-all mode (`firstLabel` set, `secondLabel` = "?") sets `label2 = "~" + firstLabel`, which is not a level of the target, then `corePredict` does `cr.firstDensity().setDouble(i, label2, ...)`; `SolidFrame.varIndex` returns -1 for an unknown name and `vars[-1]` throws ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException. `cr.firstClasses().setLabel(i, label2)` would additionally inject a new level "~x" into the nominal prediction. Trigger: `firstLabel.set("setosa")` on iris, then `predict`. Expected: one-vs-all prediction; actual: exception on the first row.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/BinarySMO.java:118,417-449` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `eps_delta = 1e-200` is used where Weka's SMO uses `m_Del = 1e-3` ("To prevent precision problems": snapping a1/a2 to 0 or C) and `m_eps` (progress test at line 431). With 1e-200 the snapping never fires, so alphas essentially never become exactly 0 or C, sets I1..I4 are almost never populated after the first step, and `supportVectors` (alpha > 0) retains every example ever touched. Result: bloated models with many near-zero "support vectors" and bLow/bUp maintained only through I0. Convergence still holds but the port does not implement the algorithm it claims to.

- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/SvmClassifier.java:154-173` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Non-probability branch: `values` has length `k*(k-1)/2` but the loop `for (j = 0; j < k; j++) ... values[j]` reads `k` elements. For a two-level target k=2, `values.length==1` and `values[1]` throws ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException. Trigger: any binary target, `probability=false` (default), `predict(df)` with distributions (default). Expected: prediction with pairwise decision values; actual: AIOOBE on the first row. (The loop is also redundant with the `dist` loop that follows and overwrites it.)
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/SvmClassifier.java:158-163,175-189` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Probability/decision values returned by libsvm are ordered by `svm_model.label[]` (order of FIRST OCCURRENCE of each class in the training rows), but the code writes `prob[j]` / `dist[j]` into density column `j` (target level index) and assumes pair `p` corresponds to level pair (j,l) in level order. Whenever the first-occurrence order differs from the dictionary order (e.g. a frame whose rows are shuffled/mapped after the dictionary was built, or a dictionary level absent from the training rows so `nr_class < k`), the densities are assigned to the wrong classes. Trigger: target dictionary [a,b], training rows where "b" appears first -> `label = {1,0}`, so P(b) is written into column "a". Expected: map through `svm_model.label`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/SvmClassifier.java:160,168,184` + `rapaio/ml/model/svm/libsvm/ProblemInfo.java:52` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] For a BINARY target, `y[i] = target.getInt(i)` gives 1 for `true` and 0 for `false`, but `ProblemInfo.levels` is `VarBinary.levels()` = ["true","false"], so index 0 = "true". `levels.get((int) score)` therefore returns "false" for class true and "true" for class false: predictions are inverted for every BINARY target. Capabilities (line 124) advertise BINARY targets. Also INT targets are advertised but `VarInt.levels()` throws IllegalStateException in `prepareFit`/`ProblemInfo.from`, so INT targets cannot be fitted at all.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/SvmClassifier.java:88-99,160` + `rapaio/ml/model/svm/libsvm/ProblemInfo.java:50-79` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] When `levels` is set explicitly, `index`/`levels` use the custom list order, but `y[i]` remains `target.getInt(i)` (the target's own dictionary index), and rows whose label is not in `levels` are still trained (only the unused `map` filters them). At predict `levels.get((int) score)` then maps a target-dictionary index into the custom list: with target dictionary [c,a,b] and `levels=[a,b]`, class "a" (y=1) is reported as "b" and class "b" (y=2) throws IndexOutOfBoundsException. Javadoc ("only those levels will be used for classification") is not honoured.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/SvmClassifier.java:131` (also `SvmRegression.java:118`, `OneClassSvm.java:94`) -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit(Frame df, Var weights)` ignores `weights` entirely although `ClassifierModel.fit(df, weights, ...)` documents them as "instance weights". Weighted and unweighted fits give identical models. (Per-class `wi` is a different thing.)
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/SvmClassifier.java:111` (also `SvmRegression.java:115`, `OneClassSvm.java:91`, `RVMRegression.java:316-317`) -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] The models are `Serializable` (via ClassifierModel/RegressionModel/ClusteringModel) but hold a non-serializable `ModelInfo` field (SVMs) / `MethodImpl` and `List<Feature>` with capturing lambdas (RVM). Serializing a fitted model throws NotSerializableException.

### svm/libsvm/

- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/libsvm/Cache.java:90-109` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Growing a partially cached column uses `h.data.pad(0, more, 1)`. `DArray.pad(axis, pad, inflation)` pads SYMMETRICALLY: the result has length `oldLen + 2*more` and the old values are relocated to positions `[more, more+oldLen)`. libsvm's `realloc` keeps the old values at `[0, oldLen)`. The caller (`SvcKernelMatrix.getQ`/`OneClassKernelMatrix.getQ`) then fills only `[oldLen, len)`, so positions `[0, min(more,oldLen))` are zeros and the rest are shifted stale values: the returned Q column is wrong. Also `h.len()` becomes `oldLen+2*more != len`, corrupting the size accounting (`size -= more`) and later `more` computations. Trigger: shrinking enabled (default) with C-SVC/nu-SVC/one-class: after `doShrinking` reduces `activeSize`, a column first requested with `len = activeSize` and later with `len = l` (e.g. `Q.getQ(i, len)` in the `gradBar` update at SolverC.java:313/325, or `reconstruct_gradient`) takes this path. Expected: the old prefix preserved at `[0, oldLen)`; actual: corrupted kernel column -> wrong gradient -> wrong model. SVR is unaffected (always requests full `l`).
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/libsvm/ProblemInfo.java:151-166` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Class weights: (a) `param.nrWeight = weighting.size()` but only keys present in `index` are copied (`pos` may end below `nrWeight`), leaving trailing entries `weightLabel=0, weight=0.0`; in `Svm.svm_train` (lines 656-668) the class with label 0 (the first target level) then gets `C *= 0.0`, i.e. C = 0 and all its alphas forced to 0. Javadoc of `wi` says unknown labels are ignored. Trigger: `wi = {a:2, b:3, zzz:4}` on target [a,b]. (b) `if (param.nrWeight == 2) { weightLabel[0] = 1; weightLabel[1] = -1; }` blindly overrides the labels; rapaio labels are level indices >= 0, so the weight at position 1 is always dropped ("class label -1 ... not found") and the weight at position 0 is applied to level 1 regardless of which map entry it came from (HashMap order). Trigger: `wi = {a:2.0, b:0.5}` on a two-level target: class "a" (label 0) gets no weight and class "b" gets whichever weight iterates first. Expected: weights applied to their own classes.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/libsvm/ModelInfo.java:56-64` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Constructor loops `for i < modelCount, for j = i+1 < modelCount` (should be over `levels.size()`), never increments `pos`, and `submodels[pos]` keeps being overwritten with a new `SubModel`; `submodels[1..]` stay null. The class is instantiated by all three SVM models but never read, so the effect is latent; still a copy-paste logic error.
- `rapaio/ml/model/svm/libsvm/Svm.java:918-946` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `svm_cross_validation` builds `subprob` without setting `subprob.random`. It is public; calling it for C_SVC/NU_SVC with `param.probability == 1` reaches `svm_train -> svm_binary_svc_probability -> prob.random.nextInt(...)` with `random == null` -> NullPointerException. The only internal caller (`svm_svr_probability`) sets `probability = 0` first, which is why it does not crash today.

### rvm/

- `rapaio/ml/model/rvm/RVMRegression.java:826-836,1000-1015` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Initial alpha for the first basis vector (both FastTipping and FastOnline) is `||phi||^2 / (phi.y/||phi||^2 - 1/beta)`. Tipping & Faul (2003) eq. (26)/step 1 is `alpha = ||phi||^2 / ( (phi.y)^2/||phi||^2 - sigma^2 )` -- the numerator of the projection must be squared, and the "best" vector is the one maximising the squared normalised projection, not the signed one. With the unsquared form alpha is dimensionally wrong and goes NEGATIVE whenever `phi.y/||phi||^2 < 0.1*var(y)` (e.g. any target with mean near 0 or negative, such as y ~ N(0,10)). A negative alpha makes `Sigma^-1 = beta*PhiHat + alpha` possibly non-PD: `FastOnline.computeSigmaAndMu` (line 1023) uses `cholesky()` and throws; FastTipping's `qr().inv()` returns a meaningless Sigma for the first iteration.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rvm/RVMRegression.java:1140` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `delta *= 1 + Math.pow(phiiDotY.getDouble(i), 0.7)`: `Math.pow(negative, 0.7)` is NaN, so every candidate with `phi_i . y < 0` gets `delta = NaN`. In the selection (lines 1141-1145) a NaN never beats a finite `bestDelta`, so such candidates can never be added, re-estimated or removed unless every candidate is NaN (then the LAST candidate is silently chosen). Trigger: any target with negative values, e.g. y centred at 0 -- roughly half the RBF features and the intercept (phi = 1, phi.y = sum(y) < 0) become unselectable. Expected: a real-valued likelihood-change criterion for all candidates.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rvm/RVMRegression.java:604-616,624-676` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] EvidenceApproximation: `oldAlpha = alpha.copy()` is taken before `pruneAlphas()`, which reassigns `alpha`/`indexes` to a shorter, re-indexed vector; `testConvergence(oldAlpha, alpha)` then compares `alpha[i]` with `oldAlpha[i]` for unrelated features. After any pruning step the convergence test compares wrong pairs and can declare convergence spuriously (or miss it). Expected: compare on the surviving indexes (`oldAlpha.sel(0, keep)`).
- `rapaio/ml/model/rvm/RVMRegression.java:177,229` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `RBFProvider.generateFeatures(random, x)` and `KernelProvider.generateFeatures(random, x)` ignore the `random` argument and call `SamplingTools.sampleWOR(n, len)`, which uses `new Random()`. With `p < 1` the selected features differ on every run even with `seed` set, so fits are not reproducible. (`p == 1` samples everything and sorts, hiding the problem.) `RandomRBFProvider` (line 273) similarly uses `noise.sampleNext()` with the distribution's own RNG.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rvm/RVMRegression.java:680` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `initializeAlphaBeta` calls `parent.getRandom()` INSIDE the per-element lambda; `getRandom()` returns `new Random(seed)` on every call, so with a seed every alpha_i gets the identical value `|nextDouble()/10|` of a fresh generator -- not the intended random initialisation. Expected: create one Random and draw from it.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rvm/RVMRegression.java:394-395` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit(Frame df, Var weights)` ignores instance weights (all three methods use unweighted least squares).

### km/

- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMCluster.java:288-347` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `repairEmptyClusters` never repairs anything: `emptyCentroids` is filled from `unique.rowList(i).isEmpty()` over the UNIQUE VALUES of `assignment`, and a unique value by construction has at least one row, so the set is always empty and the loop body never runs. Empty cluster indices are exactly the values NOT in `unique`. (The unreachable body is also wrong: `checkIfEqual(c, i, df, next)` compares against row `next` (the empty cluster index) instead of `selection`, and `found` is set as soon as ONE centroid differs.) Consequence, KMeans: `Mean.of(empty).value()` = NaN centroid; `findClosestCentroid` with `d > dd` never selects a NaN centroid, except that if cluster 0 becomes NaN, `d = NaN` and every row is assigned to cluster 0, all other clusters empty, all centroids NaN, `errors` NaN, no early stop (runs all `runs` rounds). KMedians: `sorted.getDouble(0)` on an empty var -> exception. Trigger: k=3 on data with two tight groups + Forgy init picking two points in one group.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMCluster.java:213,224-231` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `capabilities()` allows missing input values, but a NaN feature poisons `distance.compute` (NaN), forces the row into cluster 0 (NaN comparisons are false), and `Mean.of` then produces a NaN centroid for cluster 0, collapsing the clustering as above. Either missing inputs must be rejected or imputed/skipped. Instance `weights` are also ignored (line 179).
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMClusterInit.java:60-76` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `PlusPlus` samples the next centroid with probability proportional to D(x) (`distance.compute`) instead of D(x)^2 as k-means++ prescribes (`reduced` gives the squared Euclidean). Also if every non-chosen point coincides with a chosen centroid, `sum == 0` and `Doubles.div(p, 0, 0, ...)` yields NaN weights passed to `sampleWeightedWR`. Trigger: k > number of distinct rows.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMedoids.java:151,317` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Both ALTERNATE and PAM evaluate the candidate configuration with `computeError(x, nextAssign, centroidIndexes, cache)` -- the NEW assignment indexed into the OLD medoid array. `nextAssign[i]` is the argmin over `nextCentroidIndexes`, so `sum_i d(i, old[nextAssign[i]]) >= sum_i d(i, old[assign[i]]) = error` (the old assignment is optimal for the old medoids). `nextError < error` is therefore never true, the first iteration breaks with "No improvements detected", and the model returns its INITIAL medoids (random for ALTERNATE, BUILD-greedy for PAM). Expected: `computeError(x, nextAssign, nextCentroidIndexes, cache)`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMedoids.java:181-208` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `alternateSwap` builds `clusters` keyed by cluster POSITION (`assign[i]` in 0..k-1) but looks them up with `clusters.get(cint[i])`, the medoid's INSTANCE index. For medoid indices >= k the lookup is null and the cluster is skipped; for indices < k it evaluates the wrong cluster's members. Expected: `clusters.get(i)`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMedoids.java:461` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `pamSwap` skips candidates with `if (assign[h] == h)`, comparing a cluster position with an instance index. Intended: skip h that is already a medoid. Actual: current medoids are NOT excluded (a swap can produce a duplicate medoid) while ordinary instances with `assign[h] == h` (e.g. instance 0 in cluster 0) are wrongly excluded.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/KMedoids.java:106-118` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit` never sets `learned = true` (KMCluster/MWKMeans/OneClassSvm do), so `hasLearned()` is false after a successful fit and `toString()` reports `fitted=false`. Instance `weights` are ignored.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/MWKMeans.java:291-349,357` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `findMinimum` (Minkowski centre) relies on `y` being sorted ascending: `findLeft` stops at the first `y[i] >= c0` and `findRight` at the first `y[i] <= c0` scanning from the end, which only yields the neighbours of `c0` for sorted input. `recomputeCentroids` passes `xc.selsq(1, j).copy()` UNSORTED (instance order). Traced example, p=2, column [5,1,2]: c0=2 (min point error), findLeft -> 1, findRight -> 2, bisection over [1,2] returns 2, but the true minimiser (the mean) is 2.667. Wrong centroids for every p > 1 whenever the data is not sorted along each feature within a cluster (i.e. essentially always). Expected: sort `ykj` first.
- `rapaio/ml/model/km/MWKMeans.java:353-364` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] No empty-cluster handling: for an empty cluster `computeCentroidIndexes` returns `[]`, `xc` has 0 rows, and `findMinimum` calls `y.getDouble(errors.argmin())` on an empty array -> exception (p>1); for p=1 `Quantiles` of an empty var yields NaN centroid. Trigger: Forgy init choosing two near-identical points so one cluster loses all members after the first reassignment. Instance weights `w` (line 148) are ignored.

### knn/

- `rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:136-152` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `computeTop` keeps the neighbours in a `TreeSet` ordered by distance; two training rows at the SAME distance from `x` compare equal and the second is silently dropped, so `top.size() < k`. `indexes = new int[k]` is then only partially filled and the trailing entries stay 0: training instance 0 is used as a neighbour (possibly several times). Trigger: duplicate training rows (identical feature vectors), or any exact tie; also `k+1 > n`. Expected: exactly k distinct nearest neighbours (tie-break on index).
- `rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:165` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `w.clamp_(1 - eps.get(), eps.get())` passes min=1-eps, max=eps (arguments swapped). `UnaryOpClamp` first raises values below min to 1-eps and then lowers values above max to eps, so EVERY normalised distance becomes `eps` (1e-6). All kernels then return one constant for all neighbours (RECTANGULAR 0.5, TRIANGULAR 1-1e-6, INV 1e6, GAUSSIAN pdf(1e-6*q), ...): weighted k-NN degenerates to a plain unweighted mean for every kernel. Expected: `clamp_(eps, 1 - eps)`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:76-77,157` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Parameter `wdistance` ("distance function used as basis for computing weights") is accepted but never used; `computeWeights` uses `distance.get()`. Instance `weights` passed to `coreFit` are also ignored.
- `rapaio/ml/model/knn/KnnRegression.java:159-163` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `wref` is the distance to the (k+1)-th neighbour; if it is 0 (k+1 training rows identical to the query) `w.div_(0)` produces NaN (0/0) or Inf, `clamp_` leaves NaN unchanged, and the prediction becomes NaN.

### simple/

- `rapaio/ml/model/simple/L1Regression.java:68`, `rapaio/ml/model/simple/L2Regression.java:59` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `newInstance()` returns a bare `new L1Regression()`/`new L2Regression()` without `copyParameterValues(this)`, unlike every other model in the package; base parameters (`seed`, `runningHook`, ...) are lost, violating the documented "new instance with the same parameters" contract. Both models also ignore instance `weights` (unweighted median/mean) although `fit(df, weights, ...)` accepts them. ConstantRegression and RandomValueRegression are clean.

### bayes/

- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:170-178` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Posterior is computed as `exp(sumLog)` per class without subtracting the maximum log-score (no log-sum-exp). With many features `sumLog` drops below about -745 for every class, all `exp` underflow to 0, `dv.normalize()` skips (norm == 0), densities are all 0 and `findBestLabel()` (argmax of zeros) returns the FIRST level for every row. Trigger: ~100 Gaussian features with per-feature density ~1e-4 (log ~ -9) -> sumLog ~ -900. Expected: `exp(sumLog - maxLog)`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/NaiveBayes.java:143-152` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `weights` are used by the prior (`PriorMLE.fitPriors` -> `fromLevelWeights`) but by NONE of the estimators (Bernoulli/Multinoulli pass `null` to `DensityTable.fromLabels`; Gaussian/Kernel/Multinomial/Poisson never read them), so a weighted fit produces a weighted prior with unweighted likelihoods.

### bayes/nb/

- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/BernoulliEstimator.java:117-123` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The table is built with rows = test levels and cols = target levels, then `normalizeOnRows()` divides each ROW by its sum, i.e. produces P(target | test). Naive Bayes needs P(test | target), which is per-COLUMN normalisation (`normalizeOnCols()`, as MultinoulliEstimator correctly does at lines 163/196). `predict` therefore returns P(c | x) instead of P(x | c); with unbalanced classes or unbalanced feature frequencies the posterior is wrong (e.g. target counts a:90, b:10, feature true in 45 a's and 5 b's: returned P(a|true)=0.9 vs correct P(true|a)=0.5).
- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:170` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `fit` returns `false` unconditionally after successfully fitting (`hasLearned` is also never set). `NaiveBayes.coreFit` (lines 147-151) treats `false` as failure and throws `IllegalStateException("Estimator: Multinomial{...} cannot be fitted.")`, so the estimator is unusable inside NaiveBayes. Expected: `return true`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinomialEstimator.java:182-184` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `value != Math.rint(value)` is true for NaN, so a missing value in any test variable aborts `fit` with the misleading message "must contain only semi-positive integer values", although NaiveBayes advertises `allowMissingInputValues=true`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/MultinoulliEstimator.java:157,202-221` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Nominal case: the table is built with `withMissing=false`, but `predict` falls back to `testLabel = "?"` for a missing or unseen test label and calls `density.get("?", targetLevel)`; `IndexLabel.getIndex` returns -1 for the unknown row and `values.getDouble(-1, col)` throws. Trigger: a missing nominal input, or a level at predict time not seen at fit time. NaiveBayes capabilities allow missing inputs. Expected: a smoothed/uniform likelihood for unknown levels.
- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/GaussianEstimator.java:131-137,148-150` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] (a) `stats.get(label).mean()` NPEs for a target level present in the dictionary but absent from the training rows (e.g. a mapped/split frame). (b) `predict` on a row with a missing test value evaluates `pdf(NaN)` = NaN, which propagates through `NaiveBayes` (sumLog NaN -> exp NaN -> normalize divides by NaN -> all densities NaN, arbitrary label), contradicting `allowMissingInputValues=true`; OnlineStat skips NaN at fit time, so only predict is affected.
- `rapaio/ml/model/bayes/nb/PoissonEstimator.java:118-137` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The validity loop passes NaN (`NaN < 0` and `abs(NaN - rint(NaN)) > 1e-100` are both false), then `fromLevelWeights(..., testVar)` sums the raw test values per class, so ONE missing test value in a class makes `sum` NaN, `lambda` NaN, and the class is assigned `Poisson.of(1e-20)`: its likelihood for any x > 0 is ~0 and the class can never be predicted. Expected: skip missing rows in both `counts` and `sums`.

### Summary

The libsvm numerical core (SolverC/SolverNU, kernel matrices, sigmoid_train, multiclass coupling, svm_train/predict) is a faithful port, but the surrounding glue is not: the kernel Cache grow path uses a symmetric `pad` and corrupts cached Q columns whenever shrinking is active (the default), and SvmClassifier mishandles class weights, binary targets (inverted labels), explicit `levels`, the k=2 non-probability path (AIOOBE) and the alignment of libsvm's label-ordered outputs with rapaio level columns. BinarySMO crashes with `prob=true` on the default (linear) kernel and in one-vs-all mode. The clustering package is the weakest: KMedoids never performs a single improving swap (candidate cost is computed against the old medoids), KMCluster never repairs empty clusters, and MWKMeans computes Minkowski centres on unsorted data and crashes on empty clusters. RVM has a wrong initial-alpha formula (unsquared projection), a NaN heuristic that blacklists all features negatively correlated with the target, and non-reproducible feature sampling. KnnRegression's swapped `clamp_` arguments make every kernel degenerate to a uniform mean, and ties drop neighbours. Naive Bayes lacks log-sum-exp, Bernoulli normalises on the wrong axis (P(c|x) instead of P(x|c)), Multinomial's `fit` always returns false, and Poisson/Multinoulli/Gaussian mishandle missing inputs despite the classifier advertising support for them. Instance weights are accepted but ignored by every model in scope except BinarySMO and the NB prior.

Counts: 42 findings -- bug: 26, likely-bug: 8, contract: 8. Confidence: HIGH 40, MEDIUM 2, LOW 0.

## Part 08 -- ml.model: base classes, tree, boost, ensemble, meta, rule, linear

### Coverage

All files were read top to bottom (8864 lines total). Supporting files read to confirm claims: `rapaio/core/tools/DensityVector.java`, `rapaio/core/tools/DensityTable.java`, `rapaio/data/index/IndexLabel.java`, `rapaio/data/VarNominal.java` (levels/setLabel/clearRows), `rapaio/data/VarBinary.java` (levels/getLabel/getInt), `rapaio/text/Formatters.java`, `rapaio/data/RowComparators.java`, `rapaio/core/stat/WeightedOnlineStat.java`, `rapaio/ml/loss/L2LossFunction.java`, `rapaio/ml/loss/KDevianceLossFunction.java`, `rapaio/ml/common/VarSelector.java`, `rapaio/core/param/ValueParam.java`, `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArray.java` (norm), `rapaio/data/SolidFrame.java` (matrix), test `tst/rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/PruningTest.java`, `tst/rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTreeTest.java`.

Important data-model fact verified for this review: in this code base `VarNominal.levels()` does NOT contain the `"?"` missing level (missing is stored as index -1, `getInt` of a non-missing label is the 0-based dictionary index). `VarBinary.levels()` returns `["true","false"]` while `VarBinary.getLabel()` returns `"0"`/`"1"`.
