Clean files (no defect found): ClusteringResult, RunInfo, RTreeLossFunction, RowPredicate, all 11 `rowpredicate/*` records, ctree/Candidate, ctree/Node, ctree/Purity, ctree/Splitter, rtree/Candidate, rtree/SearchPayload, rtree/Splitter, GBTRtree, RForest, onerule/Rule, onerule/RuleSet, onerule/NominalRule, onerule/NumericRule, onerule/HolteBinning, Centering, Scaling (as a class; see Ridge finding), WeightedLinearRegression, BaseLinearRegressionModel.

### Findings

#### rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:408` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `getRandom()` returns an unseeded `Random` whenever `seed` equals its default `0L`, so `seed.set(0L)` (a legitimate seed) does not make the run reproducible although the Javadoc promises "running model multiple times with the same seed will produce the same results"; two fits with `seed.set(0L)` differ. Same in `RegressionModel.java:344` and `ClusteringModel.java` (`seed.get() == 0`).
- `rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierModel.java:288` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `targetLevels` stores `df.rvar(tn).levels()`, which for `VarNominal` is the live internal `dict` list, not a copy; adding a label to the training target variable after `fit` (e.g. `df.rvar("class").addLabel("new")`) silently changes `firstTargetLevels()` of the fitted model and desynchronizes it from densities/trees built at fit time.

#### rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierResult.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/ClassifierResult.java:238` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] In `toSummary` the confusion matrix for every target is built against `df.rvar(model.firstTargetName())` instead of `df.rvar(targetName)`; for a multi-target result the second and later targets are compared with the first target's truth column and produce a wrong confusion summary.

#### rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:259` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Depth limit is off by one: root has `depth = 0` and a node is only forced to be a leaf when `node.depth > maxDepth`, so children at `depth == maxDepth` are still split. `CTree.newDecisionStump()` (`maxDepth=1`) on iris grows root -> children (depth 1, still split) -> grandchildren (depth 2): a "stump" with two levels of splits. Expected: no node at depth >= maxDepth is split (compare `RTree`, where root depth is 1 and the check is `>=`, making its `maxDepth=2` stump correct).
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:285` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Candidates are sorted with `Double.compare`, which ranks `NaN` above every finite score, so a `NaN`-scored candidate (produced e.g. by `Purity.GainRatio` on a `Search.NumericRandom` split where all rows fall on one side, giving `0/0`, or by `missingPenalty` when the weight sum is 0) always wins and `NaN <= minGain` is false, so the node is split on a meaningless test. Expected: `NaN` candidates discarded.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/CTree.java:256` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Fitting on a frame with `rowCount()==0` (capabilities do not check row count) reaches `node.bestLabel = node.parent.bestLabel` for the root, whose parent is `null` -> `NullPointerException` instead of a clear validation error.

#### rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:63-67` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `NumericRandom` accumulates missing-row weights in an `int` (`int missingWeights = 0; missingWeights += w.getDouble(i)`), truncating every partial sum toward zero. With `missingPenalty=true` and fractional weights (e.g. the normalized weights AdaBoost or `Splitter.Weighted` produce, all `< 1`) the missing weight is always 0 and the penalty is never applied. `NumericBinary` correctly uses a `double`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:56-58` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `NumericRandom` picks a split row with `do { split = random.nextInt(n) } while (df.isMissing(split, testName))`; when every row of the node has a missing test value (a column that is all-missing in a subtree, allowed by `allowMissingInputValues=true`) this loop never terminates. Expected: return `null` when there is no complete row.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:120` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `NumericBinary` min-count guard is asymmetric: after processing sorted index `i` the left child has `i+1` rows and the right has `len-i-1`; the condition `i >= minCount && i < len - minCount` requires left >= `minCount+1` but right >= `minCount`. Concrete: `minCount=1`, two rows with distinct values -> no split is ever produced (`i=0` fails `0 >= 1`), and for three rows `{1,2,3}` only the split after the second value is allowed, never after the first. Expected `i + 1 >= minCount`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:152-157` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `Binary` builds the table with `DensityTable.fromLabels(false, test, target, null)` (weights `null` -> counts) and scores that table, so instance weights are ignored for BINARY test variables; `NominalFull`/`NominalBinary` build a second, weighted table for the score. Trigger: AdaBoost (re-weighted rows) over a CTree with binary inputs -- the boosted weights have no effect on split selection for those variables.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:223` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `NominalBinary` two-class shortcut sorts test levels by the raw weight of class 0 (`dtWeights.get(k,0)`) instead of the proportion of class 0 within the level (`get(k,0)/rowTotal(k)`); the Breiman ordering theorem that justifies scanning only prefixes of the sorted list holds for proportions, so the best subset split can be missed. Example: level A = (class0=10, class1=90), level B = (2, 0): A sorts before B although B is the "purest class 0" level.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Search.java:176-190,191-300` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `CTree.missingPenalty` is documented as multiplying "the score of the purity function for some given test" but `NominalFull` and `NominalBinary` never apply it; the parameter silently has no effect for nominal test variables.

#### rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Pruning.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/ctree/Pruning.java:83-110,187` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `ReducedError` with `all=true` (the default of the two-argument `prune(tree, df)`) cuts the node with the largest `delta` in every iteration even when that delta is negative (`maxAcc` starts at -1 and only `!all` excludes negative deltas), so the loop keeps cutting until the tree is a single root node, regardless of the pruning set. `PruningTest.reducedErrorPruningTest` passes only because of this (pruning a 100%-training-accuracy tree on its own training set could otherwise not reduce node count). Reduced-error pruning must never apply a cut that lowers hold-out accuracy.

#### rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:234` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Copy-paste: for a zero-weight node `node.weight = node.parent.value` assigns the parent's *mean target value* as the node weight (the line above assigns `node.value = parent.value`). Trigger: `Splitter.Ignore` with missing test values or `Search.NominalFull` with a level absent in the node -> empty child; at predict time a row with a missing test value on that split is averaged with `WeightedMean(values, weights)` using a weight equal to a target mean (possibly negative or huge) -> wrong or `NaN` prediction. Expected weight 0 (or parent weight).
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:242` with `rtree/Search.java:119` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Candidates are computed in a `.parallel()` stream while sharing the single `Random` from `getRandom()`; `Search.NumericBinary` consumes `random.nextDouble()` for tie-breaking and `Search.NominalBinary`/`Splitter` consume it too, so the order of draws depends on thread scheduling and a fixed `seed` does not reproduce the fitted tree whenever ties occur (common with discrete targets). Expected: deterministic per-variable RNG or sequential evaluation.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/RTree.java:301` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] For a row that matches no child (missing test value) the aggregated prediction returns `Mean.of(weights)` as its weight rather than the sum of the children's weights; when this aggregate is itself combined one level up it is under-weighted relative to sibling leaves whose weight is a sum. Expected: sum of child weights.

#### rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:68,78` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `NumericBinary` takes `rows = rowsComplete()` and immediately reads `rows[0]` and `rows[rows.length-1]`; when the test variable is missing in every row of the node (allowed: `allowMissingInputValues=true`), `rows` is empty and `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` is thrown from `fit`. Expected: `Optional.empty()`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:105` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Loop bounds `i = minCount .. rows.length - minCount - 2` require both children to have at least `minCount + 1` rows (left has `i+1`, right has `len-i-1`), contradicting the `minCount` Javadoc "Minimum number of observations in a leaf node"; with `minCount=1` (CART default) a node of 3 complete rows can never be split and leaves of exactly 1 row are never created by numeric splits (they are by nominal splits, which use `>= minCount`).
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Search.java:163-172` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `NominalFull` Javadoc says a candidate is built "if at least two of them have enough instances", but the code returns `Optional.empty()` unless *every* dictionary level has `count >= minCount` (`validCount != len`). Because mapped sub-frames keep the full dictionary, any nominal variable with a level absent in the node (nearly always below the root) is never split on by `NominalFull`, the default search for NOMINAL inputs (`DEFAULT_TEST_MAP`, `newC45`).

#### rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Node.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/tree/rtree/Node.java:69,78` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `boostUpdate` re-routes rows with unit weights (`VarDouble.fill(x.rowCount(), 1)`) and `Splitter.Random` draws a fresh assignment of missing-value rows, so a leaf that received only missing-routed rows at fit time can receive zero rows here; `L2LossFunction.additiveScalarMinimizer` of an empty var is `Mean` of nothing -> `NaN` leaf value, which propagates `NaN` into every GBT prediction that reaches that leaf. Also, sample weights used to fit the tree are not used in the leaf refit.

#### rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:175,200` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `corePredict` builds the result with the caller's `withClasses` but unconditionally executes `fit.firstClasses().setInt(i, best)`; `predict(df, false, true)` -> `firstClasses()` is `null` -> `NullPointerException`. Expected: classes written only when requested (or always built, as densities are).
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/AdaBoost.java:191-199` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Densities are normalized by `total = sum of alpha votes`; `alpha` can be negative (`err > 1-1/k` when `stopOnError=false`) or the learner list can be empty (first round rejected by `stopOnError`), giving `total <= 0` and densities that are negative, infinite or `NaN` (`0/0` when no learner). No guard.

#### rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:180,204,213-218` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `corePredict` honours `withClasses`/`withDistributions` when building the `ClassifierResult` but then writes `cr.firstDensity().setDouble(...)` and `cr.firstClasses().setInt(...)` unconditionally; `predict(df, true, false)` or `predict(df, false, true)` throw `NullPointerException`. The class decision is also computed from the density frame, so classes cannot be produced without densities.
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTClassifierModel.java:167` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `tree.boostUpdate(df, ...)` refits leaf values with unit weights (see `rtree/Node.java:78`), so the instance weights accepted by `fit(df, weights, ...)` influence only the tree structure and the row sampler, not the boosted leaf values; a weighted fit is therefore not a weighted K-class deviance minimizer.

#### rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:133,146,150,155-156` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Instance weights are accepted but ignored in the boosting loop: the gradient (`loss.gradient(y, fitValues)`), the tree fit (`tree.fit(xmLearn, "target")` -- two-argument form, unit weights), the leaf refit (`boostUpdate`) and the error scores are all unweighted; `weights` only reaches `initModel.fit` and the row sampler. `fit(df, weights, y)` with weights `{1, 100}` produces the same trees as unit weights.
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:128` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `initModel.get()` is fitted in place (no `newInstance()`), unlike `model.get().newInstance()` for the trees; `newInstance()`/`copyParameterValues` copies the same reference, so two `GBTRegressionModel` instances derived from each other share one `L2Regression` object and fitting the second silently overwrites the first model's initial prediction, changing its `predict` output. Aliasing bug.
- `rapaio/ml/model/boost/GBTRegressionModel.java:135` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: LOW] The gradient column is bound as `"target"`; an input frame that already contains a variable named `target` makes `bindVars` produce a duplicated name and the tree fits/predicts on the wrong column (or throws), with no validation.

#### rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:381,387` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `oobCompute` calls `oobPredictedClasses.clearRows()` (sets the nominal var's `rows` to 0) and then `setLabel(i, ...)` for every row; `VarNominal.setLabel` writes into the still-allocated backing array without restoring `rows`, so after `fit` `oobPredictedClasses()` returns a variable of size 0 (its content is unreachable). Expected: overwrite labels without clearing.
- `rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:385-393` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The OOB error loops over *all* training rows: a row that has never been out of bag has an all-zero density row, `argmax()` returns 0, so it is predicted as the first target level and counted as an error whenever its true class is not level 0, and it is counted in `totalOobInstances`. After the first tree ~63% of rows are never-OOB, so `oobError` is inflated (for iris after 1 tree roughly `0.63*2/3 + ...`), and even after many trees rows never sampled out remain wrongly counted. Expected: skip rows with zero OOB votes.
- `rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:474-477` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `toFullContent` prints `getGainVIInfo()` under the heading "Permutation Variable Importance" (copy-paste of the previous block); `getPermVIInfo()` is never printed.
- `rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:115,447` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `classWeights` is a public parameter passed to `BaggingMode.computeDensity`, but neither `HARD_VOTE` nor `SOFT_VOTE` reads it; class weights set by the user are silently ignored.
- `rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/CForest.java:167` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `capabilities()` advertises `VarType.BINARY` targets, but the default weak model `CTree` accepts only NOMINAL targets, so `CForest.newModel().fit(df, "binaryTarget")` fails inside the first weak learner with a capability exception from `CTree` rather than at the forest's own check.

#### rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingMode.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/ensemble/BaggingMode.java:70-79` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `SOFT_VOTE` divides each tree's density row by its sum `t` without guarding `t == 0`; a `CTree` leaf with zero rows (produced by `Search.NominalFull` for an absent level or `Splitter.Ignore` dropping all missing rows) has an all-zero density that `normalize()` leaves at zero, so `0/0 = NaN` is added and the whole forest density for that row becomes `NaN` (class then chosen by `argmax` over `NaN`s). Expected: skip zero-sum votes.

#### rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/meta/CStacking.java:89` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `fullName()` returns `"CStacking{stacker=...;"` -- the closing `}` is missing and the learners are not listed; `ParametricEquals`-style comparisons or logs relying on `fullName()` get a malformed description.

#### rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:156` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] With the default `MissingHandler.MAJORITY`, a test row whose value is missing on the best rule's variable returns `Pair.from(testVar, missingDensity)` where `testVar` is the *input variable name*; `corePredict` then calls `firstClasses().setLabel(i, "petal-length")`, adding the variable name as a new class level and predicting it. Expected `missingDensity.findBestLabel()`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rule/OneRule.java:200-203,175-176` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] For BINARY inputs (allowed by capabilities) `buildNominal` builds rule test labels from `VarBinary.levels()` = `["true","false"]` indexed by `getInt()+1` (so value 0 -> `"true"`, value 1 -> `"false"`), while `predict` compares `df.getLabel(row, testVar)`, which for `VarBinary` yields `"0"`/`"1"`; no `NominalRule` ever matches, and every prediction falls through to label `"?"` with an all-zero density whenever a binary variable is selected as the best rule set. The label swap also makes the printed rules wrong.

#### rapaio/ml/model/rule/ZeroRule.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/rule/ZeroRule.java:75,98` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Capabilities allow a BINARY target; `coreFit` derives `prediction` from `IndexLabel.fromVarLevels(false, binaryVar)` = `"0"`/`"1"`, but the density frame columns are `model.targetLevels()` = `VarBinary.levels()` = `["true","false"]`, so `firstDensity().setDouble(i, "1", 1)` addresses a non-existent column and `predict(df)` throws for a binary target.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rule/ZeroRule.java:62` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `newInstance()` returns `new ZeroRule()` without `copyParameterValues(this)`, violating the base-class contract "new classifier instance with the same parameters as the original" (`seed`, `runningHook`, `poolSize` are dropped when used as a weak learner in CForest/CStacking).

#### rapaio/ml/model/rule/ManualClassifier.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/rule/ManualClassifier.java:101` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] For a BINARY target (allowed) the density columns are named `"true"`/`"false"` (from `VarBinary.levels()`), but `label` is `"0"`/`"1"` (from `Unique.ofLabel` / user rules), so `v.name().equals(label)` is never true and all densities are 0 while classes are set. For NOMINAL targets it is correct.
- `rapaio/ml/model/rule/ManualClassifier.java:76-78` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit(df, weights)` computes the majority label by unweighted counts; instance weights are accepted and ignored.

#### rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java:272` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `Initialize.EXPECTED_LOG_VAR` computes `log(min(1e-12, m(1-m)))`; `min` with `1e-12` makes the result `log(1e-12) ~= -27.6` for every data set (the intended floor should be `max`). All weights are initialised to -27.6, so `x.mv(w)` saturates the logistic and the first IRLS/Newton Hessian is built from clamped `p(1-p)=1e-6`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java:257-258` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Densities are written positionally: column 0 <- `pi`, column 1 <- `1-pi`, assuming the positive label is the first target level. With `nominalLevel` set to a level other than `firstTargetLevels().get(0)` (e.g. iris `nominalLevel="versicolor"`) column 0 (`setosa`) receives the probability of `versicolor`, while `firstClasses()` is set by name and stays correct -> densities disagree with classes. For a BINARY target it is only accidentally right (`["true","false"]` vs positive `"1"`).
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java:318-325` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `toSummary` allocates `TextTable.empty(w.size()+1, 2, ...)`, writes headers in row 0, then writes coefficient `i` into row `i` (not `i+1`): the header row is overwritten by the first coefficient and the last row stays empty.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java:91,159-190` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `l1penalty` is a public parameter but is never passed to either solver (only `l2penalty` is); setting it has no effect.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java:159-190` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit(df, weights)` never uses `weights` (neither solver has a weight input); `fit(df, weights, target)` silently fits an unweighted model.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/BinaryLogistic.java:218-222` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] If `nominalLevel` is set to a string that is not a level of the target, `y` is all zeros and `negativeLabel` becomes the first level; the fit proceeds silently to a degenerate all-negative model instead of throwing.

#### rapaio/ml/model/linear/binarylogistic/BinaryLogisticIRLS.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/binarylogistic/BinaryLogisticIRLS.java:128` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The penalty added to the negative log-likelihood is `lambda * w.norm(2.) / 2`, and `DArray.norm(2)` is `sqrt(sum w^2)` (verified in `BaseDoubleStrideDArray.norm`), i.e. `lambda*||w||/2`, whereas the update (`H += lambda*I`, implicit gradient `lambda*w`) minimizes `lambda*||w||^2/2`. The reported `nlls` and the relative-change convergence test therefore track a different objective than the one being optimized; for `lambda>0` convergence can be declared/denied on the wrong function. Same at `BinaryLogisticNewton.java:133`.

#### rapaio/ml/model/linear/binarylogistic/BinaryLogisticNewton.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/binarylogistic/BinaryLogisticNewton.java:145-159` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] With `lambdap > 0` the Hessian includes `lambda*I` but the gradient used for the Newton direction is `ng = X^t (y - p)` without the `-lambda*w` term, so the fixed point of the iteration (`ng = 0`) is the *unregularized* MLE; the L2 penalty only damps the step and never changes the solution (IRLS handles this correctly through `z`). Trigger: `BinaryLogistic.newModel().solver.set(NEWTON).l2penalty.set(1.0)` converges to the same coefficients as `l2penalty=0`.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/binarylogistic/BinaryLogisticNewton.java:177` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] In the line search the candidate `wcnew` is evaluated with `negativeLogLikelihood(vy, vny, wc, ...)` -- the penalty term is computed for the previous candidate `wc` while `pp/nnp` come from `wcnew`; with `lambda>0` the compared values are inconsistent and the accepted step can be worse than rejected ones.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/binarylogistic/BinaryLogisticNewton.java:105-113` (and IRLS `:92-100`) -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: LOW] On convergence the just-computed `wnew` is discarded (`return` before `ws.add(wnew)`), so `Result.w()` is the previous iterate; with `eps` at the defaults the difference is negligible, but `iterationLoss.size()` under-reports the iterations by one.

#### rapaio/ml/model/linear/RidgeRegressionModel.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/RidgeRegressionModel.java:129-150,176-192` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] With `intercept=true` and `Centering.NONE` the intercept column is removed from the penalized solve and afterwards reconstructed as `targetMean - sum(beta_j * inputMean_j)`; with `NONE` all means are 0, so the intercept is always exactly 0 and the model is fitted *without* an intercept although `intercept=true` was requested (`newModel(lambda, Centering.NONE, Scaling.NONE)`).
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/RidgeRegressionModel.java:106-108` with `Scaling.java:40,46` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] A constant input column (or constant target) gives `Scaling.SD`/`NORM` = 0 and `X` is filled with `(x-mean)/0 = NaN` (or `Inf`); the QR solve then yields all-`NaN` coefficients silently. No validation, no fallback to scale 1.
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/RidgeRegressionModel.java:115-165` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit(df, weights)` ignores `weights` entirely (unweighted normal equations); weighted `fit` silently produces the unweighted ridge solution.

#### rapaio/ml/model/linear/LinearRegressionResult.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/LinearRegressionResult.java:101-129` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] Standard errors, t and p values are computed with the OLS formula `sigma^2 (X^tX)^{-1}` for every `BaseLinearRegressionModel`, including `RidgeRegressionModel` (whose covariance is `sigma^2 (X^tX+lambda I)^{-1} X^tX (X^tX+lambda I)^{-1}`) and `WeightedLinearRegression` (which needs `(X^tWX)^{-1}`); the printed significance for those models is therefore wrong while presented as valid inference.

#### rapaio/ml/model/linear/LinearRegressionModel.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/linear/LinearRegressionModel.java:66-72` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `coreFit(df, weights)` ignores `weights` (plain `X.qr().solve(Y)`); `fit(df, weights, ...)` accepted by the base class silently yields the unweighted fit (a separate `WeightedLinearRegression` exists, but the method contract on this class is not honoured and no error is raised).

#### rapaio/ml/model/RegressionModel.java / ClusteringModel.java
- `rapaio/ml/model/RegressionModel.java:344`, `rapaio/ml/model/ClusteringModel.java:208` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Same seed-zero issue as `ClassifierModel.java:408`: `seed.set(0L)` is treated as "no seed".
- `rapaio/ml/model/RegressionModel.java:77-79` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `stoppingHook` is declared on every regression model but no implementation in this part (`GBTRegressionModel`, `RForest`, `RTree`, linear models) ever consults it; a user-supplied stopping criterion is silently ignored. (`ClassifierModel.stoppingHook` is likewise unused by `AdaBoost`, `GBTClassifierModel`, `CForest`, `CTree`, `CStacking`.)

### Summary

The base classes and the predicate/loss plumbing are solid; the defects concentrate in the learners themselves, with recurring themes: (1) **sample weights accepted but ignored or half-applied** (ctree `Search.Binary`, `GBTRegressionModel`, `BinaryLogistic`, `RidgeRegressionModel`, `LinearRegressionModel`, `ManualClassifier`, `boostUpdate` leaf refits); (2) **BINARY variables mishandled** because `VarBinary.levels()` (`true/false`) disagrees with `getLabel()` (`0/1`), breaking `OneRule`, `ZeroRule` and `ManualClassifier` on binary data; (3) **`NullPointerException` on the documented `predict(df, withClasses, withDistributions)` flags** in `AdaBoost` and `GBTClassifierModel`; (4) **off-by-one / asymmetric min-count and depth checks** in `CTree` (`maxDepth`, `NumericBinary`) and `rtree.Search`.

Most serious: `CForest` OOB error counts never-out-of-bag rows as misclassified and `oobPredictedClasses` ends up empty; `RTree` assigns a target mean as a node weight (`node.weight = parent.value`); `BinaryLogisticNewton` ignores the L2 penalty in the gradient (regularization has no effect on the solution) and evaluates the line search with the wrong `w`; `CTree.newDecisionStump()` grows two levels; `OneRule` predicts the input variable name for missing values; `Pruning.ReducedError` with the default `all=true` collapses the tree to its root; `rtree.Search.NominalFull` never splits on a nominal variable with an absent level, contrary to its Javadoc; `rtree.Search.NumericBinary` throws on an all-missing column; `ctree.Search.NumericRandom` loops forever on an all-missing column.

Counts: 52 findings -- bug: 29, likely-bug: 11, contract: 12. Confidence: HIGH 40, MEDIUM 10, LOW 2.

### Findings

### ml/analysis

- `rapaio/ml/analysis/PCA.java:59-64` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `eps` and `maxRuns` are public parameters (with Javadoc "convergence" / "maximum number of iterations") that are never read anywhere in the class; any value set by the caller is silently ignored. Trigger: `PCA.newModel().maxRuns.set(1)` behaves identically to the default.
- `rapaio/ml/analysis/LDA.java:124` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The discriminant directions are taken as eigenvectors of `Sb * Sw^-1` instead of `Sw^-1 * Sb`. Reference: Fisher directions w solve `Sb w = lambda Sw w`, i.e. eigenvectors of `Sw^-1 Sb`. If `v` is an eigenvector of `Sb Sw^-1` then `Sw^-1 v` (not `v`) is the discriminant, so the returned `eigenVectors` are `Sw * w` -- rotated/scaled wrongly unless `Sw` is (a multiple of) the identity. Trigger: any data with correlated inputs, e.g. two Gaussian classes with covariance [[1,0.9],[0.9,1]]: the projection is not along the Fisher direction, class separation in `transform` output is degraded. Eigenvalues are correct (same spectrum), which hides the bug in `toSummary`.
- `rapaio/ml/analysis/LDA.java:112-118` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] With `scaling=false`, `Sw` is built from `xc = (mxx - vmean)/vstd` (divided by std) while `Sb` is built from `mcmeans - vmean` (not divided by std), so the two scatter matrices are expressed in different units and `Sb Sw^-1` is not the LDA generalized eigenproblem. Trigger: `LDA.newModel().scaling.set(false).fit(df, "class")` on inputs with std != 1 (e.g. one input in metres, one in millimetres) -- directions change with the units of the inputs. Also note the comment says "within scatter" but `xc` is centred on the global mean, so `Sw` is really the total scatter `St = Sw + Sb` (same eigenvectors as the true problem, different eigenvalues lambda/(1+lambda)); with the unit mismatch above even that equivalence is lost.
- `rapaio/ml/analysis/LDA.java:171` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `transform` standardizes the new frame with the new frame's own mean/std (`x.sub_(x.mean1d(0)).div_(x.std1d(0, 0))`) rather than the statistics stored at fit time, so the projection of a test set is not in the same coordinate system as training. Trigger: fit on 1000 rows, `transform` a single row -> std of one row is 0 -> division by zero -> all NaN/Inf output; transform of a shifted subset gives shifted projections.
- `rapaio/ml/analysis/LDA.java:98-104,204` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `targetLevels = var.levels()` is the variable's dictionary, which may contain levels with zero rows in `df` (a level that only appears in another split, or left after filtering). For such a level `mxx.sel(0, int[0]).mean1d(0)` is NaN, that NaN row enters `mcmeansc`, and `Sb`, the eigen-decomposition and every output become NaN. Trigger: `VarNominal` with levels {a,b,c} where no row has label "c", or a CV fold that lacks one class. Expected: unused levels ignored or an explicit exception.

### ml/common

- `rapaio/ml/common/Capabilities.java:172-175` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `checkTargetCount` reports `minInputCount` / `maxInputCount` in its messages ("requires more than N target variables") instead of `minTargetCount` / `maxTargetCount`; copy-paste error yielding misleading exception text (e.g. a classifier with inputs [1,10000] and targets [1,1] given 2 targets says "does not allow more than 10000 target variables").
- `rapaio/ml/common/VarSelector.java:63-75` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `withVarNames` returns `new VarSelector(mCount)`, i.e. the copy's `mVars` is the computed count, not the mode constant. Consequences: (a) for `VarSelector.all()` with n>0 names the copy has `mVars = n` (not `M_ALL`), so `name()` prints `VarSelector[n]` and `nextVarNames` samples a random permutation instead of returning the names in order; (b) for `auto()` a second `withVarNames` on the copy (refit on a frame with a different variable count, as tree ensembles do) treats the old `ceil(sqrt(n))` as a fixed `m` instead of recomputing it; (c) the method also mutates `this.mCount` on the prototype as a side effect. Also `fixed(0)` silently becomes ALL and `fixed(-1)` becomes AUTO (no validation).

### ml/common/distance

- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/distance/MinkowskiDistance.java:54,63` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `pow(delta, p)` is used without `abs`. Reference: `d = (sum |x_i - y_i|^p)^(1/p)`. For odd integer p negative differences cancel positive ones (x=(0,0), y=(1,-1), p=3 -> reduced = 1 + (-1) = 0, compute = 0 instead of 2^(1/3)); for non-integer p any negative difference gives NaN (x=(0), y=(1), p=1.5 -> `pow(-1,1.5)` = NaN). Same bug in both `reduced` overloads.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

- `rapaio/ml/common/distance/Distance.java:39` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc states the triangle inequality reversed: `distance(x,y) >= distance(x,z)+distance(y,z)`; the metric property is `d(x,y) <= d(x,z) + d(z,y)`.

### ml/common/kernel

Trigger: x=(0), y=(2), sigma=1: expected exp(-2/2)=0.368, actual exp(-4/2)=0.135. Fix needs `sqrt`.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

Trigger: x=(0), y=(1), sigma=2: expected 1/(1+1/4)=0.8, actual 1/(1+1/4)=0.8 coincidentally; x=(0), y=(2), sigma=2: expected 1/(1+4/4)=0.5, actual 1/(1+16/4)=0.2.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

Trigger: alpha=1, c=0, x.y=1: expected tanh(1)=0.762, actual atan(1)=0.785; for large arguments atan tends to pi/2=1.571 instead of 1, so kernel values exceed 1.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

Code squares the already squared norm: `1 - ||x-y||^4 / (||x-y||^4 + c)`. Trigger: ||x-y||^2=2, c=2: expected 0.5, actual 1-4/6=0.333.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/kernel/InverseMultiQuadricKernel.java:56-57` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc: `1 / sqrt(||x-y||^2 + c^2)`. Code: `1 / sqrt(||x-y||^4 + c^2)`. Trigger: ||x-y||^2=3, c=4: expected 1/sqrt(19)=0.229, actual 1/sqrt(25)=0.2.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/kernel/MultiQuadricKernel.java:53-54` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc: `sqrt(||x-y||^2 + c^2)`. Code: `sqrt(||x-y||^4 + c^2)`. Same trigger as above: expected sqrt(19), actual 5.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/kernel/LogKernel.java:53` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc: `-log(||x-y||^d + 1)`. Code: `-log1p((||x-y||^2)^d)` = `-log(||x-y||^(2d) + 1)`. Trigger: d=1, ||x-y||=3: expected -log(4), actual -log(10).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/kernel/PowerKernel.java:52` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc: `-||x-y||^d`. Code: `-(||x-y||^2)^d = -||x-y||^(2d)`. Trigger: d=1, ||x-y||=3: expected -3, actual -9.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/kernel/CircularKernel.java:53-58` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Support test is inverted and uses the squared norm: Javadoc says compute when `||x-y|| < sigma`, zero otherwise; code returns 0 when `dot < sigma` (dot = squared norm) and otherwise evaluates the formula with `f = dot/sigma >= 1`, where `sqrt(1 - f^2)` and `acos(-f)` are NaN. Net effect: k = 0 inside the support and NaN outside; in particular `k(x,x) = 0`. Trigger: x=y, sigma=1 -> 0 (expected 1); ||x-y||^2=4, sigma=1 -> NaN (expected 0).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28, with `arccos(f)` rather than the Javadoc's `arccos(-f)`; see Status.

Also `f` should be `||x-y||/sigma`, not the squared norm.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.

Additionally `dot` is the squared norm, so the formula computed is `theta sin(||x-y||^2/theta)/||x-y||^2`, and the Javadoc is a copy of WaveletKernel's and does not describe this kernel at all.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: `k(x, x) = 1`, norm instead of squared norm, Javadoc rewritten.

Trigger: x=(0,1), y=(0,1) -> NaN, expected 1.~~ FIXED 2026-09-28.
- ~~`rapaio/ml/common/kernel/LinearKernel.java:36` (inherits `AbstractKernel.java:37`) -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `LinearKernel.isLinear()` returns false (not overridden) while `PolyKernel(1,...)` returns true; consumers such as `BinarySMO` therefore treat the linear kernel as non-linear (no weight vector). Related: `PolyKernel.isLinear()` (line 60) returns true for any `slope`/`bias`, and the consumer's linear path uses the plain inner product, so `PolyKernel(1, bias, slope != 1)` predictions computed via `linear_weights` ignore `slope` (MEDIUM; consumer is out of scope but the contract "isLinear" is what enables it).~~ FIXED 2026-09-28: `LinearKernel.isLinear()` is true, `PolyKernel.isLinear()` also requires `slope == 1`, contract documented on `Kernel`.
- `rapaio/ml/common/kernel/cache/KernelCache.java` -- clean for its single-threaded use in BinarySMO. Note (not a defect under current usage): `SolidCache` (`boolean[n*n]`, `double[n*n]`) and `MapCache` (`HashMap`) are not thread-safe; using one `KernelCache` from parallel streams would race.

Files clean: Kernel.java, AbstractKernel.java, PolyKernel.java (formula), RBFKernel.java, GeneralizedMinKernel.java, SplineKernel.java, MinKernel.java, WaveletKernel.java, EuclideanDistance.java, Manhattan.java, Constant.java, ClassWeights.java.

### ml/eval

- `rapaio/ml/eval/ClusterSilhouette.java:186-197,207` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] With `similarity=true` the neighbour cluster is (correctly) chosen as the one with the largest average similarity, but the score is still `s = (b - a)/max(a,b)`, which is the dissimilarity formula. For a similarity, a good clustering has `a > b`, so `s` is negative for well-separated clusters and positive for bad ones -- the sign is inverted relative to the dissimilarity case. Trigger: two perfectly separated clusters, similarity matrix with 1 within and 0 between: a=1, b=0, s = -1 (expected +1).
- `rapaio/ml/eval/ClusterSilhouette.java:179,207` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Singleton clusters: `a[row]` is set to 0 when the cluster has no other member, so `s = (b - 0)/b = 1`. Rousseeuw's definition (and sklearn/R `cluster::silhouette`) set `s(i) = 0` for singletons; the code rewards every singleton with the maximum score, inflating `overallScore` for clusterings with many singletons.
- `rapaio/ml/eval/ClassifierEvaluation.java:141`, `rapaio/ml/eval/RegressionEval.java:171` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `seed == 0` means "unseeded" (`new Random()`), so `seed.set(0L)` cannot be used to get a reproducible run and silently gives non-reproducible splits; no Javadoc documents the sentinel.
- `rapaio/ml/eval/RandIndex.java` -- clean (n=1 yields 0/0 = NaN; arguably acceptable).
- `rapaio/ml/eval/ClassifierEvaluationResult.java`, `RegressionEvaluationResult.java`, `RegressionEval.java` -- clean; shared metric instances are only mutated under `scoresLock`.

### ml/eval/metric

- `rapaio/ml/eval/metric/Confusion.java:85,125,140-143` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Row/column indices come from `getInt(i)` but labels come from `levels(true)`; for `VarType.BINARY` (accepted by `validTypes`) `VarBinary.levels()` is `["true","false"]` while `getInt` returns 1 for true and 0 for false, so true values land in the row/column labelled "false" and vice versa. Consequently `tp = cm(0,0)` counts actual-false/predicted-false, `fp`/`fn` are swapped with respect to the printed labels, and precision/recall/F1/G are computed for the class "false" while the table claims positive = "true". Trigger: `Confusion.from(VarBinary.copy(1,1,0), VarBinary.copy(1,0,0))`: printed matrix shows 1 true predicted true, actually it is the one false case. Nominal inputs are unaffected (level index == getInt).
- `rapaio/ml/eval/metric/ROC.java`, `Gini.java`, `Accuracy.java`, `LogLoss.java`, `RMSE.java`, `MAE.java` -- clean. Checked: ROC trapezoid AUC with tie groups is correct (`|dFP| * (TP_prev + TP)/2` per distinct score, final segment to (n,p), divided by `p*n`); threshold rows are "score >= threshold"; missing scores/classes skipped consistently in both passes; Gini and weighted Gini match the reference (Kaggle) formulas including the descending-score/ascending-index tie order; LogLoss clips to `[eps, 1-eps]`. Minor: ROC `auc` is NaN when p==0 or n==0 (0/0), and the first-pass sentinel `prev = Double.MIN_VALUE` (smallest positive denormal) is used where `+Infinity` is used in the second pass -- harmless unless a score is exactly +Infinity (then the row count is off by one and the last row stays zero) -- LOW.

### ml/eval/split

- `rapaio/ml/eval/split/StratifiedKFold.java:63` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `rows.get(df.getInt(i, strataName))` with a missing strata value (`getInt` = -1 for nominal missing) throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException(-1)` with no meaningful message; and unlike `KFold` there is no `folds > rowCount` / `folds <= 0` check, so `folds > rowCount` silently yields empty test folds (metrics NaN) and `folds <= 0` throws from `IntStream.range`/`strata.get(0)`.
- `rapaio/ml/eval/split/KFold.java:52` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `folds <= 0` is not validated (`new Mapping[0]` then `mappings[0]` -> `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`; negative -> `NegativeArraySizeException`) while `folds > rowCount` is. Fold assignment itself is correct: every row appears in exactly one test fold per round, all rows shuffled with the supplied `Random`.

### ml/loss

- `rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:154,168` vs `109,135` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `error`, `errorScore` and `residualErrorScore` use `alpha` (a quantile level in [0,1], default 0.25) directly as the Huber threshold delta: `a < alpha ? a^2/2 : alpha*a - alpha^2/2`, whereas `gradient` and the scalar minimizers use `rho = alpha-quantile of |residuals|` as delta (Friedman's definition). The reported loss and the optimized loss are therefore different functions. Trigger: residuals (10, 20, 30), alpha=0.25: gradient uses rho ~ 10 (quadratic region up to 10), while `error` treats everything above 0.25 as linear (0.25*a - 0.03125). Reference: L_delta(r) = r^2/2 for |r|<=delta else delta|r| - delta^2/2 with the same delta in both.
- `rapaio/ml/loss/HuberLossFunction.java:82-84`, `rapaio/ml/loss/L1LossFunction.java:49-51` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `scalarMinimizer(Var y, Var weight)` ignores `weight` and returns the unweighted result (Javadoc of `LossFunction.scalarMinimizer(y, weight)` promises the minimizer of `sum weight_i * loss(y_i, c)`). Trigger: y=(0,10), w=(1,100): L1 expected weighted median 10, returned unweighted median 5.
- `rapaio/ml/loss/L2LossFunction.java:65-66` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `gradient` computes `len = min(y.size, y_hat.size)` but then iterates `y.size()`; if `y_hat` is shorter (the case `error`/`errorScore` explicitly tolerate) it throws instead of truncating like its siblings; if `y_hat` is longer the extra values are silently ignored (also by `error`).
- `KDevianceLossFunction.java` -- clean with respect to Friedman's K-class gamma `(K-1)/K * sum r / sum |r|(1-|r|)`; `gradient`/`error` throwing `IllegalStateException` is documented by message (not flagged).

### math

- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:450-457` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `normalCdf(x, mu, sd)` returns 0 for every non-finite non-NaN `x`, including `+Infinity`; `Phi(+inf)` must be 1. Consequences: `erf(+inf) = 2*0 - 1 = -1` (expected 1), `erfc(-inf) = 2*normalCdf(+inf) = 0` (expected 2), `normalCdf(+inf) = 0`. `cdfMarsaglia` itself handles both infinities correctly, so the early return is purely harmful.
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:527-539` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `cdfMarsaglia` computes `0.5 + s*exp(-q/2 - ln sqrt(2 pi))`; for `x <~ -6` the second term is `-0.5 + p` with `p < 1e-9` so the addition loses all relative precision, and for `x <~ -8.3` (`p < 2^-54`) the result is exactly 0 (or, with the ~1e-16 rounding noise of the series, a slightly negative number). Hence `erfc(x)` for `x > ~5.9` returns 0 instead of e.g. erfc(6)=2.15e-17, erfc(8)=1.1e-29, and `normalCdf(-9)` returns 0 instead of 1.1e-19; `normalQuantile`'s refinement step for tiny `p` is fed the same garbage. Marsaglia's own note limits the algorithm to |x| < ~7; the tail needs a continued-fraction/asymptotic erfc.
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:615-618,639` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `betaIncReg` Javadoc and the validation accept `a = 0` or `b = 0` ("a and b must be positive or zero") but `lnBeta(0, b) = lnGamma(0) = NaN` and `log(a) = -inf`, so `betaIncReg(0.5, 0, 1)` returns NaN instead of 1 (I_x(0,b) = 1 for x>0). Either reject 0 or handle it.
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:626-629` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: LOW] The symmetry switch `return 1 - betaIncReg(1 - x, b, a)` relies on `1 - (1 - x) == x`; when `x` is within one ulp above the switch point `(a+1)/(a+b+2)`, rounding of `1 - x` can put the swapped argument back over the threshold and the two calls recurse into each other until `StackOverflowError`. Needs a run to find a concrete (x, a, b); a guard such as computing the flag once and calling a non-switching worker removes the risk.
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:667` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: LOW] `lentz` loops `while (abs(delta - 1) > 1e-15)` with no iteration cap. The partial numerators tend to +-x/4, not 0, so `c_n*d_n -> 1` only in the limit; for large a, b (>~1e5) the rounding noise in `c_n*d_n` is of the order of the tolerance and the loop may never exit (Numerical Recipes' betacf uses MAXIT for exactly this reason).
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:719-722` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `invBetaIncReg(p, a, b)` throws "interval does not appear to bracket the root" for the valid inputs `p = 0` and `p = 1` (Javadoc: "p any value in the range [0,1]"): `fx1 = betaIncReg(0) - p = -p`, `fx2 = 1 - p`, so `fx1*fx2 = 0 >= 0`. Expected: return 0 resp. 1.
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1080-1089` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `gcd(a, b)` never terminates when either argument is 0 (`gcd(5, 0)`: a != b, a > b, `a -= 0` forever) and misbehaves for negative arguments (`gcd(-3, 5)`: b keeps growing until int overflow). Reference: gcd(n, 0) = |n|.
- `rapaio/math/MathTools.java:1107-1109` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `combinations(0, k)` returns 1 for `k > 0`; C(0, k) = 0 for k > 0 (the `k > n` check comes after the `n == 0` shortcut). Also the documented overflow detection "check the sign" is unreliable: the intermediate `c *= n - d` can wrap to a positive value (e.g. `combinations(66, 33)`: C(66,32)*34 exceeds Long.MAX_VALUE although C(66,33) = 7.2e18 fits), so a wrong positive value can be returned (MEDIUM).
- `lnGamma`, `digamma`, `incGamma`/`incGammaC`, `stirlingErr`, `deviancePart`, `logBinomial`, `pdfPois`, `normalQuantile`, `log1pExp`, `logit`, `logistic`, `fdist` (upper tail; matches its only caller's p-value use) -- checked against fdlibm/Cephes/Loader references; clean. Note `lnGamma(x<0)` returns NaN by design (fdlibm reflection removed), `incGamma(a<=0, x)` returns 0 like Cephes.

### math/optimization

- `rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:211-213` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] In `IRLS0M`, `w.div(w.sum())` and `w.mul(w)` are the non-in-place DArray operations (only `div_`/`mul_` mutate), so both results are discarded. The normalization is harmless (a scalar cancels in the LS solve) but the squaring is essential: Burrus' M0 uses `W = diag(|e|^{(p-2)/2})` applied to both sides of `Ax = b`, i.e. normal equations `A^T W^2 A x = A^T W^2 b`; the code solves `A^T W A x = A^T W b` with `W = |e|^{(p-2)/2}` instead of `|e|^{p-2}`, so it iterates towards the minimizer of a different (wrong) norm. For p=1 the weights are computed directly as `1/|e|` and happen to be correct; for every other p != 2 the result is wrong. Trigger: `IRLSSolver.newMinimizerM0(4)` on an overdetermined system -- the returned x does not minimize `||Ax-b||_4`. (Line 268 in `IRLS1M` has the same discarded `div` but there it is only the harmless normalization.)
- `rapaio/math/optimization/IRLSSolver.java:245-247,286` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `MethodImpl` clamps `k` to [0.5, 0.99] for p<2 while the `k` parameter Javadoc promises [0.7, 0.9]; and `IRLS1M` records `errors` as the L2 norm (`nn = 2`) for p <= 2 but the Lp norm for p > 2, so `errors()` is not the objective being minimized for p < 2. Minor, but `hasConverged` is decided on that quantity.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:291-303` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `secant_optimization` never re-evaluates `f`: `fa` and `fb` keep their initial values while `xa`, `xb` are updated, so the iteration is the linear recurrence `x_{n+1} = (x_{n-1} fb - x_n fa)/(fb - fa)` with constant coefficients, which converges to a point determined only by the two starting values and reports `converged = true`. Trigger: f(x)=x^2-2, x0=1, x1=2 (fa=-1, fb=2): iterates 4/3, 16/9, 40/27, ... -> converge to 1.6 (root is 1.41421); `isConverged()` is true. Reference secant: `x_{n+1} = x_n - f(x_n)(x_n - x_{n-1})/(f(x_n) - f(x_{n-1}))` with fresh `f(x_n)`.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:134-153` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `bisection_optimize` terminates only on `|f(xc)| < eps` (default 1e-15); there is no interval-width test. When `|f'(root)|` is large enough that `|f|` cannot get below eps at any double near the root (e.g. f(x) = 1e6 (x - 0.3): the closest double gives |f| ~ 5e-11), the loop runs all `maxIt` = 100000 iterations on a stalled interval (xa, xb adjacent doubles, xc equal to one of them) and returns the correct root with `converged = false`.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:223-233` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] ITP pre-computation `n_half = ceil(log((xb - xa)/(2 eps)))` (a) uses the natural log where the ITP method defines `n_1/2 = ceil(log2((b-a)/(2 eps)))`, so `n_max` is too small by a factor ~1.44 and the projection radius `r` goes negative before the method has converged, forfeiting the minmax guarantee; (b) after the swap at line 223 (`fa > fb`), `xa > xb` whenever f is decreasing on the bracket, so `xb - xa < 0`, `log(negative)` = NaN and `n_half = (int) ceil(NaN) = 0`. Trigger: f(x) = 1 - 2x, x0 = 0, x1 = 1. The method still converges (every update is a valid bracketing step) but as a plain truncated-interpolation scheme, not as ITP; with a non-integer `k2` and `xb < xa`, `pow(xb - xa, k2)` is NaN and the step degenerates to bisection.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:229-270` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] With the default `x0 = x1 = NaN` (or any NaN bracket), `itp_optimize` passes the `fa*fb > 0` check (NaN > 0 is false), skips the loop (`abs(NaN) > 2 eps` is false), and returns `x = NaN` with `converged = true` and `iterations = 0`. Expected: `IllegalArgumentException` like `bisection_optimize`/`secant_optimization` give for non-finite brackets. `regula_falsi_optimize`, `brent_optimization` and `ridder_optimization` likewise accept NaN/infinite brackets and spin for `maxIter` iterations.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:172` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `regula_falsi_optimize` uses the relative test `|xb - xa| < eps * |xb + xa|`, which cannot be met when the root is at (or straddles) 0 because `|xb + xa| -> 0` as fast as `|xb - xa|`; termination then depends on `f(r)` becoming exactly 0. Trigger: f(x) = x (1 + x^2) on [-1, 2]: r converges to ~1e-17 with f(r) != 0, the loop runs to `maxIter` and reports `converged = false`. Also `fa - fb == 0` (no bracket check) gives r = NaN and a `maxIter` spin.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/scalar/RootFind1D.java:344,415` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Brent and Ridder terminate on the absolute test `|xb - xa| < eps` (default 1e-15). For roots of magnitude >~ 10 the spacing of doubles exceeds eps (ulp(1000) = 1.1e-13), so once the bracket is two adjacent doubles it can never shrink further; the loops run `maxIter` = 100000 iterations and report `converged = false` for a root that was found exactly. Trigger: f(x) = x - 1000, bracket [0, 2000], Brent or Ridder. A tolerance of the form `eps * max(1, |x|)` (or `2 ulp`) is needed; ITP's `2 eps` width test has the same weakness.
- `rapaio/math/optimization/SteepestDescentSolver.java:106-116` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: LOW] After the loop exhausts `maxIt`, the last iterate's gradient is not tested, so a run that reaches the tolerance exactly on the final step reports `hasConverged() == false`; and when the line search returns t = 0 (non-descent direction) the loop spins without progress until `maxIt`. Both terminate; noted as off-by-one on the convergence report only.
- `BacktrackLineSearch.java`, `LearningRateLineSearch.java`, `LineSearch.java`, `Solver.java`, `functions/*` -- clean. Backtracking has no iteration cap, but `t *= beta` reaches 0 in ~2100 steps and `f(x) > fx + 0` is false, so it terminates (returning 0) even for non-descent directions.

### Summary

Overall quality is uneven: the evaluation code (ROC/AUC with ties, Gini, KFold, LogLoss, accuracy, confusion matrix for nominal inputs) and the ported special functions (lnGamma, incomplete gamma, digamma, Stirling/Loader deviance) are correct, but the kernel package and the optimizers contain many formula-level errors. Most serious (HIGH, wrong results on ordinary input): 12 of 22 kernels (all kernel and distance items fixed 2026-09-28, see Status) do not compute the documented function (`deltaSumSquares` treated as the norm instead of the squared norm in Exponential, Cauchy, StudentT, RationalQuadratic, InverseMultiQuadric, MultiQuadric, Log, Power, Wave; inverted support test in Circular/Spherical yielding 0 on the diagonal and NaN/garbage off it; `atan` instead of `tanh` in Sigmoid); LDA extracts eigenvectors of `Sb Sw^-1` instead of `Sw^-1 Sb` and standardizes test data with test statistics; `RootFind1D` secant never re-evaluates f and converges to a wrong point with `converged=true`; `IRLSSolver` M0 discards the weight squaring (non-in-place `mul`), minimizing the wrong norm; `MinkowskiDistance` omits `abs`; `Confusion` swaps true/false for `VarBinary` inputs; `MathTools.normalCdf(+inf)=0` so `erf(+inf)=-1`, `erfc` underflows to 0 beyond x~6, `gcd(n,0)` hangs, `invBetaIncReg(0|1)` throws; Huber `error()` uses the quantile level as the delta. Counts: bug 27, likely-bug 12, contract 11 (HIGH 41, MEDIUM 6, LOW 3). Clean files are listed per package above.

### Findings

#### rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java

- `rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:78-102` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `parameters()` walks the class hierarchy with `clazz` but reads `this.getClass().getDeclaredFields()` on every iteration, so fields declared in a superclass are never inspected; the Javadoc promises a recursive hierarchy search. Trigger: `abstract class Base extends AbstractNetwork { Linear l1; }` and `class Net extends Base { Linear l2; }` -- `parameters()` returns only `l2`'s tensors, `l1` is silently never optimized (loss does not decrease on that layer). Expected: use `clazz.getDeclaredFields()`.
- `rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:114-132` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same `this.getClass()` bug in `state()`, and here there is no dedup: the concrete class's layer states are merged once per ancestor level. For `class Net extends AbstractNetwork` the loop runs for Net, AbstractNetwork and Object, so every layer's tensors appear 3 times in the `NetworkState`; `saveState` writes each tensor 3 times (file 3x larger) and `loadState` reads them back in the same order (so the round trip is consistent), but superclass-declared layers are missing from the state entirely and are silently not restored.
- `rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:241-253` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `loadState` reads the stored dtype and `Shape` and ignores both; no check against the target tensor. Trigger: save a `Linear(4,3)` (12 doubles), load into `Linear(3,3)`: `readeDoubleArray` copies the first 9 values in C order and returns normally -- silently wrong weights. Loading a smaller saved tensor into a larger one throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` from `array[p++]` instead of a meaningful error.
- `rapaio/nn/layer/AbstractNetwork.java:150-165` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `batchForward` runs `forward()` for all batches concurrently on `tm.outerExecutor()`. In train mode `BatchNorm1D.forward11` (lines 84-89 of that file) does a non-atomic read-modify-write of `sampleMean`/`sampleStd` (`sampleMean = tm.var(sampleMean.value().mul(1-m).add_(...))`), so concurrent batches lose running-statistics updates. Trigger: any network containing `BatchNorm1D`, `train()`, `batchForward(bs, ...)` with >1 batch.

#### rapaio/nn/layer/BatchNorm1D.java

- `rapaio/nn/layer/BatchNorm1D.java:57-58,84,93-94` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `sampleMean`/`sampleStd` are initialised to zeros/ones in the constructor, so both `== null` branches are dead: the first-training-step branch (line 84) never runs, and the eval-mode guard "BatchNorm1D requires at least one optimization step" (line 94) can never fire. Calling the layer in eval mode before any training silently normalises with mean 0 / std 1 instead of raising the documented error.

#### rapaio/nn/layer/Conv1D.java

- `rapaio/nn/layer/Conv1D.java:35,65,68` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Weight initialiser is a fixed `U(-0.5, 0.5)` independent of fan-in (`inChannels/groups * k`), and the bias is initialised to constant 1. For `Conv1D(tm, 64, 64, 5, ...)` the pre-activation variance is 320 * 1/12 ~ 27x the unit-variance target; deep stacks saturate/explode at initialisation. Expected a fan-in scaled bound such as `1/sqrt(inChannels/groups*k)` (and zero or small-uniform bias).

#### rapaio/nn/layer/Conv2D.java

- `rapaio/nn/layer/Conv2D.java:64` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Init bound is `sqrt(groups / (inChannels * (kH+kW)/2))`; the fan-in is `inChannels/groups * kH*kW`, so the formula uses the *mean* kernel side instead of the kernel *area*. For a 3x3 kernel the weight variance is 3x the `1/fan_in` convention (`kH*kW=9` vs `(kH+kW)/2=3`); for 5x5 it is 5x. Only 1x1 kernels are correct. Bias is again constant 1 (line 70).

#### rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java

- `rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java:51-60` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Gradients are doubled when `batchForward` is given >= 2 batches. Each `loss.forward(...)` pre-sets `last.setGrad(1)` on its per-batch loss tensor L_i (MSELoss:50, NLL:51/52); `batchForward` then builds `S = L_1.add(L_2)...` and sets `S.grad = 1`. During backward, `AddNode`'s back edge calls `L_i.addGrad(1)`; since `L_i.grad` is already `1`, `addGrad` takes the `add_` path and `L_i.grad` becomes 2, so every parameter gradient is 2x what the sum loss implies (for 3 batches, `((L_1+L_2)+L_3)`: L_3 gets 2, the inner AddNode gets 1 and passes 1 to L_1, L_2 which become 2 -- still all 2x). Trace: 2 batches, MSE, single parameter w: d(sum)/dw = g_1+g_2, code yields 2(g_1+g_2). With a single batch `lossTensor == lossOut.tensor()` and `setGrad(1)` overwrites, so the bug appears only with >= 2 batches. Root cause: losses setting a gradient in `forward` and `Tensor.addGrad` accumulating into it.
- `rapaio/nn/loss/AbstractLoss.java:59-60` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Output.tensor()` holds the *sum* of per-batch losses while `Output.lossValue()` is the *mean* over batches (`lossValue / batches.size()`); the reported value does not correspond to the tensor that is differentiated (and, combined with the finding above, the effective gradient is 2*B times the gradient of the reported number). Also NPE on `lossTensor.setGrad` if `batches` is empty.

#### rapaio/nn/loss/MSELoss.java

- `rapaio/nn/loss/MSELoss.java:46-48` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `y.setValue(y.value().stretch(1))` is applied whenever `pred` is a matrix, without checking that `y` is a vector, and it mutates the caller's tensor. (a) Multi-output regression `pred (n,k)`, `y (n,k)`: y becomes `(n,k,1)`, `pred.sub(y)` broadcasts `(1,n,k)` vs `(n,k,1)` -> `IllegalArgumentException` when n != k, or a silent `(n,n,n)` all-pairs loss when n == k. (b) Calling `forward(pred, y)` twice with the same `y` vector (e.g. validation loss every epoch): second call sees `y (n,1)`, stretches to `(n,1,1)`, `pred (n,1) - y` broadcasts to `(n,n,1)` -> loss = sum over all pairs `(pred_i - y_j)^2 / n`, no exception. Expected: only stretch when `y.isVector()`, and do it on a local copy.

#### rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLikelihoodLoss.java, NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss.java

- `rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLikelihoodLoss.java:46-48`, `rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss.java:46-48` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Both mutate the caller's `y` tensor (`y.setValue(...stretch(1))`); the operation is idempotent here (guarded by `isVector()`), but a caller's target tensor silently changes shape from `(n)` to `(n,1)` after the first loss call.
- `rapaio/nn/loss/NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss.java:49` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] The class is named after PyTorch's NLLLoss (which consumes log-probabilities) but computes `pred.log()`; fed with the output of the provided `LogSoftmax` layer it takes log of negative numbers -> NaN loss and NaN gradients. It is a cross-entropy-on-probabilities; no Javadoc states which input is expected.

#### rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java

- `rapaio/nn/optimizer/Adam.java:90-93` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Optimizer` Javadoc (Optimizer.java:36-37) states a tracked tensor with `requiresGrad == false` is skipped ("useful for freezing parts of the network"). `Autograd` never produces a gradient for such a tensor, so `step()` throws `RuntimeException("Gradient is null for param")`. Trigger: `net.parameters().get(0).requiresGrad(false); Optimizer.Adam(tm, net.parameters()).step()`. Same for a parameter that simply did not take part in the current graph.
- Adam update formulas verified: m/v EMAs, bias correction `1-beta^t` with t starting at 1 and incremented after all params, `lr*m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)`, L2 weight decay into the gradient, maximize sign -- all correct. AMSGrad takes max over bias-corrected v_hat rather than raw v (a known variant, not a defect).

#### rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java

- `rapaio/nn/optimizer/SGD.java:85-104` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Same contract violation as Adam: `gt = tensor.grad()` is null for a frozen (`requiresGrad(false)`) or unused parameter and `gt.mul(lr.get())` / `gt.add(...)` throws `NullPointerException` instead of skipping. Momentum/dampening/nesterov/weight-decay formulas match the reference implementation.

#### rapaio/nn/optimizer/NoGrad.java

- `rapaio/nn/optimizer/NoGrad.java:26-34` (with `rapaio/nn/Optimizer.java:41-44`) -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc says NoGrad "provide[s] context for making inference faster"; the class is empty and nothing consults it, so the autograd graph and all back-edges are still built during inference. No speedup and no memory saving is delivered.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/BatchVtmNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/BatchVtmNode.java:34-41` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `DArray.bvtm` accepts a batch of matrices `bm (b,n,m)` (and scalars), and the forward pass therefore succeeds, but the backward assumes `bm` is a single matrix. For `bv (b,n)`, `bm (b,n,m)`: d_bv = `grad(b,m).bvtm(bm.t())` where `t()` reverses *all* axes to `(m,n,b)` -> `IllegalArgumentException` (batch dim m != b) or silent garbage when b == m == n; d_bm = `bv.t() (n,b) mm grad (b,m)` = `(n,m)`, the wrong shape for `bm (b,n,m)` (stored as-is by `addGrad`, corrupting the parameter gradient). Correct: d_bv[i] = grad[i] @ bm[i]^T, d_bm[i] = bv[i]^T grad[i] (bmm), or reject rank-3 `bm` in the constructor.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:48-50,64-65` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `DArray.conv1d` documents and accepts an unbatched `(C_in, L)` input, but the node reads `x.value().dim(2)` in the constructor (`ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` for rank 2) and `selsq(0, batch)` in the weight backward. Forward through the DArray op works; the tensor op throws on the same input.
- `rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv1dNode.java:41` (root cause in `rapaio/darray/manager/base/BaseDoubleStrideDArrayConvolutions.java:84,207`) -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `conv1d` (and `unfold1d`) compute `L_out = floorDiv(L + 2p - d(k-1), s)` while the DArray Javadoc (and conv2d, lines 259-260) use `floorDiv(L + 2p - d(k-1) - 1, s) + 1`; the two differ by one whenever `(L + 2p - d(k-1)) mod s != 0`. Trigger: `x (1,1,6)`, `k=2`, `stride=2`: documented L_out = 3, produced L_out = 2 -- the last valid window is silently dropped from the Conv1D layer output. Because forward and `unfold1d` share the wrong formula the weight gradient stays shape-consistent, and `outputPadding` (= 2 = stride in this example) absorbs the difference for the input gradient, so no exception is raised. Reported here because it changes Conv1D layer results; the fix belongs to darray.
- Weight gradient (`gy[g] @ unfold(x[g])^T` accumulated into a reshape view via `mm(other,to)`, which adds) and bias gradient (`sum1d(2).sum1d(0)`) verified correct; `convTranspose1d` weight layout `(C_out, C_in/groups, k)` matches the documented transposed-conv convention.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv2dNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/Conv2dNode.java:54-59` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] A single `outputPadding = max(outputPaddingH, outputPaddingW)` is passed to `convTranspose2d`, which adds it to *both* spatial dims. Whenever the two remainders differ the input gradient has the wrong shape. Trigger: `x (1,1,5,6)`, `w (1,1,2,2)`, `stride=2`, `padding=0`: `outH = 2`, `outW = 3`, `outputPaddingH = 5-4 = 1`, `outputPaddingW = 6-6 = 0`, `max = 1` -> gradient shape `(1,1,5,7)` for an input `(1,1,5,6)`. `addGrad` copies it as-is (shape mismatch stored silently on first contribution, `add_` failure on a second). Correct: compute the transposed conv with per-axis output padding, or crop/pad the result to `x.shape()`. The code comment acknowledges the assumption.
- Weight gradient via `unfold2d` (row order `(c,kh,kw)` matches the `(outDepth, inDepth*kH*kW)` weight reshape) and bias gradient verified correct.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java:46-48` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `inplace=true` mutates `child.value()` after the child's producers have already captured that array for their own backward. Trigger: `y = x.sigmoid(); z = y.dropout(0.5, rnd, true)`: `SigmoidNode`'s back edge computes `grad * y*(1-y)` from `this.value()`, which is now the masked/rescaled array, so dx is wrong wherever the mask is 1 (scaled by 1/(1-p)) and 0 where masked. Same for Tanh/Exp/Sqrt/Std nodes upstream, and for view nodes (`ReshapeNode`, `NarrowNode`, `StretchNode`) whose value shares storage with their input (the input's value is mutated too). No version check exists; either the flag should be removed or documented as unsafe after any op that reuses its output in backward.
- `rapaio/nn/tensors/DropoutNode.java:47-52` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] No validation of `p`: `p = 1` gives mask all-zero and `div_(1-p)` = 0/0 -> every output and every gradient is NaN (reference implementations return zeros); `p > 1` or `p < 0` reaches `Bernoulli.of(p)` unvalidated.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/GatherNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/GatherNode.java:35` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Backward uses `scatter`, whose implementation (`AbstractStrideDArray.scatter_`, `setDouble`) *overwrites* the destination; repeated indices lose contributions instead of summing. Derived: dx[j] = sum over all i with index[i]==j of dy[i]. Trigger: `x (1,3) = [a,b,c]`, `index (1,2) = [[0,0]]`, `y = [a,a]`, `dy = [1,1]`: expected dx = `[2,0,0]`, code gives `[1,0,0]`. Any target tensor with duplicate class labels per row is unaffected (one index per row), but e.g. attention/embedding-style gathers along axis 0 with repeated ids are wrong. Needs a scatter-add.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/Std1dNode.java, StdOnNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/Std1dNode.java:37`, `rapaio/nn/tensors/StdOnNode.java:40` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] When an external `mean` tensor is supplied, its gradient is hard-coded to zero. That is only correct if `mean` equals the actual mean of `x` along the axis (then `sum(x-mu)=0`); for any other supplied `mean` the true derivative is d std/d mu = `-sum(x-mu)/(dof*std)` != 0, and the x-gradient (which treats mu as constant) is then the partial, not total, derivative. The API accepts arbitrary `mean` tensors without documenting this precondition.

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/Mean1dNode.java, Sum1dNode.java, LogSoftmaxNode.java, SoftmaxNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/Mean1dNode.java:35`, `rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/Sum1dNode.java:32`, `rapaio/nn/tensors/LogSoftmaxNode.java:34`, `rapaio/nn/tensors/SoftmaxNode.java:35` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] Negative axes are accepted by the forward (`reduce1d`/`var1d` normalise `axis < 0`, `Shape.dim` accepts negatives) but the backward calls `strexp(axis, ...)` = `stretch(axis).expand(axis, ...)`, and `ArrayStrideLayout.stretch` (line 327) / `expand` (line 362) throw `IndexOutOfBoundsException` / `IllegalArgumentException` for `axis < 0`. Trigger: `x (2,3)`, `x.mean1d(-1).sum()` then backward -> exception in backward after a successful forward. (Assumption: `softmax1d`/`logsoftmax1d` also accept negative axes; the reductions certainly do.)

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/MeanOnNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/reduce/MeanOnNode.java:33-41` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] When `shape` covers the whole tensor (`x.rank() == shape.rank()`), `meanOn(shape, true)` returns a rank-0 scalar (`reduceOn` returns `dm.scalar(...)` before applying keepDim). In backward `g` is then a scalar, `g.dim(0)` returns 1 (Shape.dim on rank 0), which differs from `x.dim(0)` so `g.expand(0, n)` is called and `ScalarStrideLayout.expand` throws "Scalar stride cannot be expanded". Trigger: `x (3,4)`, `x.meanOn(Shape.of(3,4))` then backward. Also reached through `Tensor.stdOn(shape)` with a full shape, since it builds `x.meanOn(shape)` internally. Correct: `strexp` every axis when the gradient is a scalar (as `SumNode` does).

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/shape/ReshapeNode.java

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/shape/ReshapeNode.java:32-33` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Forward interprets indices in `askOrder` (`DArray.reshape(shape, Order)` Javadoc: "the asked order is ... the order in which data is interpreted for reshape"); backward calls `this.grad.reshape(x.shape())` = `Order.A` on a gradient that `addGrad` stored as a dense C copy, i.e. always a C-order interpretation. For `askOrder == Order.F` (public API `Tensor.reshape(shape, Order.F)`) the index maps are different and the gradient is permuted. Trace: `x (2,3)`, `y = x.reshape(Shape.of(6), Order.F)` gives `y[k] = x[k%2][k/2]`; backward gives `dx[i][j] = dy[3i+j]` where the correct value is `dx[i][j] = dy[i+2j]`; e.g. `dy = [0,1,2,3,4,5]` -> code `dx = [[0,1,2],[3,4,5]]`, correct `dx = [[0,2,4],[1,3,5]]`. `Tensor.reshape(Shape)` passes `Order.C` explicitly and is correct; only the explicit-order overload is affected (and `Order.A` on F-stored inputs such as `Linear`'s F-ordered weights).

#### rapaio/nn/tensors/unary/LogNode.java (with Tensor.java:441)

- `rapaio/nn/tensors/unary/LogNode.java:32`, `rapaio/nn/Tensor.java:440-442` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] Backward is `grad * (1/x).nanToNum_(eps)`, and `Tensor.log()` passes `eps = -1`. `nanToNum_` replaces NaN, -inf and +inf by the same fill, so at `x == 0` the derivative `1/x = +inf` becomes `-1`: the gradient keeps its magnitude bounded but its *sign is flipped*. Trigger: `NegativeLogLikelihoodLoss` on a prediction whose target-class probability underflowed to exactly 0 (float softmax): dL/dp = `(-1/n) * (-1) = +1/n > 0`, so gradient descent pushes the already-zero probability further *down*; the correct direction is up (true derivative -inf). Any positive finite fill (e.g. `1/eps`) would preserve the sign. Also the parameter name `eps` on `Tensor.log(double eps)` suggests `log(x+eps)`, but the forward is a plain `log` (still -inf at 0); the value is only a gradient fill.

### Summary

The autograd core (topological ordering, `computeGrad` propagation, `addGrad` copy-then-accumulate, back-edge clearing) is sound, and every elementwise/binary/softmax/standardize gradient I derived matches the code. The serious problems sit at the edges: `AbstractLoss.batchForward` doubles all gradients for >= 2 batches because losses pre-set `grad = 1` and `addGrad` accumulates into it; `AbstractNetwork.parameters()/state()` use `this.getClass()` instead of the walked `clazz`, so superclass-declared layers are never optimized or saved (and states are duplicated 3x); `GatherNode` uses an overwriting scatter so repeated indices lose gradient; `Conv2dNode` uses one `outputPadding` for both axes (wrong gradient shape for non-square remainders); `ReshapeNode` ignores the forward `askOrder` in backward; `MeanOnNode` throws on full-shape reductions; `BatchVtmNode` breaks for batched matrices; `MSELoss` mutates `y` unconditionally and breaks multi-output targets or a second call. `SGD`/`Adam` crash on frozen parameters contrary to the `Optimizer` Javadoc, and both convolution layers use fan-in-incorrect initialisers. Counts: bug 9, likely-bug 8, contract 10 (27 findings; 22 HIGH confidence, 5 MEDIUM). One darray defect (conv1d output length) is reported because it changes Conv1D layer results.
