### Findings

#### rapaio/graphics/base/XWilkinson.java

- `rapaio/graphics/base/XWilkinson.java:236-237` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `Labels.getFormattedValue` formats with `NumberFormat.getInstance(Locale.getDefault())`, whose default `maximumFractionDigits` is 3, so any axis whose tick step is below 1e-3 gets duplicated/rounded labels. Input: numeric axis over data in [0, 0.0005] (step 0.0001): ticks 0.0001, 0.0002 are both rendered "0" and 0.0005 as "0" or "0.001". Expected: labels with enough fraction digits to distinguish the ticks (the value is already rounded to `significantDigits(step)` on the line before; the formatter then destroys it).
- `rapaio/graphics/base/XWilkinson.java:300` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `double step = z == -1 ? j * q : j * q * Math.pow(base, z);` special-cases exponent -1 to `j*q` (the same step as z == 0) instead of `j*q/base`, so steps of magnitude base^-1 (0.2, 0.25, 0.5, 0.3 ... with j = 1) are never candidates. Input: `base10().search(0, 1, 5)`. Actual: the search must reach 0.2/0.25 through higher skip factors j (with lower simplicity score) or falls back to z = -2 steps; the existing test `XWilkinsonTest.testLocale` encodes the resulting non-natural output `0.15;0.3;0.45;...` for [0.123, 0.96]. Expected: `j*q*base^z` for every z (Talbot/Wilkinson definition); a correct search yields 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8 or 0.25, 0.5, 0.75 for that range.
- `rapaio/graphics/base/XWilkinson.java:224-230` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: LOW] `getList` compares `min + i * step <= max` on unrounded doubles; `searchBounded` derives `bounded.min/max` by adding/subtracting one step from the found labels, so accumulated rounding can make the last label exceed `max` by 1 ulp and be dropped (one tick fewer than intended). Needs a run to find a concrete range.
- `rapaio/graphics/base/XWilkinson.java:349-370` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `searchBounded(min, max, maxM)` with `maxM < 2` leaves `best == null` and returns null (callers NPE). Axis guarantees `spots >= 2`, so this only bites direct users of the public API.

#### rapaio/graphics/opt/GOpts.java

- `rapaio/graphics/opt/GOpts.java:129-131,150-152,382-395` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `color()`/`fill()` with zero indexes (or `color(Var)` with an empty Var) produce a zero-length array; `getColor(row)`/`getFill(row)`/`getSz`/`getPch` then compute `row % 0` and throw `ArithmeticException: / by zero` at paint time, far from the option. Expected: validate at option construction (or treat empty as "no colour").
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/GOpts.java:181-190` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `pch(Var, int... mapping)`: `mapping.length` is dereferenced before the `mapping != null` check (dead null-guard), and an out-of-range index silently maps to `mapping[0]` instead of being reported. Input: `pch(VarInt.wrap(5), 1, 2)` -> every row drawn with pch 1.

#### rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java

- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:174-186` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `GrayColorPalette` uses internal range [0, 256] and wraps `index %= 256`, so the maximum of the input range maps to black. Input: `Palette.gray(0, 1).getColor(1.0)`: proportion 1 -> value 256 -> index 256 -> 0 -> `Color(0,0,0)`. Expected white (`Color(255,255,255)`).
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:188-199` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `HueBlueRed.getColor` ignores `inputRange`; `Palette.hueBlueRed(start, end)` accepts a range that has no effect (value is used raw as a 0..240 hue).
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:201-219` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `RedBlueGradient.getColor` ignores `inputRange` (`Palette.redBlue(start, end)` is silently the same as `redBlue()`) and computes channels as `255*pp` with `pp = value/360` unclamped, so `new Color(r,g,b,a)` throws `IllegalArgumentException("Color parameter outside of expected range")` for any value outside [0, 360]. Input: `Palette.redBlue().getColor(400)` or `getColor(-1)`.
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:221-236` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `RedGreenBluePalette.getColor` ignores `inputRange` (`Palette.rgb(start, end)` range silently ignored) and throws `IllegalArgumentException` from `new Color` for values < 0 (`255 - index > 255`) or > 510. NaN maps to floor(NaN)=0 -> pure red.
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:318-336` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `IndexedPalette.getColor`: `(int) Math.floor(NaN)` is 0, so a missing value (`GOpts.color(Var)` with a missing cell) is silently coloured with palette entry 0 (black), indistinguishable from category 0; `Infinity` maps to `Integer.MAX_VALUE % n`. The `inputRange` passed to the constructor is never used.
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:338-357` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `BiColorGradient.getColor` interpolates `start*p + end*(1-p)`, so the colour at the range start is `end` and at the range end is `start` (reversed). Input: `Palette.bicolor(Color.RED, Color.BLUE).getColor(0)` returns blue; expected red.
- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Palette.java:370-386` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `MonoHueGradient` constructor passes `super(new Range(minSat, maxSat), inputRange)`, i.e. the saturation range becomes `inputRange` and the data range becomes `internalRange`; `getColor` then normalises the data value against [minSat, maxSat] and maps it into the data range as saturation. Input: `Palette.hueMono(0f, 0.2f, 0.8f, 1f, 0, 100).getColor(50)`: cut(50) into [0.2,0.8] = 0.8 -> p = 1 -> saturation = 100 -> `HSBtoRGB` with saturation 100 produces garbage. Expected saturation 0.5.

#### rapaio/graphics/opt/Sizes.java

- `rapaio/graphics/opt/Sizes.java:31,66-68` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `getRelativeSizes`/`getAbsoluteSizes` copy `relativeSizes`/`absoluteSizes` into an array of `length` without checking `length >= sizes.length`; `GridLayer.of(1, 2, widths(0.3, 0.3, 0.4))` throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` at paint with no indication of the offending option. Expected: validation with a clear message (or truncation).

#### rapaio/graphics/plot/Axis.java

- `rapaio/graphics/plot/Axis.java:129-136` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `Domain.hardLim(hardMin, hardMax)` does not check `hardMin <= hardMax`; `Plot.xLim(10, 0)` yields `min=10, max=0`, negative `length()`, an inverted axis, and `allowUnion` (`x >= 10 && x <= 0`) rejects every data point silently.
- `rapaio/graphics/plot/Axis.java:341` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `TypeTime.computeArtifacts` tests `index != intervals.length - 1` (the starting index) where it means `i` (the current one); when the search starts at the smallest interval the "go one step finer" fallback is skipped for every coarser `i`, even though `intervals[i + 1]` would be valid.

#### rapaio/graphics/plot/GridLayer.java

- `rapaio/graphics/plot/GridLayer.java:105-114` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Javadoc of `add(int x, int y, Plot)` says "Cells are indexed starting with 1", x horizontal, y vertical; the code is 0-based and the first argument is the row (`add(row, col, ...)`, `assign[i][j]` with `i` in `[0, rows)`). Input: 2x2 grid, `add(2, 2, plot)` per the doc -> `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`; `add(1, 0, p)` places in row 1, col 0, not column 1.
- `rapaio/graphics/plot/GridLayer.java:93-103` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `add(Plot)` on a full grid falls through, re-parents the plot's options and returns without adding it; the plot is silently dropped. Expected: exception or a documented no-op.
- `rapaio/graphics/plot/GridLayer.java:65-83,131-172` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `buildViewport` reserves `TITLE_PAD` and pads in `viewport`, but `prepare`/`paint` lay cells out from the raw rectangle `r` (`r.x`, `r.y`, `r.getWidth()`, `r.getHeight()`), so `viewport` is dead and the top row overlaps the title band; each `Plot.paint` first fills its cell white, so the title drawn at `r.y + TITLE_PAD` is painted over. Input: `gridLayer(1,1).title("T").add(plot)` -> title invisible.
- `rapaio/graphics/plot/GridLayer.java:171` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `g.plot.paint((Graphics2D) g2d.create(), rect)` never disposes the created `Graphics2D` (resource leak, one per cell per paint).

#### rapaio/graphics/plot/Plot.java

- `rapaio/graphics/plot/Plot.java:105-113` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Constructor calls `this.options.bind(opts)` and discards the result; `GOpts.bind` returns a new child set and does not mutate `this`, so every option passed to `new Plot(opts)` / `Plotter.plot(opts)` is silently ignored (`options` stays empty). Input: `plot(color(2)).points(x, y)` -> points drawn black. Expected: `this.options.apply(opts)` (as `GridLayer` does).
- `rapaio/graphics/plot/Plot.java:180-182` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `yUnscale` returns `(viewport.y - y) * len / height + 1. + yAxis.min()`; the `+ 1.` should be `+ yAxis.length()` (inverse of `yScale`). Input: axis [0,10], viewport y=0,h=100: `yUnscale(yScale(5)) = (0-50)*10/100 + 1 + 0 = -4`, expected 5. Correct only when `length() == 1`. Public API, exposed through `Artist.yUnscale`.
- `rapaio/graphics/plot/Plot.java:65,68` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Plot implements Figure extends Serializable` but holds `final GOpts options` (not `Serializable`, and `NamedParamSet` holds non-serializable lambdas); `ObjectOutputStream.writeObject(plot)` throws `NotSerializableException`. Same for `GridLayer` (`options`) and every `Artist` (field `GOpts options`). Consequence: `IdeaPrinter.draw` (which serialises the Figure through `ClassMarshaller.marshallDraw`) always fails and only logs at INFO.

It also always intersects with `yAxis.min()` for the left end and `yAxis.max()` for the right end, which is wrong for negative slopes, and divides by `a` (Infinity for a == 0).

Input: `transformToBW(ImageTools.makeImage(plot, 100, 100), f)`. (Also `bi.getData()` copies the whole raster on every pixel.)

#### rapaio/printer/TextTable.java

- `rapaio/printer/TextTable.java:127,145,158` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `text.indexOf('.') > -0` is `> 0`, so a value whose decimal point is at index 0 (e.g. user text ".5" passed to `floatString`) is not anchored and is laid out as a two-piece cell with empty right part (right-aligned), misaligned with the other decimals in the column. Intended `>= 0`.
- `rapaio/printer/TextTable.java:187-190,251-256` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `set(row, col, null, null)` (or `textLeft(r, c, null)`) leaves `center` null too and `computeFinalTexts` dereferences `center[i][j].length()` -> `NullPointerException` at render, far from the offending call.
- `rapaio/printer/TextTable.java:258-278,315-334` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] Column widths use `String.length()` (UTF-16 units), so supplementary characters (emoji, some CJK) count 2 while occupying one column and East-Asian wide characters count 1 while occupying two; columns containing such labels are misaligned. Documented purpose is aligned tabular output.
- `rapaio/printer/TextTable.java:364-372,404-411` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] No validation of `headerRows <= rows` / `headerCols <= cols`: `headerCols >= cols` makes `computeSplitText` return an empty string silently; `headerRows > rows` makes `computeMergeText` allocate `new String[negative]` -> `NegativeArraySizeException`. Separator spaces (one per column) are also not counted against `consoleWidth`, so split output exceeds the requested width by up to the number of columns.

#### rapaio/printer/idea/ClassMarshaller.java, IdeaPrinter.java

- `rapaio/printer/idea/ClassMarshaller.java:41-55` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `marshallDraw` serialises the `Figure` with `ObjectOutputStream`; every concrete Figure (`Plot`, `GridLayer`, all `Artist`s) holds a non-serializable `GOpts` (see Plot finding), so this always throws `NotSerializableException`, and `IdeaPrinter.draw` catches it and logs at INFO -- the IDEA draw path never works and the failure is effectively silent.
- `rapaio/printer/idea/IdeaPrinter.java:38-49` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `draw(Figure, int width, int height)` discards `width`/`height` and `draw(Figure, POpt...)` discards `options`; `graphicWidth`/`graphicHeight` options are accepted but ignored (the `CommandBytes` graphical width/height are never set).

#### rapaio/printer/opt/POpts.java, NamedParamSet.java

- `rapaio/printer/opt/POpts.java:31,55` and `rapaio/printer/opt/NamedParamSet.java:30-42` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `POpts implements Serializable` and `POpt implements Serializable`, but the superclass `NamedParamSet` is not serializable and has no no-arg constructor (`InvalidClassException` on serialization), and `NamedParam.fun` is a non-serializable `Function` lambda; serialising any `POpts`/`POpt` fails.
- `rapaio/printer/opt/NamedParamSet.java:39-42,72-84` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] `register(this.getClass())` scans only the declared static fields of the *concrete* class; a subclass of `GOpts`/`POpts` (the constructors are protected) would register no defaults and `getParamValue` would NPE on `ref.defaults.get(name).getValue(...)`. `setParent(null)` on a non-root set has the same effect.

#### rapaio/printer/standard/FigurePanel.java

- `rapaio/printer/standard/FigurePanel.java:145-148` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `FigurePrinter.getOptions()` returns null although `Printer.getOptions()` promises an options instance; `printer.printSummary(printable)` (default `Printer` method -> `Printable.toSummary(printer, ...)` -> `printer.getOptions().bind(...)` in implementations such as `HistogramTable.toSummary`) and `TextTable.getDynamicText(printer)` throw `NullPointerException`.

### Summary

Overall the drawing pipeline (axes, viewport transforms, clipping in `Lines`, tick search) is sound for the common numeric case, but the option plumbing and the less-used artists are fragile: several defaults are null `fill` colours combined with `Graphics2D.setColor(null)` being a no-op, which silently ignores `color(...)` (ABLine, Segment) or paints with a stale colour (BoxPlot factor constructor, PolyFill), and per-artist state (alpha composite, clip) leaks across artists. The most serious defects: `Plot(opts)` discards all plot-level options; `GridLayer` titles are painted over and its `add(x,y)` contract is 0-based row/col not 1-based x/y; `Points.yAxisType` uses the wrong variable; `IsoCurves` drops contour segments through the lower-left corner and its `isOutside` check is inverted and unit-mismatched; `Silhouette` horizontal bars use an absolute pixel coordinate as width; `CandlestickChart` separators use a pixel difference as a coordinate; palettes are wrong at their range end (`gray`), reversed (`bicolor`), have swapped ranges (`hueMono`) or ignore the given range and throw outside it (`redBlue`, `rgb`, `hueBlueRed`); tick labels below 1e-3 collapse to duplicates through `NumberFormat`'s 3-fraction-digit default and the z == -1 step special case distorts the Wilkinson search; `hist2d` NPEs without `fill` and AIOOBEs with `xLim`; `transformToBW` AIOOBEs on any alpha image; every `Figure`/`POpts` claims `Serializable` but cannot be serialised, so the IDEA printer never works. Counts: 25 bug, 28 likely-bug, 12 contract (65 findings; 58 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 1 LOW confidence).

### Findings

#### rapaio/util/Hardware.java
- `rapaio/util/Hardware.java:76-88` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] The MAC branch yields `Integer.parseInt(tokens[1])` of the FIRST line of `sysctl -a hw` output without checking the key, so `L2_CACHE_SIZE` becomes whatever the first sysctl line's value is (e.g. `hw.ncpu: 8` gives 8 bytes) or, if that value is non-numeric, the default via the swallowed NumberFormatException. It never looks at `hw.l2cachesize`. Should filter on the key name before parsing.
- `rapaio/util/Hardware.java:59-74` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] The WIN branch parses every non-header line with `Integer.parseInt(tokens[0])`; `wmic` output ends with blank line(s), and `"".split("\\s+")` yields `[""]`, so `parseInt("")` throws, the exception is logged and the already computed `size` is discarded in favour of `DEFAULT_L2CACHE_SIZE`. The intended value is effectively never returned on Windows. Blank/whitespace-only lines should be skipped.

#### rapaio/util/Pair.java
- `rapaio/util/Pair.java:59-61` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `update(Pair<T1,T2> p)` calls `update(v1, v2)` with the receiver's own fields instead of `p.v1, p.v2`, so it is a no-op. Input: `a=Pair.from(1,2); a.update(Pair.from(3,4))` leaves `a` as `{1,2}` instead of `{3,4}`.

#### rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java
- `rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java:1964-1978` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `binarySearch(double[], from, to, key)` uses `<`/`>` on doubles; when `a[mid]` or `key` is NaN both tests are false and the method returns `mid` as "found". The library's own sorts (`quickSort`, `mergeSort`, `Double.compare` order) place NaN at the end, so for `a={1.0, 3.0, NaN}`, `binarySearch(a, 5.0)` probes mid=1 (3<5, from=2), then mid=2 (NaN) and returns 2 instead of `-4`; `binarySearch({1,2,3}, NaN)` returns 1. Should use `Double.compare` like the sorts do (the comparator overload at 2020 is fine).
- `rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java:1673-1680` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `parallelQuickSort(double[] x, double[] y, from, to)` is missing the `else`: for ranges `< PARALLEL_QUICKSORT_NO_FORK` it runs `quickSort(x,y,from,to)` and then still creates a ForkJoinPool and sorts the same range again with `ForkJoinQuickSort2`. Result is still sorted, but the first branch is dead work and a pool is created for every small call. Compare with the correct `if/else` at 1118-1125 and 1357-1365.
- `rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java:885-887, 1123-1125, 1362-1364, 1677-1679` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: MEDIUM] The ForkJoinPool is created outside try-with-resources and `pool.shutdown()` is only reached when `pool.invoke` returns normally; a comparator that throws (or an AIOOBE from a bad range) leaks the pool (workers are daemon and time out, so not permanent, but it is inconsistent with `Ints`, which uses try-with-resources at 968, 1013, 1305, 1574).
- `rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java:1409-1421` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: MEDIUM] `stabilize` groups equal keys with `x[perm[i]] != x[perm[curr]]`; for a block of NaN keys (which the indirect sorts treat as equal via `Double.compare`) `NaN != NaN` is true, so the block is never re-sorted and the documented guarantee "`x[perm[i]] = x[perm[i+1]]` implies `perm[i] <= perm[i+1]`" fails for NaN ties. Input: `x={NaN,NaN}`, `perm={1,0}` stays `{1,0}`. Should compare with `Double.compare(...) != 0` (or `Double.doubleToLongBits`).
- `rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java:362-371` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `nanCount` returns the number of NON-NaN elements (it `continue`s on NaN and counts the rest). The name states the opposite; the same name on `DArray.nanCount()` counts NaNs. `DoublesTest` asserts the current behaviour, so internal callers (`nanVariance`) are consistent, but `rapaio-experiment DVectorDense.nanCount` delegates here and thus returns the complement of what `DArray.nanCount` returns.
- `rapaio/util/collection/Doubles.java:141-156` vs `Ints.java:164-181` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] Copy-paste asymmetry between variants: `Doubles.iterator(array, start, len)` takes a length while `Ints.iterator(array, start, end)` takes an exclusive end index. Same name and shape, different third-argument meaning; `Ints.iterator(a, 5, 3)` silently yields nothing where the Doubles variant would yield 3 elements.

#### rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java
- `rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java:123-130` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `slotsFor` loops `while (slots < needed) slots <<= 1` on an `int`; when `needed > 2^30` the shift overflows to `Integer.MIN_VALUE` (still `< needed`), then to `0`, and `0 << 1 == 0` forever: the constructor never returns. Input: `new Int2IntOpenHashMap(42, 0.75, 900_000_000, QUADRATIC)` (needed = 1_200_000_001). Any `allocation > 0.75 * 2^30 ≈ 805M` hangs instead of throwing; a smaller `loadFactor` lowers the threshold proportionally (e.g. `loadFactor=0.01`, `allocation=11_000_000`). The same overflow makes `2 * slots` in `allocate` negative once `slots` reaches `2^30`, so growth via `grow()` at ~805M live entries would throw `NegativeArraySizeException` rather than a clear capacity error.
- `rapaio/util/collection/Int2IntOpenHashMap.java` (probing, removal, rehash otherwise) -- checked and clean: power-of-two slots with `& mask`, triangular probing `pos += round` is a full permutation of a power-of-two table, `threshold <= slots-1` guarantees an empty slot so `find` terminates, no `remove` exists so no tombstone/back-shift issue, `MISSING` as key is rejected in `get/put` and reported absent by `containsKey`, values equal to `MISSING` are handled by testing the key slot, `grow()` rehashes with `putInArray` which cannot double-count.

#### rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java
- `rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java:106-113` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] Same infinite loop as `Int2IntOpenHashMap.slotsFor`: `capacityFor` overflows `capacity <<= 1` to `MIN_VALUE` then `0` and never exits for `needed > 2^30` (e.g. `new IntOpenHashSet(42, 0.75, 900_000_000, QUADRATIC)`). `grow()` at 207 (`old.length << 1`) likewise goes negative at `2^30` capacity and throws `NegativeArraySizeException` from `Ints.fill`.
- `rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java:186-197` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `addAll(Collection<? extends Integer>)` throws a bare `ClassCastException` (no message) for a `null` element (`null instanceof Integer` is false) where the `Collection` contract specifies `NullPointerException`; the pre-scan also iterates the argument twice, so a one-shot iterable/`Collection` view backed by a consumed stream would add nothing.
- `rapaio/util/collection/IntOpenHashSet.java` (probing, MISSING handling, iterator) -- checked and clean: `Integer.MIN_VALUE` member tracked by `hasMissingValue`, `size()` includes it, `contains/add/clear/toArray` all consult it, iterator works on a snapshot copy so no concurrent-modification hazard, `grow()` reinserts with `find` on the fresh table.

#### rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java
- `rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:249-256` -- [severity: bug] [confidence: HIGH] `equals(a, aStart, b, bStart, len)` loops `for (i = 0; i < len - aStart; i++)`; the bound must be `len`. With `aStart > 0` it compares only `len - aStart` elements, and with `aStart >= len` it compares nothing and returns `true`. Input: `a={0,0,1}`, `b={9,9,2}`, `equals(a, 2, b, 2, 1)` compares 0 elements and returns `true` although `a[2]=1 != b[2]=2`. `IntsTest` only exercises `aStart == 0`. The `Doubles` variant (445) is correct.
- `rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:1570-1578` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `parallelQuickSort(int[] x, int[] y, from, to)` is missing the `else` (same defect as `Doubles.java:1673`): small ranges are sorted sequentially and then a ForkJoinPool is created and the range is re-sorted. All other `parallelQuickSort*` overloads in this file have the `else`.
- `rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:275-297` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `isDenseArray` Javadoc says it "Returns the start element value ... `-1` otherwise", but the method returns `boolean`; it also dereferences `indexes[0]` unconditionally, so an empty array throws `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` (`DMatrixDenseC:477,499` call it without a length guard; `DVectorDense:171` guards).
- `rapaio/util/collection/Ints.java:312-324` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `argmax(array, start, end)` guards only `array.length == 0` (returns -1) but then reads `array[start]`; an empty RANGE on a non-empty array (`start == end`) returns `start` as if it were a maximum, and `start == array.length` throws AIOOBE instead of returning -1. The `Doubles.argmax/argmin` variants have no sentinel at all (return `offset` for size 0), so the two variants disagree on the empty case.

#### rapaio/util/parralel/ParallelStreamCollector.java
- `rapaio/util/parralel/ParallelStreamCollector.java:240-248` -- [severity: contract] [confidence: HIGH] `Dispatcher.stop()` catches `InterruptedException` from `workingQueue.put(POISON_PILL)`, completes the signaller exceptionally, but does not restore the interrupt flag (`Thread.currentThread().interrupt()`), unlike `CompletionOrderSpliterator.nextCompleted` at 172-176; the caller's interrupt is lost.
- Otherwise clean: exceptions from the mapper go through `handle()` → `completionSignaller.completeExceptionally` → every pending future is completed exceptionally and cancelled, `join()` rethrows as `CompletionException`; the semaphore is always released via `withFinally`; the private dispatcher thread is shut down in `stop()`/`handle()` and the caller-owned `executor` is (correctly) not shut down; `combiner` refuses parallel streams.

#### rapaio/util/time/PrettyTimeInterval.java
- `rapaio/util/time/PrettyTimeInterval.java:519-531` -- [severity: likely-bug] [confidence: HIGH] `getInstantList(start, end)` is inconsistent at the upper bound: when `end` is aligned, `last = end` and the list ends at `end`; when `end` is not aligned, `last = getInstantAfter(end)` and, because the loop condition is `first <= last`, the list includes one bucket start strictly AFTER `end`. Input (`_2_MIN`): `start=14:36, end=14:54` → last element 14:54; `start=14:36, end=14:55` → last element 14:56 (> end). `Axis.java:323-329` compensates by trimming a trailing element that is after `end`, which confirms the extra element is unintended. Either the unaligned case should stop at `getInstantBefore(end)` or the aligned case should include `getInstantAfter(end)`.
- Otherwise clean: all `getInstantBefore` variants use UTC, `truncatedTo` + `minus(h % n)` is correct for every n dividing 24/60, `Instant.truncatedTo(DAYS)` floors correctly for negative epochs, `_1_YEAR/_1_MONTH` `getInstantAfter` = first day of next period (leap years handled by `TemporalAdjusters`).

### Summary

Overall the package is solid, mostly fastutil/Solr/parallel-collectors code faithfully copied; the primitive open-hash collections, Murmur3, comparators and function interfaces are correct. The serious defects are the hand-written helpers: `Ints.equals` compares the wrong number of elements (silent `true`), `Pair.update(Pair)` is a no-op, `Doubles.binarySearch` reports NaN slots as matches for any key, `Hardware` computes garbage on macOS (and discards its result on Windows), and both open-hash constructors spin forever for large `allocation` values instead of failing. The two `parallelQuickSort(x, y, from, to)` overloads sort small ranges twice (missing `else`), and `PrettyTimeInterval.getInstantList` has an off-by-one at an unaligned upper bound that callers currently patch around.

Counts: bug 6 (Hardware MAC, Pair.update, Doubles.binarySearch, Int2IntOpenHashMap.slotsFor, IntOpenHashSet.capacityFor, Ints.equals), likely-bug 5 (Hardware WIN, Doubles parallelQuickSort missing else, Doubles pool not closed on exception, Ints parallelQuickSort missing else, PrettyTimeInterval.getInstantList), contract 7 (Doubles.stabilize NaN, nanCount naming, iterator len/end asymmetry, IntOpenHashSet.addAll, Ints.isDenseArray, Ints.argmax, Dispatcher.stop interrupt). Total findings: 18.
